. We are going to use it frequently in our paper (see Lemma
5.12

(1)
). For a short exact sequence, in a different way, it was proved earlier by Mosher
[
Mos96
]
.
In a hyperbolic geodesic metric space, geodesics (and hence quasi-geodesics) diverge exponentially. In a tree of metric bundles
X
, qi lifts are quasi-geodesics. So, they diverge exponentially provided
X
is hyperbolic. This property is captured in the following definition for special types of quasi-geodesics, namely, qi lifts (see also
necessity of flaring
in Introduction
1
to get more on this). This definition is a generalization of Bestvina-Feighn’s hallway flaring condition (
[
BF92
]
) in a natural way as defined in
[
MS12
, Definition
1.12
]
for metric bundles.

Definition 5.6
.

Suppose
k\geq 1
. A tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
is said to satisfy
k
-
flaring condition
(see Figure
2
) if
\exists~M_{k}>0
,
n_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\lambda_{k}>1
depending on
k
such that the following holds.

For every pair
(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})
of
k
-qi lifts of a geodesic
\gamma:[-n_{k},n_{k}]\rightarrow[a,b]\subseteq B
joining
a,b
with
d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(0),\gamma_{1}(0))>M_{k}
, we have,

\lambda_{k}d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(0),\gamma_{1}(0))<max\{d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(-n_{k}),\gamma_{1}(-n_{k})),d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(n_{k}),\gamma_{1}(n_{k}))\}

where
d^{f}
denotes the fiber distance in the corresponding fiber. Abusing terminology, we sometimes simply say that
(X,B,T)
satisfies
k
-flaring condition suppressing the constants
M_{k},n_{k},\lambda_{k}
. We say that
(X,B,T)
satisfies a flaring condition if it satisfies
k
-flaring condition for all
k\geq 1
.

Figure 2
.
Flaring Condition

Now we will state the following Lemma
5.7
without a proof. These results correspond to
[
MS12
, Lemma
2.17
, Lemma
2.18
]
in metric bundles situation. One has a similar proofs in trees of metric bundles. The results
(1)
of Lemma
5.7
is defined as
uniform flaring condition
in the book
[
KS22
]
, and
(2)
says that the neck are quasiconvex subset of the base in the sense of
[
MS12
]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying
k
-flaring condition with constants
M_{k},n_{k},\lambda_{k}
. Then for all
D\geq 0
there is
\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k,D)
and
R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}=R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k,D)
satisfying the following.

Let
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}
be two
k
-qi lifts of a geodesic
\gamma:[0,r]\rightarrow[a,b]\subseteq B
joining
a,b\in B
(resp.
a,b\in V(B)
). Let
0=r_{0}<r_{1}<\cdots<r_{n}=r
such that
r_{i+1}-r_{i}=1
for
0\leq i\leq n-2
and
r_{n}-r_{n-1}\leq 1
. Then:

(1)

d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(r_{i}),\gamma_{1}(r_{i}))>M_{k}
for all
1\leq i\leq n-1
and

max\{d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(0),\gamma_{1}(0)),d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(r),\gamma_{1}(r))\}\leq D

implies
r=d_{B}(a,b)\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}
.

(2)

max\{d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(0),\gamma_{1}(0)),d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(r),\gamma_{1}(r))\}\leq D
implies for all
0\leq i\leq n

d^{f}(\gamma_{0}(r_{i}),\gamma_{1}(r_{i}))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}.

Throughout the thesis, we directly will not use flaring condition in proving results instead Lemma
5.7
.
We make the following remark for later use, and it follows from the Definition
5.6
.

Remark 5.8
.

Let
k\geq 1
. Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying
k
-flaring condition with constants
M_{k},n_{k},\lambda_{k}
. Let
S
be a subtree of
T
. Then
(a)
it satisfies
k^{\prime}
-flaring condition for all
k^{\prime}\leq k
with the same constants, and
(b)
the restriction
\pi_{X}|_{X_{S}}:X_{S}\rightarrow B_{S}
also satisfies
k
-flaring condition with the same constants.

Motivated by the main theorems of
[
BF92
]
and
[
MS12
]
, we define the following.

Definition 5.9
.

We say that a tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
satisfies the hyperbolic axioms, in short, axiom
H
with parameters
\delta_{0}\geq 0,N\geq 0
and
L_{0}\geq 1
if the following hold.

(1)

Let
u\in V(T)
and
a\in B_{u}
. Then
F_{a,u}
is
\delta_{0}
-hyperbolic and the barycenter map
\partial^{3}F_{a,u}\rightarrow F_{a,u}
is
N
-coarsely surjective.

(2)

Let
e=[v,w]
be an edge in
T
. Let
\mathfrak{e}=[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
, and
m_{\mathfrak{e}}
be the mid point of
\mathfrak{e}
. Then the incident maps
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{v}}:\pi_{X}^{-1}(m_{\mathfrak{e}})\rightarrow F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
,
\vartheta_{\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{w}}:\pi_{X}^{-1}(m_{\mathfrak{e}})\rightarrow F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are
L_{0}
-qi embeddings.

(3)

Lastly, let
B
be
\delta_{0}
-hyperbolic. This assumption is the same as
B_{v}
is
\delta_{0}
-hyperbolic for all
v\in V(T)
.

5.1.
Conventions and notations

(1)

Unless otherwise specified, our tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
always satisfies the axiom
H
with constants
\delta_{0},N,L_{0}
as in Definition
5.9
. Hence we will use
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
,
L^{\prime}_{0}
,
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
and
L^{\prime}_{1}
for constants depending on
\delta_{0}
and
L_{0}
as in Lemma
3.3
.

(2)

Suppose
B^{\prime}
is connected subspace of
B
and
S
is subtree of
T
. Unless otherwise specified, by
b\in B^{\prime}
and
u\in S
, we always mean
b\in B^{\prime}\cap B_{\pi_{B}(b)}
and
u\in V(S)
respectively.

(3)

If
X_{1}
is a
K
-qi section over
B_{1}
, then it is a compatible
\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2K)
-qi section over
B_{1}
. Thus, now onward, by a
K
-qi section, we always mean a compatible
K
-qi section.

Notation 5.10
.

We use these notations throughout this paper except Section
4
. We denote the composition map
\bm{\pi_{B}\circ\pi_{X}}
by
\bm{\pi}
. For a subtree
S\subseteq T
,
\bm{X_{S}:=\pi^{-1}(S)}
and
\bm{B_{S}:=\pi_{B}^{-1}(S)}
. In particular, for
v\in V(T)
,
\bm{X_{v}:=\pi^{-1}(v)}
and
\bm{B_{v}:=\pi_{B}^{-1}(v)}
. Let
v,w\in T
,
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. We denote
\bm{F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}([\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}])}
. The induced path metric on
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
is denoted by
\bm{d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}}
, and we use
\bm{N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}}
to mean neighborhood of subsets of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
in the
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric. For a fiber
F_{a,u}
, where
u\in V(T)
and
a\in B_{u}
, we simply use
\bm{d^{f}}
,
\bm{diam^{f}}
,
\bm{N^{f}}
and
\bm{[x,y]^{f}}
(or
\bm{[x,y]_{F_{a,u}}}
) to denote respectively the induced path metric on
F_{a,u}
, the diameter of a subset of
F_{a,u}
in
d^{f}
-metric, the neighborhood of a subset of
F_{a,u}
in
d^{f}
-metric and a geodesic inside
F_{a,u}
joining
x,y\in F_{a,u}
. Since it will be clear from the context which fiber we are working with, we are not being more specific on
d^{f},~N^{f}
etc. Lastly,
\bm{P_{\mathfrak{w}}:=P_{F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}},F_{\mathfrak{w},w}}}
.

We want to put metric bundle (Definition
5.1
) structure on subspace of
X
. Suppose
X_{1}\subseteq X
and
S
is a subtree of
T
such that the restriction map
\pi_{X}|_{X_{1}}:X_{1}\rightarrow B_{S}
is surjective.

Definition 5.11

(K-metric bundle and special
K
-ladder)
.

With the above, we say that
X_{1}
forms a
K
-metric bundle
over
B_{S}
if there is a
K
-qi section through each point of
X_{1}
over
B_{S}
such that the image lies inside
X_{1}
. Further, we say that
X_{1}
forms a
special
K
-ladder
if
X_{1}
forms a
K
-metric bundle along with two
K
-qi sections
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
over
B_{S}
such that
X_{1}=\bigcup\limits_{v\in S,~b\in B_{v}}[F_{b,v}\cap\Sigma_{1},F_{b,v}\cap\Sigma_{2}]_{F_{b,v}}
. In this case, sometimes we denote
X_{1}
by
\mathcal{L}_{K}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
or simply by
\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
when
K
is understood.

Lemma
5.12
(2) is proved for metric graph bundles (see
[
MS12
, Definition
1.5
]
) in
[
MS12
, Lemma 3.1]
, although it holds for metric bundles. Since the same proof works, we omit it.

Lemma 5.12
.

(
[
MS12
, Proposition
2.10
, Proposition
2.12
, Lemma
3.1
, Lemma
3.3
]
)

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying axiom
H
. (For
(3)
, axiom
H
is not required.) Then given
K\geq 1
and
R\geq 2K
there is
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)>K
such that the following holds.

(1)
There exists a constant
K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}\geq 1
depending only on
\delta_{0},N
(constants of axiom
H
) such that through each point
x\in X_{v}
there is a
K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
-qi section over
B_{v}
in the path metric of
X_{v}
, where
v\in V(T)
.

(2)
Let
v\in V(T)
and
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
be two
K
-qi sections over
B_{v}
. Then
\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
is a special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-ladder over
B_{v}
.

(3)
Let
\Sigma
be a
K
-qi section over an isometrically embedded subspace
B_{1}\subseteq B
. Let
s:[a,b]\rightarrow X
be a qi lift of a geodesic segment
[a,b]\subseteq B_{1}
such that
Im(s)\subseteq\Sigma
. Then
N_{R}(\Sigma)
is path connected and if the induced path metric is
d^{\prime}
then
d^{\prime}(s(a),s(b))\leq 2Kd_{B}(a,b)
. Moreover,
N_{2K}(\Sigma)
is
K(2K+1)
-qi embedded inside any geodesic subspace containing
N_{R}(\Sigma)
(with respect to their induced path metric).

Remark 5.13
.

In the view of Lemma
5.12

~(2)
, for
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote the
i^{th}
iteration by
C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(C^{(i-1)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)),
where
C^{(0)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)=K
. In other words, if
\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
is bounded by two
C^{(i-1)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-qi sections
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}
over
B_{v}
, then
\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
is a special
C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-ladder over
B_{v}
.

As an application of Lemma
5.12

(2)
along with the fact that quadrilaterals are slim in hyperbolic spaces, we have the following. We omit the proof.

Lemma 5.14
.

Given
K\geq 1
there is
K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}=K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}(K)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying axiom
H
. Let
S
be a subtree of
T
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{K,S}=\{\gamma:\gamma\textrm{ is a }K\textrm{-qi section }\textrm{ over }B_{S}\}\neq\emptyset
. For
w\in S
,
b\in B_{w}
, let
H_{b,w}=\textrm{hull}\{\gamma(b):\gamma\in\mathcal{G}_{K,S}\}\subseteq F_{b,w}
and
H=\bigcup_{w\in S,~b\in B_{w}}H_{b,w}
. (Here quasiconvex hull is considered in the corresponding fiber.) Then
H
is
K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}
-metric bundle over
B_{S}
. In particular, if
\mathcal{G}_{K,S}=\{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\}
then
\bigcup_{w\in S,~b\in B_{w}}[\gamma_{1}(b),\gamma_{2}(b)]^{f}
forms a special
K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}
-ladder over
B_{S}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

Given
k\geq 1
there exists
k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}=k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(k)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying axiom
H
. Let
v\in T
and
\Sigma_{i}
be
k
-qi section over
B_{v}
for
i=1,2,3
. Suppose
\{x^{(i)}_{b,v}\}=\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{b,v},i=1,2,3
and
z_{b,v}
is a
\delta_{0}
-center of geodesic triangle
\triangle(x^{(1)}_{b,v},x^{(2)}_{b,v},x^{(3)}_{b,v})\subseteq F_{b,v},~b\in B_{v}
. Then the map
s:B_{v}\rightarrow X
defined by
b\mapsto z_{b,v}
is a
k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}
-qi section over
B_{v}
.

Proof.

Let
b_{1},b_{2}\in B_{v}
such that
d_{B}(b_{1},b_{2})\leq 1
. Since
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow B
is
1
-Lipschitz, we only have to prove that
d_{X_{v}}(z_{b_{1},v},z_{b_{2},v})
is uniformly bounded. Define a map
\psi:F_{b_{1},v}\rightarrow F_{b_{2},v}
by
\psi(x^{(i)}_{b_{1},v})=x^{(i)}_{b_{2},v}
for
i=1,2,3
and
\forall~z\in F_{b_{1},v}\setminus\{x^{(i)}_{b_{1},v}:i=1,2,3\}
, we take
\psi(z)\in F_{b_{2},v}
such that
d(z,\psi(z))\leq c_{0}
(as in Definition
5.2
). Note that
d_{X_{v}}(x,\psi(x))\leq 2k+c_{0}
for all
x\in F_{b_{1},v}
. Then by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.15
]
,
\psi
extends to a
g(2k+c_{0})
-quasi-isometry from
F_{b_{1},v}
to
F_{b_{2},v}
for some function
g:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Therefore, by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.29

(2)
]
,
d^{f}(\psi(z_{b_{1},v}),z_{b_{2},v})
is bounded by a constant
D
, depending only on
\delta_{0}
(hyperbolicity constant of
F_{b_{i},v}
) and
g(2k+c_{0})
. Hence, we can take
k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}:=D+2k
.
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

Given
K\geq 1
there exists
K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}=K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles satisfying axiom
H
. Let
v\in T
and
\Sigma_{i}
be
K
-qi section over
B_{v}
for
i=1,2,3,4
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{1}=\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2})
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}=\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{3},\Sigma_{4})
be special ladders over
B_{v}
formed by these sections. Let
a\in B_{v}
and
\bar{P}_{a}:\mathcal{L}_{1}\cap F_{a,v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{2}\cap F_{a,v}
be modified projection in the metric
F_{a,v}
(see Definition
2.24
). Further, suppose
\bar{P}_{a}(\mathcal{L}_{1}\cap F_{a,v})=[p_{a},q_{a}]_{F_{a,v}}
such that
p_{a}
is closest to
\Sigma_{3}\cap F_{a,v}
and
q_{a}
is that to
\Sigma_{4}\cap F_{a,v}
in the metric
F_{a,v}
. Moreover, we define
s_{1}:B_{v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{2}
and
s_{2}:B_{v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{2}
by
a\mapsto p_{a}
and
a\mapsto q_{a}
respectively, where
a\in B_{v}
.

Then
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are
K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}
-qi sections over
B_{v}
lying inside
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

Proof.

Suppose
b,c\in B_{v}
such that
d_{B}(b,c)\leq 1
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{i}\cap F_{b,v}=[x_{i},y_{i}]
and
\mathcal{L}_{i}\cap F_{c,v}=[s_{i},t_{i}]
for
i=1,2
, where
\Sigma_{1}(b)=x_{1},~\Sigma_{1}(c)=s_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}(b)=y_{1},~\Sigma_{2}(c)=t_{1}
and
\Sigma_{3}(b)=x_{2},~\Sigma_{3}(c)=s_{2}
,
\Sigma_{4}(b)=y_{2},~\Sigma_{4}(c)=t_{2}
. Suppose
\bar{P}_{b}([x_{1},y_{1}])=[p_{1},q_{1}]
and
\bar{P}_{c}([s_{1},t_{1}])=[p_{2},q_{2}]
such that
p_{1}
,
p_{2}
are closest to
x_{2}
,
s_{2}
respectively in the metric
F_{b,v}
and
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
are closest to
y_{2}
,
t_{2}
respectively in the metric
F_{c,v}
. Since
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow B
is
1
-Lipschitz, we only need to show that
d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},p_{2})
and
d_{X_{v}}(q_{1},q_{2})
are uniformly bounded. We will show only the former one as a similar proof works for the later case.

Let
\bar{P}_{b}(x_{1})=x_{1}^{\prime},\bar{P}_{b}(y_{1})=y_{1}^{\prime},\bar{P}_{c}(s_{1})=s_{1}^{\prime},\bar{P}_{c}(t_{1})=t_{1}^{\prime}
. Note that
x^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}]\subseteq[x_{2},y_{2}]
or
x^{\prime}_{1}\in[y^{\prime}_{1},q_{1}]\subseteq[x_{2},y_{2}]
and
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{2},t^{\prime}_{1}]\subseteq[s_{2},t_{2}]
or
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[t^{\prime}_{1},q_{2}]\subseteq[s_{2},t_{2}]
. Depending on the position on
x^{\prime}_{1}
and
s^{\prime}_{1}
, we consider the following four cases.

Like in Lemma
5.15
, we define a map
\psi:F_{b,v}\rightarrow F_{c,v}
such that
\psi(x_{i})=s_{i},~\psi(y_{i})=t_{i},~i=1,2
and for all other points
x\in F_{b,v}
, we take
\psi(x)\in F_{c,v}
such that
d_{X_{v}}(x,\psi(x))\leq c_{0}
(as in Definition
5.2
). Then
d_{X_{v}}(x,\psi(x))\leq 2K+c_{0},\forall~x\in F_{b,v}
, and so by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.15
]
,
\psi
extends to a
g(2K+c_{0})
-quasi-isometry from
F_{b,v}
to
F_{c,v}
for some function
g:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Thus by Lemma
2.21

(2)
, there is
k=2K+D_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},g(2K+c_{0}),\delta_{0})
such that

(1)

\displaystyle d_{X}(x^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{1})\leq k\textrm{ and }d_{X}(y^{\prime}_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})\leq k

Case
1: Suppose
x_{1}^{\prime}\in[p_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}]
and
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{2},t^{\prime}_{1}]
. Then by
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
, there is constant
C_{1}
depending on
\delta_{0}
such that
d^{f}(p_{1},x^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
and
d^{f}(p_{2},s^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
. Combining with inequation
1
, we have
d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},p_{2})\leq k+2C_{1}
.

Figure 3
.

Case
2: Suppose
x_{1}^{\prime}\in[y^{\prime}_{1},q_{1}]
and
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[t^{\prime}_{1},q_{2}]
. In this case,
y^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{1},x^{\prime}_{1}]
and
t^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{2},s^{\prime}_{1}]
. Again by
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
,
d^{f}(p_{1},y^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
and
d^{f}(p_{2},t^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
. Combining with inequation
1
, we get,
d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},p_{2})\leq k+2C_{1}
.

Case
3: Suppose
x_{1}^{\prime}\in[p_{1},y^{\prime}_{1}]
and
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[t^{\prime}_{1},q_{2}]
. In this case,
t^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{2},s^{\prime}_{1}]
. Applying
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
, we get,
d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},x^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
and
d_{X_{v}}(p_{2},t^{\prime}_{1})\leq C_{1}
. We define a map (like above)
\psi:F_{b,v}\rightarrow F_{c,v}
such that
\psi(x^{\prime}_{1})=s^{\prime}_{1},~\psi(y_{2})=t_{2}
and for all other points
x\in F_{b,v}
, we take
\psi(x)\in F_{c,v}
such that
d_{X_{v}}(x,\psi(x))\leq c_{0}
(as in Definition
5.2
). Then
\forall~x\in F_{b,v}
,
d_{X_{v}}(\psi(x),x)\leq k+c_{0}
(see inequation
1
). Thus by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.15
]
,
\psi
extends to a
g(k+c_{0})
-quasi-isometry from
F_{b,v}
to
F_{c,v}
for some function
g:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Applying Morse Lemma
2.5
,
\exists~\zeta\in[s^{\prime}_{1},t_{2}]\subseteq[s_{2},t_{2}]
such that
d_{X_{v}}(y^{\prime}_{1},\zeta)\leq c_{0}+D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{0},g(k+c_{0}),g(k+c_{0}))=k^{\prime}
(say). Then
d_{X_{v}}(\zeta,t^{\prime}_{1})\leq d_{X_{v}}(\zeta,y^{\prime}_{1})+d_{X_{v}}(y^{\prime}_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})\leq k+k^{\prime}
. Since fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded in
X
, so
d^{f}(\zeta,t^{\prime}_{1})\leq\phi(k+k^{\prime})
. In particular,
d^{f}(s^{\prime}_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})\leq\phi(k+k^{\prime})
. Thus
d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},p_{2})\leq d_{X_{v}}(p_{1},x^{\prime}_{1})+d_{X_{v}}(x^{\prime}_{1},s^{\prime}_{1})+d_{X_{v}}(s^{\prime}_{1},t^{\prime}_{1})+d_{X_{v}}(t^{\prime}_{1},p_{2})\leq 2C_{1}+k+\phi(k+k^{\prime})
.

Case
4: Lastly, we assume that
x_{1}^{\prime}\in[y^{\prime}_{1},q_{1}]
and
s^{\prime}_{1}\in[p_{2},t^{\prime}_{1}]
. But this is same as Case
3
.

Therefore, we can take
K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}:=2C_{1}+k+\phi(k+k^{\prime})
(maximum of all constants we get in the above four cases).
∎

6.
Semicontinuous families: flow space and ladder

Our standard assumptions for this section are that the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
must satisfy axiom
H
and a flaring condition (see Subsection
5.1
). However, by Remark
5.8

(a)
one can observe that
k
-flaring condition for a large
k
is enough (see introduction of Section
7
,
8
,
9
).

Motivated by the construction of semicontinuous families of spaces in
[
KS22
, Chapter
3
]
, we build subspaces analogous to that in trees of metric bundles. We follow the same terminology used in
[
KS22
]
. Also, following
[
KS22
]
, we will see two special kinds of subspaces: flow spaces and ladders. These are the building blocks, which will be shown to be hyperbolic, towards proving Theorem
1.4
.

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles as in Definition
5.2
. Suppose
\mathfrak{Y}=\bigcup_{v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}},b\in B_{v}}Q_{b,v}
where
Q_{b,v}\subseteq F_{b,v}
and
T_{\mathfrak{Y}}:=\textrm{hull}(\pi(\mathfrak{Y}))
. With this we define the following.

Definition 6.1

(Semicontinuous families)
.

Let
K\geq 1,C\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq 0
. We say that
\mathfrak{Y}\subseteq X
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family in
X
with a central base
\mathfrak{B}=\pi_{B}^{-1}(\mathfrak{T})
for some central subtree
\mathfrak{T}
in
T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
if the following hold.

(1)

Let
v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
and
b\in B_{v}
. Then
Q_{b,v}
is a
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex subset of
F_{b,v}
and
\bigcup_{b\in B_{v}}Q_{b,v}\subseteq X_{v}
forms a
K
-metric bundle (see Definition
5.11
) over
B_{v}
. Moreover,
\bigcup_{v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}},~b\in B_{v}}Q_{b,v}
forms a
K
-metric bundle over
\mathfrak{B}
in
X
.

(2)

Let
v,w\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
such that
w\notin{\mathfrak{T}},d_{T}(v,w)=1,d_{T}({\mathfrak{T}},w)>d_{T}({\mathfrak{T}},v)
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Then
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}),Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\epsilon
(see Notation
5.10
for
P_{\mathfrak{w}}
) and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K,\forall~x\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Moreover, if both
v,w\in{\mathfrak{T}}
then
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v},Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K
.

(3)

Suppose
w\notin T_{\mathfrak{Y}},v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Then the pair
(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w}.w})
is
C
-cobounded in the metric
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
.

(4)

Additionally, let
B^{\prime}\subseteq\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
be
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedded subspace in
B
. Suppose
v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
and
B^{\prime}_{v}:=B_{v}\cap B^{\prime}
. Then
\forall~v\in T_{\mathfrak{Y}}\textrm{ and }\forall~b\in B_{v}\setminus B^{\prime}_{v}
, diam
{}^{f}(Q_{b,v})\leq C
. Let
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}(B^{\prime})\cap\mathfrak{Y}
.

Remark 6.2
.

(a)
The condition
(4)
is used in Section
8
(more precisely, in Lemma
8.14
), otherwise, all the time
B^{\prime}=\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
and so
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{Y}
.

(b)
If
T
is a single vertex, say,
\{u\}
then
\mathfrak{Y}
is
K
-metric bundle over
B_{u}
.

(c)
If
\pi_{B}:B\rightarrow T
is a graph isomorphism, then
\mathfrak{Y}
is the same as the semicontinuous family defined in the book
[
KS22
, Chapter
3
]
.

(d)
(
Maximality
) ‘Moreover part’ in conditions
(1)
and
(2)
are equivalent provided first parts of
(1)
and
(2)
hold. Let
z\in\mathfrak{Y}
and
t_{z}
be the nearest point projection of
\pi(z)
on
\mathfrak{T}
. Suppose
B_{z}=\pi_{B}^{-1}([t_{z},\pi(z)])\cup\mathfrak{B}
. Then it follows from the conditions
(1)
and
(2)
that there is a compatible
K
-qi section (see Definition
5.4
), say,
\Sigma_{z}
over
B_{z}
lying inside
\mathfrak{Y}
. Sometimes (more precisely, in Subsection
7.2
), we work with maximal qi sections through points in
\mathfrak{Y}
as follows. Let
S
be a subtree of
T_{\mathfrak{Y}}
containing
\mathfrak{T}\cup[t_{z},\pi(z)]
. Note that
B_{z}\subseteq B_{S}
. Let
\mathcal{G}=\{\eta:\eta\textrm{ is a compatible }K\textrm{-qi section}\textrm{ over }B_{S}\textrm{ through }z\textrm{ lying inside }\mathfrak{Y}\}
. We put an order ‘
\leq
’ (inclusion) on
\mathcal{G}
as follows. For
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}
, we say
\eta\leq\eta^{\prime}
if and only if
\eta\subseteq\eta^{\prime}
. This order ‘
\leq
’ makes
\mathcal{G}
a poset. It is easy to see every chain has an upper bound in
\mathcal{G}
. Therefore, by Zorn’s lemma, we get a maximal compatible
K
-qi section through
z
over a base, say,
B_{S}
containing
B_{z}
and contained in
\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
. By abusing notation, we still denote the base for this maximal section by
B_{z}
.

(e)
One also can introduce other (uniform) constants for quasiconvexity of sets
Q_{b,v}
in
F_{b,v}
and qi embedding of
B^{\prime}
in
\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
instead of
2\delta_{0}
and
(1,6\delta_{0})
respectively. However, for simplicity, we will exclusively work with these constants.

(f)
Later on, in our statements, we suppress the dependence on the constants
C,\epsilon
and the other structural constants of the tree of metric bundles when dealing with semicontinuous family.

Now we will see nice properties (see Theorem
6.3
, Proposition
6.4
and Corollary
6.5
) enjoyed by semicontinuous families. In the proof, we use the same notations as in the Definition
6.1
. First, we prove that there is uniformly coarsely Lipschitz retraction of
X
on semicontinuous families. This is motivated by Mitra’s retraction in
[
Mit98a
]
(see also
[
MS12
, Theorem
3.2
]
and
[
KS22
, Theorem
3.3
]
). In this thesis, we refer this retraction as
Mitra’s retraction
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Given
K\geq 1,\epsilon\geq 0
and
C\geq 0
there is a constant
L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}(K)
such that the following holds.

If
\mathfrak{Y}
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family (as in Definition
6.1
) in
X
, then there is
L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}
-coarsely Lipschitz retraction
\rho_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}=\rho_{\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}}:X\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
of
X
on
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
X_{vsp}=\cup_{u\in T}X_{u}
and
x,y\in X_{vsp}
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq 1
. Then by Lemma
2.1
, it is enough to define a map
\rho:X_{vsp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
for which
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))
is uniformly bounded.

Let us define
\rho:X_{vsp}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
as follows. Suppose
x\in X_{vsp}
and
b=\pi_{X}(x),u=\pi(x)
. If
b\in B^{\prime}
, then
\rho(x)
is defined to be a nearest point projection on
Q_{b,u}
in metric
F_{b,u}
. Now suppose
b\notin B^{\prime}
. Let
a
be a nearest point projection of
b
on
B^{\prime}
and
\pi_{B}(a)=v
. Since
B^{\prime}
is
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedded in
\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
, so is in
B
. Then
B^{\prime}
is
K^{\prime}
-quasiconvex in
B
, where
K^{\prime}=K_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},max\{1,6\delta_{0}\},0)
. Note that
a
is coarsely well defined. We also assume that
a_{-}\in[a,b]_{B}
such that
a\neq a_{-}
and
d_{B}(a,a_{-})\leq 1
, and let
\pi_{B}(a_{-})=w
. Let
x^{\prime}
be a nearest point projection of
x
on
F_{a_{-},w}
in the metric
X
. Then we define
\rho(x)
as nearest point projection of
x^{\prime}
on
Q_{a,v}
in the path metric
F_{aa_{-}}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}([a,a_{-}]_{B})
.

Now we prove
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))
is uniformly bounded where
x,y\in X_{vsp}
and
d_{X}(x,y)\leq 1
. Let
\pi_{X}(x)=a,~\pi_{X}(y)=b
and
\pi(x)=v,~\pi(y)=w
. We consider the following cases, depending on the position of
a,b,v
and
w
.

Case 1
: Suppose
a,b\in B^{\prime}
. We consider two subcases, depending on whether
v=w
or
v\neq w
.

Subcase
(1A): Suppose
v=w
. We proof it by dividing into two parts, when
a=b
and
a\neq b
.

Subsubcase
(1AA): Suppose
a=b
. Since
Q_{a,v}
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{a,v}
, by Lemma
2.20

(1)
,
d^{f}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq 2C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},2\delta_{0})=L_{1}
(say).

Subsubcase
(1AB): Suppose
a\neq b
. Note that
d_{B}(a,b)\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq 1
. Now through each point in
\mathfrak{Y}\cap X_{v}
there is
K
-qi section over
B_{v}
lying inside
\mathfrak{Y}\cap X_{v}
. Define a map
\psi:F_{a,v}\rightarrow F_{b,v}
as follows. For
z\in Q_{a,v}
take
\psi(z)\in Q_{b,v}
such that
d_{X_{v}}(\psi(z),z)\leq 2K
. For
z\notin Q_{a,v}
take
\psi(z)\in F_{b,v}
such that
d(\psi(z),z)\leq c_{0}
(as in Definition
5.2
). In either case,
d_{X_{v}}(\psi(z),z)\leq 2K,\forall~z\in F_{a,v}
. Then by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.15
]
,
\psi
is
g(2K+c_{0})
-quasi-isometry for some function
g:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Again
\forall~\xi\in Q_{b,v},\exists~\eta\in Q_{a,v}
such that
d_{X_{v}}(\xi,\eta)\leq 2K
and
\eta
is further
2K
-close to a point in
\psi(Q_{a,v})
, i.e.
Q_{b,v}\subseteq N_{4K}(\psi(Q_{a,v}))
in the metric of
X_{v}
. Since fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded,
Q_{b,v}\subseteq N^{f}_{\phi(4K)}(Q_{a,v})
(see
5.10
for notation). Then
\psi(Q_{a,v})\subseteq Q_{b,v}
implies
Hd^{f}(\psi(Q_{a,v}),Q_{b,v})\leq\phi(4K)
in the metric of
F_{b,v}
.

Let
y_{1}
be a nearest point projection of
y
on
\psi(Q_{a,v})
in the metric of
F_{b,v}
. Now by Lemma
2.21

(1)
, there is a constant
K_{1}=K_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},g(2K+c_{0}),2\delta_{0})\geq 2\delta_{0}
such that
\psi(Q_{a,v})
is
K_{1}
-quasiconvex in
F_{a,v}
, and so by Lemma
2.20

(2)
,
d^{f}(y_{1},\rho(y))\leq E_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K_{1},\phi(4K))
. Again Lemma
2.21

(2)
says that there is
D=D_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},g(2K+c_{0}),2\delta_{0})
for which
d_{X_{v}}(\psi(\rho(x)),y_{1})\leq D
. By the definition of
\psi
, we also have
d_{X_{v}}(\rho(x),\psi(\rho(x)))\leq 2K
. Hence combining these four inequalities, we have
L_{2}=2K+D_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},g(2K+c_{0}),2\delta_{0})+E_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K_{1},\phi(4K))
such that
d_{X_{v}}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq L_{2}
.

Subcase
(1B): Suppose
v\neq w
. Then it follows that
d_{X}(x,y)=1
,
d_{B}(a,b)=1
,
x\in F_{\mathfrak{v},v},~y\in F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
a\in B_{v}
,
b\in B_{w}
. To make things notationally consistent, we assume that
a=\mathfrak{v},b=\mathfrak{w}
. Note that
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]\subseteq B^{\prime}\subseteq\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
. Now irrespective of whether
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is an edge in
\mathfrak{B}
or not, we have
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}),Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{2K,\epsilon\}
. Then by Lemma
3.4
,
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq R_{\ref{need-in-mitra's-proj}}(2\delta_{0},K,max\{2K,\epsilon\})=L_{3}
(say).

Case 2
: Suppose one of
a,b
belongs to
B^{\prime}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
a\in B^{\prime}
and
b\notin B^{\prime}
. Here we also consider the following subcases, depending on whether
v=w
or
v\neq w
.

Subcase
(2A): Suppose
v=w
. Let
\pi_{X}(\rho(y))=a^{\prime}
. Then
d_{B}(a,a^{\prime})\leq 2
, and so
Hd_{X_{v}}(Q_{a,v},Q_{a^{\prime},v})

\leq 2K+K=3K
. Again, since diam
{}^{f}(Q_{b,v})\leq C
then diam
(Q_{a,v})\leq 4K+C
. Thus diam
{}^{f}(Q_{a,v})\leq\phi(4K+C)
, and so
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq 3K+\phi(4K+C)=L_{4}
(say).

Subcase
(2B): Suppose
v\neq w
. For the consistency of notation, we assume that
a=\mathfrak{v},b=\mathfrak{w}
. Without loss of generality, we let
d_{T}(\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime}),v)<d_{T}(\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime}),w)
. Note that in this case,
\rho(y)
is nearest point projection of
y
on
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Then by Lemma
3.5
,
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq R_{\ref{need-in-mitra's-proj1}}(2\delta_{0})=L_{5}
(say).

Case 3
: Suppose
a,b\notin B^{\prime}
. Let
a^{\prime}=\pi_{X}(\rho(x)),b^{\prime}=\pi_{X}(\rho(y))
and
a^{\prime}_{-}\in[a^{\prime},a]_{B}
,
b^{\prime}_{-}\in[b^{\prime},b]_{B}
such that
a^{\prime}_{-}\neq a^{\prime}
,
b^{\prime}_{-}\neq b^{\prime}
and
d_{B}(a^{\prime},a^{\prime}_{-})\leq 1,d_{B}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime}_{-})\leq 1
. Since
d_{X}(x,y)\leq 1
, then
\pi_{B}(a^{\prime})=\pi_{B}(b^{\prime})
and
\pi_{B}(a^{\prime}_{-})=\pi_{B}(b^{\prime}_{-})
. Let us rename
\pi_{B}(a^{\prime})
as
v
and
\pi_{B}(a^{\prime}_{-})
as
w
not to make notation-heavy. We consider the following subcases depending on whether
v=w
or
v\neq w
.

Subcase
(3A): Suppose
v=w
. Since
B^{\prime}
is
K^{\prime}
-quasiconvex in
B
(where
K^{\prime}=K_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{0},max\{1,6\delta_{0}\},0)
) and
d_{B}(a,b)\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq 1
, so by Lemma
2.20

(1)
,
d_{B}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\leq 2C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K^{\prime})
. Note that
\mathfrak{Y}\cap X_{v}
forms a
K
-metric bundle and so
Hd_{X_{v}}(Q_{a^{\prime},v},Q_{b^{\prime},v})\leq 2KC_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K^{\prime})+K
. Since
a^{\prime}_{-},b^{\prime}_{-}\in B_{v}
then diam
{}^{f}(Q_{a^{\prime},v})\leq\phi(4K+C)
and diam
{}^{f}(Q_{b^{\prime},v})\leq\phi(4K+C)
(see
Subcase
(2A) for instance). Hence
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq 2KC_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K^{\prime})+K+\phi(4K+C)=L_{6}
(say).

Subcase
(3B): Suppose
v\neq w
. For the consistency of notation, we assume that
a^{\prime}=b^{\prime}=\mathfrak{v}
and
a^{\prime}_{-}=b^{\prime}_{-}=\mathfrak{w}
. In the same way, we consider the following two subcases.

Subsubcase
(3BA): Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be an edge in
\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
d_{B}(\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime}),v)<d_{B}(\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime}),w)
. By the assumption diam
{}^{f}(Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq C
and so
diam(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq 2\epsilon+C
in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Then by Lemma
2.18
, there is a constant
C_{1}
depending on
\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{0}
and
2\epsilon+C
such that the pair
(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C_{1}
-cobounded in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Therefore,
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq C_{1}=L_{7}
(say).

Subsubcase
(3BB): Suppose
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is not an edge in
\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
. Then by definition of semicontinuous family
\mathfrak{Y}
, the pair
(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. So
d_{X}(\rho(x),\rho(y))\leq C
.

Suppose
L=max\{L_{i},C:1\leq i\leq 7\}=max\{L_{i}:1\leq i\leq 7\}
. Therefore, by Lemma
2.1
, one can take
L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}=C_{\ref{imp-coarse-retraction}}(L)
.
∎

Next we show that a uniform neighborhood of semicontinuous families are path connected in
X
and uniformly properly embedded in
X
with the induced path metric from
X
. As a consequence, we will see that it is also (uniformly) qi embedded in
X
(see Corollary
6.5
).

Proposition 6.4
.

Suppose
K\geq 1,C\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq 0
. Then for all
L\geq max\{2\delta_{0}+1,2K\}
there exists
\eta_{\ref{semicts-is-proper-emb}}=\eta_{\ref{semicts-is-proper-emb}}(K,L):\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the following holds.

If
\mathfrak{Y}
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family (as in Definition
6.1
) in
X
, then
N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})
is path connected and with the path metric on
N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y})
induced from
X
, the inclusion
i:N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})\hookrightarrow X
is
\eta_{\ref{semicts-is-proper-emb}}
-proper embedding.

Proof.

It is clear that
N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})
is path connected. We denote the path metric on
N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})
induced from
X
by
d^{\prime}
.

For second part, we first show that for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
,
x,y\in\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
and
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
we have bound on
d^{\prime}(x,y)
in terms of
r
; in the end, we show the same for points in
N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y})
. Fix
u\in\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime})
. We take
t
, the center of the tripod in
T
with vertices
\pi(x)
,
\pi(y)
,
u
if
[\pi(x),\pi(y)]_{T}\cap\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime})=\emptyset
; otherwise,
t\in[\pi(x),\pi(y)]_{T}\cap\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B}\cap B^{\prime})
arbitrary. Let
a=\pi_{X}(x),b=\pi_{X}(y)
. Then
d_{B}(a,b)\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
. Since the inclusion
B^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
is
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedding,
d_{B^{\prime}}(a,b)\leq r+6\delta_{0}
. Let
c\in B_{t}\cap[a,b]_{B^{\prime}}
be arbitrary. Then
d_{B^{\prime}}(a,c)\leq r+6\delta_{0}
and
d_{B^{\prime}}(c,b)\leq r+6\delta_{0}
. By taking
K
-qi lifts of geodesics
[a,c]_{B^{\prime}}
and
[c,b]_{B^{\prime}}
in
\mathfrak{Y}
(more precisely, in
\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
), we get,
x_{1},y_{1}\in Q_{c,t}
such that
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq 2K(r+6\delta_{0})
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2K(r+6\delta_{0})
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). Now
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq d_{X}(x_{1},x)+d_{X}(x,y)+d_{X}(y,y_{1})\leq d^{\prime}(x_{1},x)+d_{X}(x,y)+d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 4K(r+6\delta_{0})+r

\Rightarrow d^{f}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq\phi(4K(r+6\delta_{0})+r)
.

Note that
N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}+1}(Q_{c,t})\subseteq{N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})}\cap F_{c,t}
as
L\geq 2\delta_{0}+1
, and
x_{1},y_{1}\in Q_{c,t}
. Then by Lemma
2.22
, there is
D(r)
depending on
r
such that
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq D(r)
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})+d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})+d^{\prime}(y_{1},y)\leq 4K(r+6\delta_{0})+D(r)
.

Now suppose
x,y\in N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
. Then
\exists~x_{1},y_{1}\in\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime}
such that
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq L
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq L
. So
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq 2L+r
. Thus by above,
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq 4K(2L+r+6\delta_{0})+D(2L+nr)
. Hence combining these inequalities, we get,
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq 4K(2L+r+6\delta_{0})+D(2L+r)+2L
.

Therefore, we can take
\eta_{\ref{semicts-is-proper-emb}}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
sending
r\mapsto 4K(2L+r+6\delta_{0})+D(2L+r)+2L
.
∎

As a consequence we have the following corollary (see Lemma
2.2
).

Corollary 6.5
.

Suppose
K\geq 1,C\geq 0
and
\epsilon\geq 0
. Then for all
L\geq max\{2\delta_{0}+1,2K\}
there exists
L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}=L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(K,L):=L_{\ref{lrape_qi-emb}}(\eta_{\ref{semicts-is-proper-emb}}(K,L),L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}(K),L)
such that the following holds.

If
\mathfrak{Y}
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family (as in Definition
6.1
) in
X
, then the inclusion
i:N_{L}(\mathfrak{Y}^{\prime})\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}
-qi embedding in
X
.

Remark 6.6
.

Conclusion of Theorem
6.3
, Proposition
6.4
and Corollary
6.5
hold for
\mathfrak{Y}
as well.

6.1.
Flow space

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles as in Definition
5.2
. Let
u\in T,a\in B_{u}
. Given a subset
A_{a,u}
of
F_{a,u}
, we define (rather, construct) the flow space of
A_{a,u}
, which is a semicontinuous family in
X
with central base (possibly bigger than)
B_{u}
. The construction of this flow space is by induction as follows. Let
k\geq K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
be fixed.

Step 1
:
\mathcal{G}_{a,u}=\{\gamma:\gamma\textrm{ is a }k\textrm{-qi section over }B_{u}\textrm{ through a point in }A_{a,u}\}
. Let
b\in B_{u}
and
Q_{b,u}=\textrm{hull}\{\gamma(b):\gamma\in\mathcal{G}_{a,u}\}\subseteq F_{b,u}
; where quasiconvex hull is considered in the corresponding fiber. Note that
Q_{b,u}
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{b,u}
and by Lemma
5.12

(2)
,
\bigcup_{b\in B_{u}}Q_{b,u}
forms a
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k)
-metric bundle over
B_{u}
.

Step 2
: We extend this to other
X_{v}
by induction on
d_{T}(u,v)
. Suppose we have extended it till
X_{v}
, where
d_{T}(u,v)=n
. Let
w\in T
such that
d_{T}(u,w)=n+1
and
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. We denote
Q_{a,t}
as the intersection of the flow space we are constructing with
F_{a,t}
for
t\in T,a\in B_{t}
. We first flow
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and then by Step
1
above in the entire
X_{w}
, provided
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset
.

Let us fix
R\geq R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
and let
R^{\prime}=R^{\prime}_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},R)
. Note that
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and so is
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(see Lemma
3.3
~(2)
). Suppose
N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{R}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset
. Then by Lemma
2.16

(2)
,
P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{R^{\prime}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}=:Q^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
(say). Let
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}:=\textrm{hull}(Q^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\subseteq F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
, where quasiconvex hull is considered in
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Note that
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
.
Now we apply Step
1
to
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
by considering all
k
-qi section over
B_{w}
through points in
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
.

If
N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{R}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\emptyset
, then we will not ‘flow’
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in that direction. In other words, let
S
be the component of
T\setminus\{v\}
containing
w
. Then
\forall~t\in S\textrm{ and }\forall~b\in B_{t}
, we have,
Q_{b,t}=\emptyset
.

Now we prove the following properties which verify that the subspace we are constructing is a semicontinuous family. Let
v,w\in T
such that
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
.

Property 1
: Suppose
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}\neq\emptyset
and
N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{R}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\emptyset
. Then the pair
(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C:=D_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Indeed,
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(see Lemma
3.3
~(2)
). So by Lemma
2.16
we are done.

Property 2
: Suppose
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are nonempty. Then
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{K^{\prime}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})
for some uniform constant
K^{\prime}
.

Proof.
Let
x\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Then
\exists~x_{1},x_{2}\in Q^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
x^{\prime}\in[x_{1},x_{2}]_{F_{\mathfrak{w},w}}
such that
d^{f}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. Let
y_{1},y_{2}\in Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq R^{\prime},~i=1,2
. Note that
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
L^{\prime}_{0}
-qi embedded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(Lemma
3.3
). Then by slimness of quadrilateral in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
with vertices
x_{1},x_{2},y_{1}
and
y_{2}
, there is
x^{\prime\prime}\in Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})\leq 2D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0})+R^{\prime}+2\delta^{\prime}_{0}+2\delta_{0}
. Thus
d(x,x^{\prime\prime})\leq 2D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0})+R^{\prime}+2\delta^{\prime}_{0}+2\delta_{0}+\delta_{0}=:{K^{\prime}}
.

Property 3
: Suppose both
Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are nonempty. Then
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}),Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\epsilon
for some uniform constant
\epsilon
.

Proof.
Property (2) tells that
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{2K^{\prime}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}))
. Again by construction
P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\subseteq Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. So
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Q_{\mathfrak{v},v}),Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2K^{\prime}=:\epsilon
.

We denote
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(A_{a,u}):=\bigcup\limits_{v\in T,b\in B_{v}}Q_{b,v}
; where
K=max\{K^{\prime},C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k)\}
.

Definition 6.7

(
Flow space).

We say
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(A_{a,u})=\bigcup_{v\in T,b\in B_{v}}Q_{b,v}
is the flow space of
A_{a,u}
with parameters
k\geq K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
and
R\geq R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
. It is clear from the construction that
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(A_{a,u})
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family, where
K=K_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(k,R)=max\{K^{\prime},C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k)\},C=D_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
and
\epsilon=\epsilon_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(R)
are as in above properties.

In particular, for any
u\in T
and
a\in B_{u}
, suppose
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(F_{a,u})
is the flow space of
F_{a,u}
with parameters
k\geq K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}},~R\geq R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
, where
K=K_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(k,R)
. Then by Lemma
5.12

(1)
,
X_{u}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{K}(F_{a,u})
. In this case, we denote the flow space by
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and we say
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
is the flow space of

X_{u}
with parameters
k,R
.

Although
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(A_{a,u})
depends on the constants
C,\epsilon
and the other structural constants of
(X,B,T)
, we make them implicit in our notation.

We have defined the flow space of a subset of a fiber and of
X_{u}
for
u\in T
in Definition
6.7
. Below we make it a bit general and will use this in Section
9
.

Definition 6.8

(
Flow space of metric bundles).

Fix
k\geq K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
. Let
S
be a subtree of
T
and
R\geq max\{R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}),k\}
. Suppose
H
is a
k
-metric bundle over
B_{S}
(see Definition
5.11
). Let
H_{b,u}:=H\cap F_{b,u}
for
u\in S,b\in B_{u}
. We also assume that
H_{b,u}
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{b,u}
. Suppose
\mathcal{S}(S,1)=\{w\in T:d_{T}(S,w)=1\}
. Let
w\in\mathcal{S}(S,1)
and
v\in S
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
T_{wv}
be the connected component of
T\setminus\{v\}
containing
w
along with the edge
[v,w]
. Suppose
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Let
\mathcal{F}l^{T_{wv}}_{K}(H_{\mathfrak{v},v})
is the flow space of
H_{\mathfrak{v},v}
only inside
X_{T_{wv}}
(with the parameters
k,R
) such that
\mathcal{F}l^{T_{wv}}_{K}(H_{\mathfrak{v},v})\cap X_{v}=H\cap X_{v}
, where
K=K_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(k,R)
. We define
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(H):=\bigcup_{w\in\mathcal{S}(S,1)}\mathcal{F}l^{T_{wv}}_{K}(H_{\mathfrak{v},v})
as flow space of
H
.

It is clear that
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(H)
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family with a central base
B_{S}
, where
K=K_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(k,R)
,
C=D_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
,
\epsilon=\epsilon_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(R)
.

Let us record some constants from the above discussion in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.9
.

Given
k\geq K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
and
R\geq max\{R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}),k\}
there are constants
K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}=K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(k,R)=K_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(k,R),\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}=\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(R)=\epsilon_{\ref{flow-space-def}}(R)
and
C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}=D_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
such that the following hold.

(1)

Let
u\in T
. Then
\mathcal{F}l_{K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}}(X_{u})
is a
(K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}},C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}},\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}})
-semicontinuous family with a central base
B_{u}
.

(2)

Let
S
be a subtree of
T
and
H
be a
k
-metric bundle over
B_{S}
. Then
\mathcal{F}l_{K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}}(H)
is a
(K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}},C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}},\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}})
-semicontinuous family with a central base
B_{S}
.

Note that in Lemma
6.9
,
(2)
needs the condition
R\geq max\{R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}),k\}
, whereas
(1)
holds for
R\geq R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
.

Consider the flow spaces with parameters
k,R
as taken in Lemma
6.9
. Let
\bm{K}=K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(k,R)
,
\bm{C}=C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}
and
\bm{\epsilon}=\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(R)
. Flow spaces being semicontinuous families, we have and restate the following three results for flow spaces (see Proposition
6.3
, Proposition
6.4
, Corollary
6.5
and also Remark
6.6
), as they will be utilized extensively in Section
8
and Section
9
.

Proposition 6.10
.

There exists
L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}(K)
-coarsely Lipschitz retraction
\rho_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{F}l_{K}(Z)
where
Z\in\{X_{u},H\}
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Given
L\geq max\{2K,2\delta_{0}+1\}
there is
\eta_{\ref{fsandgss-are-proper-emb}}=\eta_{\ref{fsandgss-are-proper-emb}}(K,L):\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
such that the inclusion
i:N_{L}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(Z))\hookrightarrow X
is
\eta_{\ref{fsandgss-are-proper-emb}}
-proper embedding in
X
, where
Z\in\{X_{u},H\}
.

Corollary 6.12
.

Given
L\geq max\{2K,2\delta_{0}+1\}
, there is
L_{\ref{fsandgss-are-qi-emb}}=L_{\ref{fsandgss-are-qi-emb}}(K,L)
such that the inclusion
i:N_{L}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(Z))\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{\ref{fsandgss-are-qi-emb}}
-qi embedding, where
Z\in\{X_{u},H\}
.

Flow space being semicontinuous family, the fundamental and crucial property is the existence of qi sections through each point over the respective domain. If one carefully analyses the construction, one will realize the following. Given a qi section, one can construct, by taking larger neighborhood at the junction (more precisely, in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
), a flow space containing the qi section. The following lemma captures this property. Since it is straightforward, we omit the proof.

Lemma 6.13
.

Given
K\geq K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}},R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}))
there are constants
K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}=K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}(K)=K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(K,K),C_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}=C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})
and
\epsilon_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}=\epsilon_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}(K)=\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(K)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
S\subseteq T
is a subtree and
u\in S
. Let
\gamma
be a
K
-qi section over
B_{S}
. Suppose
\mathcal{F}l_{K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}}(X_{u})
is the flow space of
X_{u}
with parameters
K
and
K
. Then
\gamma\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}}(X_{u})
and
\mathcal{F}l_{K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}}(X_{u})
is a
(K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}},C_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}},\epsilon_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}})
-semicontinuous family.

Notation 6.14
.

We define a function
\kappa^{(i)}\longmapsto\kappa^{(i+1)}
to measure the iteration in the above Lemma
6.13
. In other words, suppose
\kappa\geq K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}},R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}))
. Define
\kappa^{(0)}=\kappa,\kappa^{(i+1)}=K_{\ref{flow-sp-contains-qi-sec}}(\kappa^{(i)},\kappa^{(i)})
. Then for the flow space
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(i)}}(X_{u})
of
X_{u}
with parameters
k=\kappa^{(i-1)}
and
R=\kappa^{(i-1)}
, we have,
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(i)}}(X_{u})\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(i+1)}}(X_{u})
.

6.2.
Ladder

Suppose
(X,B,T)
is a tree of metric bundles. Let
K\geq 1,C\geq 0,~\epsilon\geq 0
. A ladder
\mathcal{L}\subseteq X
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family with a central base, say,
\mathfrak{B}
such that fibers are geodesic segments. However, in addition, we also have the following extra properties.

(L1)
: For all
v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
(where
T_{\mathcal{L}}=\textrm{hull}(\pi(\mathcal{L}))
),
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{v}
is a special
K
-ladder (see Definition
5.11
) over
B_{v}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
forms a special
K
-ladder over
\mathfrak{B}
.

We also have the following orientation on fiber geodesic.

Notation 6.15
.

\mathfrak{T}:=\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B})
,
\mathcal{L}_{a,v}:=\mathcal{L}\cap F_{a,v}~\forall~a\in B_{v},\forall~v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
.

We fix
u\in\mathfrak{T}
once and for all. As
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{u}
is bounded by two
K
-qi sections over
\mathfrak{B}
, we set one of them as
top
and the other one as
bot
to give an orientation on
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{u}
; where the abbreviation
top
and
bot
is coming from ‘top’ and ‘bottom’ respectively. So we have an orientation for each fiber geodesics of
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{u}
as ‘
bot
to
top
’. We put orientation on
\mathcal{L}
by induction on
d_{T}(u,v)
as follows, where
v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
. Let
v,w\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
such that
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
and
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. We also assume that
w\notin\mathfrak{T}
; and it is mentioned in the end for the case
w\in\mathfrak{T}
. The orientation to fiber geodesics of
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{w}
depend on that of
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{v}
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}:=[x_{\mathfrak{v},v},y_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}:=[x_{\mathfrak{w},w},y_{\mathfrak{w},w}]^{f}
such that
top(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
bot(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Let
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v},x_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v},y_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K
. Let
h_{wv}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
be a monotonic map (see Lemma
2.8
) sending
x_{\mathfrak{w},w}
to
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
to
\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(h_{wv}(x),x)\leq k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
for all
x\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. We fix this
h_{wv}
once and for all for such
v,w
. The orientation in
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{w}
depends on the order of how
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
appear in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Let the
K
-qi sections
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
bound
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{w}
such that
\gamma_{1}(\mathfrak{w})=x_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
\gamma_{2}(\mathfrak{w})=y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. If
y_{\mathfrak{v},v}\leq\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}<\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\leq x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, then we set
\gamma_{1}
to be
top
and
\gamma_{2}
to be
bot
for
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{w}
. In other words,
top(\mathcal{L}_{a,w})=\gamma_{1}(a)
and
bot(\mathcal{L}_{a,w})=\gamma_{2}(a)
,
a\in B_{w}
. If
y_{\mathfrak{v},v}\leq\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}<\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\leq x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, then we set
\gamma_{2}
to be
top
and
\gamma_{1}
to be
bot
for
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{w}
. In other words,
top(\mathcal{L}_{a,w})=\gamma_{2}(a)
and
bot(\mathcal{L}_{a,w})=\gamma_{1}(a)
,
a\in B_{w}
. However, by renaming, we always denote
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in Im(h_{wv})
for the closest point (in the induced metric on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
) to
x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in Im(h_{wv})
for the closest point to
y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Otherwise, i.e., if
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, then we set any one of
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
as
top
and the other one as
bot
. If
w\in\mathfrak{T}
then
v\in\mathfrak{T}
. Then the monotonic map
h_{wv}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
sends
x_{\mathfrak{w},w}
to
x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
to
y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. We let
top(\mathcal{L}):=\cup_{a\in B_{v},v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}}top(\mathcal{L}_{a,v})
and
bot(\mathcal{L}):=\cup_{a\in B_{v},v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}}top(\mathcal{L}_{a,v})
.

(L2)

Quasi-isometric (qi) section in
\mathcal{L}
: Let
x\in\mathcal{L}
such that
t=\pi(x)
. Suppose
s
is the nearest point projection of
t
on
\mathfrak{T}
. Then there is a
K
-qi section through
x
lying inside
\mathcal{L}
over
B_{x}:=\mathfrak{B}\cup B_{[s,t]}
. By a qi section in
\mathcal{L}
, we always mean that it obeys the order at the junction between two metric bundles given by the family
\{h_{wv}\}
. In other words, suppose
u\in\mathfrak{T}
is fixed (as mentioned above) and
\Sigma
is a qi section in
\mathcal{L}
over, say,
B_{1}
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be an edge in
B_{1}
joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
corresponding to the edge
[v,w]
in
T
such that
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
. Then
\Sigma(\mathfrak{v})=h_{wv}(\Sigma(\mathfrak{w}))
. Thus
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma(\mathfrak{v}),\Sigma(\mathfrak{w}))\leq k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
. As
max\{K,k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)\}=k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
,
\Sigma
would be a
k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
-qi section. By abusing notation, we still say
\Sigma
is
K
-qi section.

Definition 6.16

(
Ladder).

A ladder
\mathcal{L}\subseteq X
with parameters
K\geq 1,C\geq 0,\epsilon\geq 0
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family with a central base, say,
\mathfrak{B}
along with a family of monotonic maps
\{h_{wv}\}
(as described above) and
(L1),~(L2)
.

We refer
\mathcal{L}
as
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
. Sometimes we denote
\mathcal{L}
by
\mathcal{L}_{K}
to emphasise
K
.

One can think of
x_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
as uniformly close to nearest point projections (in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric) on
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
of the points
x_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
respectively. (This uniform bound is measured in terms of
\epsilon
.) Again it follows from the definition of ladder that the pairs
([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
and
([y_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
are uniformly cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. This is proved in the lemma below.

Lemma 6.17
.

Given
K\geq 1,C\geq 0,\epsilon\geq 0
there exist
C_{\ref{for-subladder}}=C_{\ref{for-subladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
and
\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}}=\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
. Let
[v,w]
be an edge in
T
. Suppose
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
such that
w\notin\mathfrak{T}
and
d_{T}(\mathfrak{T},v)<d_{T}(\mathfrak{T},w)
. Let
z\in[\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
h_{wv}(z^{\prime})=z
for
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
(with the notation used in the Definition
6.16
). Then:

(1)
The pairs
([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
and
([y_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
are
C_{\ref{for-subladder}}
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
.

(2)

Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},z]^{f}),[x_{\mathfrak{w},w},z^{\prime}]^{f})\leq\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}},~Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}([y_{\mathfrak{v},v},z]^{f}),[y_{\mathfrak{w},w},z^{\prime}]^{f})\leq\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}}
.

Proof.

(1)
We prove only for the pair
([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
as the other one has a similar proof. For ease of notation, let
x_{1}=x_{\mathfrak{v},v},x_{2}=x_{\mathfrak{w},w},x_{3}=\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Let
x^{\prime}_{1}=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x_{1})
and
x^{\prime\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime\prime}_{1})\leq\epsilon
. Suppose
x_{4}=h_{wv}(x^{\prime\prime}_{1})
and
x^{\prime}_{4}=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x_{4})
. Note that
x_{3}\in[x_{4},x_{1}]^{f}
. Now
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime\prime}_{1},x_{4})\leq K
implies
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{4})\leq 2K
. So
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{4})\leq\epsilon+2K
. Again,
[x_{1},x_{4}]^{f}
is a
L^{\prime}_{0}
-quasi-geodesic in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(see Lemma
3.3
). Then by
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
, there is a constant
C_{1}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0}
such that
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{1},x_{4}]^{f}),[x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{4}]_{F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}})\leq C_{1}
. Let
x^{\prime}_{3}=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x_{3})
. Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{3},x^{\prime}_{1})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{3},[x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{4}]_{F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}})+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{4})\leq C_{1}+\epsilon+2K
. Again applying
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
to
[x_{1},x_{3}]^{f}
, the diameter of
P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{1},x_{3}]^{f})
in the metric
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
is bounded by
\leq 2C_{1}+(C_{1}+\epsilon+2K)=3C_{1}+\epsilon+2K
. Therefore, by Lemma
2.18
, we can take a constant depending on
\lambda^{\prime}_{0},\delta^{\prime}_{0}
and
3C_{1}+\epsilon+2K
as our required constant
C_{\ref{for-subladder}}
. So, we are done.

(2)
We only prove that
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},z]^{f}),[x_{\mathfrak{w},w},z^{\prime}]^{f})
is uniformly bounded as the other one has a similar proof. We continue with the notations used in
(1)
. From the above proof, we note that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{3})\leq C_{1}+\epsilon+2K
. So
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{3})+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{3},x_{3})+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{3},x_{2})\leq C_{1}+\epsilon+2K+K+K=C_{1}+\epsilon+4K
. Let
x^{\prime}=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x)
for
x\in[x_{1},z]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. If
x\in[x_{3},z]^{f}
, then
\exists~y\in[x_{2},z^{\prime}]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
such that
h_{wv}(y)=x
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y,x)\leq K
. So
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y,x^{\prime})\leq 2K
. If
x\in[x_{1},x_{3}]
, then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},x_{2})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}_{1})+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime}_{1},x_{2})\leq C_{\ref{for-subladder}}+C_{1}+\epsilon+4K=2(2C_{1}+\epsilon+3K)
. So
P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{\mathfrak{v},v},z]^{f})
is contained in
2(2C_{1}+\epsilon+3K)
-neighborhood of
[x_{2},z^{\prime}]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. For the other inclusion, let
\xi^{\prime}\in[x_{2},z^{\prime}]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Then there is
\xi\in[x_{3},z]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
h_{wv}(\xi^{\prime})=\xi
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\xi,\xi^{\prime})\leq K
. Then
\xi^{\prime}
is contained in
2K
-neighborhood of
P_{\mathfrak{w}}([x_{1},z]^{f})
in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. As
\xi^{\prime}
is arbitrary in
[x_{2},z^{\prime}]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. So, we take
\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}}=2(2C_{1}+\epsilon+3K)
.
∎

Definition 6.18

(
Subladder).

Suppose
\mathcal{L}
is
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder.
A subladder
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{L}
is a
(K^{\prime},C^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime})
-ladder whose fiber geodesics are subsegments of the corresponding fiber geodesics of the ladder
\mathcal{L}
and the family of monotonic maps are restrictions of the given family
\{h_{wv}\}
. The constants
K^{\prime}
,
C^{\prime}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
depend on the given ones.

Definition 6.19

(
Girth and Neck).

Suppose
\mathcal{L}
is a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
. Let
B_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{B}
and let
\ell(\alpha)
denote the length of a fiber geodesic
\alpha
. Girth of the ladder
\mathcal{L}
over
B_{1}
is denoted by
\mathcal{L}^{g}|_{B_{1}}
and defined as inf
\{\ell(\mathcal{L}_{b,v}):v\in\pi_{B}(B_{1}),b\in B_{v}\cap B_{1}\}
. For a
A\geq 0
,
~A
-neck of the ladder
\mathcal{L}
inside
B_{1}
is denoted by
\mathcal{L}^{n}(A)|_{B_{1}}
and defined as
\{b\in B_{1}:\ell(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})\leq A,~\pi_{B}(b)=v\}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder. Let
x,y\in\mathcal{L}
and
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
be
K
-qi sections through
x,y
over
B_{x},B_{y}
respectively. Suppose
B_{xy}=B_{x}\cap B_{y}
. Then the restriction of
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
over
B_{xy}
form a special
K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}(K)
-ladder over
B_{xy}
. We denote the restriction by
\mathcal{L}_{xy}
. With these notations, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.20
.

Given
K\geq 1
,
C\geq 0,\epsilon\geq 0
and
A\geq M_{K}
(coming from
K
-flaring condition) there exists
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}=K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(K,A)
such that the following holds.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
. Then for
x,y\in\mathcal{L},\mathcal{L}_{xy}^{n}(A)|_{B_{xy}}
is
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}
-quasiconvex in
B_{xy}
, and consequently, in
B
as well.

Proof.

If
\mathcal{L}_{xy}^{n}(A)|_{B_{xy}}=\emptyset
, then there is nothing to prove. Suppose
\mathcal{L}_{xy}^{n}(A)|_{B_{xy}}\neq\emptyset
and
a,b\in\mathcal{L}_{xy}^{n}(A)|_{B_{xy}}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{y}(s))>A\geq M_{K},~\forall~s\in[a,b]\setminus\{a,b\}
. So by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(a,b)\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(K,A)
. Hence one can take
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}:=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(K,A)
.
∎

We finish this subsection by noting an interesting fact; which gives a criterion for a family of geodesic segments in the fibers to form a ladder.

Lemma 6.21
.

Given
K^{\prime}\geq 1,C^{\prime}\geq 0,\epsilon^{\prime}\geq 0
, we have constants
k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(K^{\prime})
,
c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(C^{\prime})
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(\epsilon^{\prime})
such that the following holds.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}
is a collection of geodesic segments in the fibers such that:

(1)

T_{\mathcal{L}}:=\textrm{hull}(\pi(\mathcal{L}))
. For all
v\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
,
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{v}
form a special
K^{\prime}
-ladder in
X_{v}
over
B_{v}
bounded by two
K^{\prime}
-qi sections. We also have a subtree
\mathfrak{T}
in
T_{\mathcal{L}}
with the following. Suppose
v,w\in T_{\mathcal{L}}
with
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}:=\mathcal{L}\cap F_{\mathfrak{v},v}=[x_{\mathfrak{v},v},y_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}:=\mathcal{L}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}=[x_{\mathfrak{w},w},y_{\mathfrak{w},w}]^{f}
. If
v,w\in\mathfrak{T}
, then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{\mathfrak{v},v},x_{\mathfrak{w},w})

\leq K^{\prime}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y_{\mathfrak{v},v},y_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K^{\prime}
. Otherwise, if
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
, then
x_{\mathfrak{w},w},y_{\mathfrak{w},w}\in N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{K^{\prime}}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
.

(2)
In the second part of
(1)
, where
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
, we have,

Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\epsilon^{\prime}.

(3)
Let
v\in T_{\mathcal{L}},w\notin T_{\mathcal{L}}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Then the pair
(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C^{\prime}
-cobounded in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
.

Then
\mathcal{L}
is a
(k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}},c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}})
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}:=\pi_{B}^{-1}(\mathfrak{T})
.

Proof.

We only need to find
k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}
and set an orientation on the fiber geodesics along with the family
\{h_{wv}\}
of monotonic maps. Fix
u\in\mathfrak{T}
. Suppose
[v,w]
is an edge in
T_{\mathcal{L}}
with
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
. Let us fix once and for all
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{\mathfrak{w},w},\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K^{\prime},~d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y_{\mathfrak{w},w},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K^{\prime}
with
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cap[x_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}
; and in the case if
v,w\in\mathfrak{T}
, then
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=x_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. We inductively fix an orientation as discussed in Subsection
6.2
.

Now we apply Lemma
2.8
on
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
and
L^{\prime}_{0}
-quasi-geodesics
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. So we get a monotonic map, say,
h_{wv}:\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
h_{wv}
is
L_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K^{\prime})
-quasi-isometry. Also, we have
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,h_{wv}(x))\leq k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K^{\prime})
for all
x\in\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
, and
h_{wv}(x_{\mathfrak{w},w})=\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v},~~h_{wv}(y_{\mathfrak{w},w})=\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. We fix once and for all such maps
h_{wv}
.

Therefore, one can take
k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=max\{k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K^{\prime}),K^{\prime}\}
and
c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=C^{\prime}
,
\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}=\epsilon^{\prime}
.
∎

7.
Hyperbolicity of ladder

In this section, we show that a uniform neighborhood of a ladder (see Definition
6.16
) is uniformly hyperbolic with the induced path metric. We divide the proof into two cases.
(1)
Ladder with small girth (see Definition
6.19
); here we construct paths for any pair of points in the ladder and prove that they satisfy the conditions of Proposition
2.10
(see Proposition
7.1
).
(2)
For general ladder, we subdivide it into (uniformly) small (but not too small) girth ladders and show that they satisfy all conditions of Proposition
2.11
(see Theorem
7.11
). Let
K\geq 1,C\geq 0,\epsilon\geq 0
and let
\mathcal{L}_{K}
be a
(K,C,\epsilon)
-ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
. In this section, we fix
notation
\bm{L}_{\bm{Kr}}:=\bm{N}_{\bm{r}}(\bm{\mathcal{L}}_{\bm{K}})
for
r\geq 0
. Additionally, we will use the same notations as introduced in Definition
6.16
and in Notation
6.15
for ladders. In view of Remark
5.8
, in this section, we require the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
to satisfy
C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-flaring condition (see below).

7.1.
Hyperbolicity of ladders (small girth)

We refer to Remark
5.13
for the notation of
C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
,
i\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
. Fix
\kappa=C^{(3)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
for this Subsection
7.1
. Given
A_{0}\geq 0
, we let
A=max\{M_{C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)},A_{0}:i=0,1,2,3\}
, where
M_{C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)}
is coming from the
C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-flaring condition. Note that
C^{(i+1)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)\geq C^{(i)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Suppose
R\geq 2\kappa
and
A
as above. Then there exists
\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}=\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}(K,A_{0},R)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}^{g}|_{\mathfrak{B}}\leq A_{0}
(see Definition
6.19
). Then
L_{KR}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}_{K})
is
\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}
-hyperbolic with respect to the path metric induced from
X
.

Proof.

Idea of the proof:

The proof of this proposition is long, so we break it up into several cases. We first define a path, say,
c(x,y)
for a pair of distinct points
x,y\in\mathcal{L}
; and we fix it once and for all. Then we show that this family of paths satisfies Proposition
2.10
. Hence the hyperbolicity of
L_{KR}
follows.

Notation 7.2
.

Let
x,y,z\in\mathcal{L}
. We use the following notations for Proposition
7.1
. For a fixed
u\in\mathfrak{T}
,
B_{s}=\mathfrak{B}\cup\pi_{B}^{-1}([u,\pi(s)]),~s\in\{x,y,z\};~B_{xy}=B_{x}\cap B_{y},~B_{xyz}=B_{x}\cap B_{y}\cap B_{z}
. We use
\bar{x}
to denote
\pi_{X}(x)
(projection under
\pi_{X}
), and same way,
\bar{y}=\pi_{X}(y),~\bar{z}=\pi_{X}(z)
. We will denote the path metric on
L_{KR}
induced from
X
by
d^{\prime}
.

Definition of family of paths:

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{L}
. Suppose
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
are
K
-qi sections in
\mathcal{L}
over
B_{x},B_{y}
through
x,y
respectively. If
[\pi(x),\pi(y)]\cap\mathfrak{T}=\emptyset
then we take
u_{xy}
as the center of the triangle
\triangle(u,\pi(x),\pi(y))
for some
u\in\mathfrak{T}
. Otherwise,
u_{xy}
is the nearest point projection of
\pi(x)
on
\mathfrak{T}
. Let
U_{xy}=\mathcal{L}_{xy}^{n}(A)|_{B_{xy}}
be the
A
-neck of the special ladder
\mathcal{L}_{xy}
bounded by
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
(see Definition
6.19
) over the common base
B_{xy}
. Then
U_{xy}
is
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(K,A)
-quasiconvex (see Lemma
6.20
). Let
\bm{t}_{\bm{xy}}
be a nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
U_{xy}
and let
\bm{v}_{\bm{xy}}:=\pi_{B}(t_{xy})
. We take
K
-qi lifts
\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
of geodesics
\bm{\alpha}_{\bm{xy}}:=[\bar{x},t_{xy}]_{B}
and
\bm{\gamma}_{\bm{xy}}:=[\bar{y},t_{xy}]_{B}
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
respectively. Denote
\mu_{xy}=[\Sigma_{x}(t_{xy}),\Sigma_{y}(t_{xy})]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{t_{xy},v_{xy}}
.

In general,
\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}
and
\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
are not continuous. To make them continuous, we consider the following. Fix points
\bar{x}=a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}=t_{xy}
on
[\bar{x},t_{xy}]_{B}
such that
d_{B}(a_{i},a_{i+1})=1
for
1\leq i\leq n-2
and
d_{B}(a_{n-1},a_{n})\leq 1
. So we get a discrete path joining
\Sigma_{x}(a_{1})
and
\Sigma_{y}(a_{n})
with an order
\Sigma_{x}(a_{1})<\Sigma_{x}(a_{2})<\cdots<\Sigma_{x}(a_{n})
. Consider the path
[\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}]=[\Sigma_{x}(a_{1}),\Sigma_{x}(a_{2})]\cup[\Sigma_{x}(a_{2}),\Sigma_{x}(a_{3})]\cup\cdots\cup[\Sigma_{x}(a_{n-1}),\Sigma_{x}(a_{n})]
based on this discrete path. Now
[\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}]
is a continuous path. Similarly, we have the continuous path
[\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}]
path corresponding to
\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
.

We define
c(x,y):=\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}\cup\mu_{xy}\cup\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
and
[c(x,y)]=[\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}]\cup\mu_{xy}\cup[\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}]^{-}
, where
[\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}]^{-}
denotes the path corresponding to
[\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}]
with opposite orientation. We see that there is an asymmetry in the definition of
[c(x,y)]
and the number of choices are involved. However, for each unordered pair
\{x,y\}
, we fix once and for all a choices and choose either
[c(x,y)]
or
[c(y,x)]
as the path joining
x
and
y
.

Note that
Hd_{L_{KR}}(c(x,y),[c(x,y)])
is uniformly bounded. To prove condition (1) of Proposition
2.10
for our family of paths, we show that arc-length parametrization of
[c(x,y)]
is properly embedded. For condition (2), we show the slimness of paths
c(x,y)
’s; and that is enough. Later on, we will use
c(x,y)
for the notation of paths instead
[c(x,y)]
.

Different choices of geodesics,
[\bar{x},t_{xy}]
and
[\bar{y},t_{xy}]
give rise to path joining
x,y
, that are
2K\delta_{0}
(uniformly) Hausdorff close to
c(x,y)
. However, we will have to think about other two natural questions as follows.

(1)

Are
c(x,y)
and
c(y,x)
uniformly Hausdorff close?

(2)

Suppose
\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}
are two different
K
-qi sections through
x
and
y
respectively lying inside
\mathcal{L}
. Let
c^{\prime}(x,y)
be a path joining
x
and
y
coming from the construction above for the qi sections
\Sigma^{\prime}_{x},~\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}
. Are
c(x,y)
and
c^{\prime}(x,y)
uniformly Hausdorff close?

These two questions are proven in
[
MS12
, Section
3
]
for the case, metric graph bundles (see
[
MS12
, Definition
1.5
]
). However, we will establish that these are also true in our case (see Lemma
7.3
and Corollary
7.5
). The proof idea involves playing with quasiconvex subsets
U_{xy}
and lifts in qi sections.

Lemma 7.3
.

With the hypothesis as in Proposition
7.1
, there exists
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}=D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}(\kappa,A)
such that
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c(y,x))\leq D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}
.

Proof.

We can think of
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
as
\kappa
-qi sections in
\mathcal{L}
through
x,y
respectively. By our notation,
t_{yx}
is a nearest point projection of
\bar{y}
on
U_{yx}(=U_{xy})
. Let
\alpha=[t_{xy},t_{yx}]
. Also,
\alpha_{yx}=[\bar{y},t_{yx}],\gamma_{yx}=[t_{yx},\bar{x}]
; and
\tilde{\alpha}_{yx},\tilde{\gamma}_{yx}
are lifts of
\alpha_{yx},\gamma_{yx}
in
\Sigma_{y},\Sigma_{x}
respectively. Further,
v_{yx}:=\pi_{B}(t_{yx})
and
\mu_{yx}:=[\Sigma_{y}(t_{yx}),\Sigma_{x}(t_{yx})]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{t_{yx},v_{yx}}
. Finally,
c(y,x)=\tilde{\alpha}_{yx}\cup\mu_{yx}\cup\tilde{\gamma}_{yx}
. Since
U_{xy}
is
~K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex, the arc-length parametrizations of
\alpha_{xy}\cup\alpha
and
\alpha_{yx}\cup\alpha
are
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))
-quasi-geodesics (by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.31

(2)
]
). So by Lemma
2.5
, there is
D
depending on
\delta_{0}
,
3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
such that
Hd_{B}(\gamma_{yx},\alpha_{xy}\cup\alpha)\leq D
and
Hd_{B}(\gamma_{xy},\alpha_{yx}\cup\alpha)\leq D
. Again,
\alpha\subseteq B_{xy}
and
t_{xy},t_{yx}\in U_{xy}
. So by Lemma
5.7

(2)
,
\forall~s\in\alpha,~d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{y}(s))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. Below, we prove that
c(x,y)
lies inside uniform neighborhood of
c(y,x)
. Then by the symmetry of proof we will be done.

Let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\gamma_{yx}
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})=d_{B_{xy}}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D
. So by taking
\kappa
-qi lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,x))\leq d^{\prime}(\xi,\tilde{\gamma}_{yx})\leq 2\kappa D
.

Let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\mu_{xy}
. Then from above,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,x))\leq 2\kappa D+A
.

Finally, let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha_{yx}\cup\alpha
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. If
\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha_{yx}
, then by taking
\kappa
-qi lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,x))\leq d^{\prime}(\xi,\tilde{\alpha}_{yx})\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
. Again if
\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha
, then
\eta^{\prime}
is further
D
-close to
\gamma_{yx}
, i.e.
\exists~\eta^{\prime\prime}\in\gamma_{yx}
such that
d_{B}(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime})\leq D
. Therefore, by taking lifts of geodesics
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
and
[\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
and
\Sigma_{x}
respectively, we get,

\displaystyle d^{\prime}(\xi,\tilde{\gamma}_{yx})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\kappa\delta_{0}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)+2\kappa D\textrm{ (since $\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha$)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\kappa(\delta_{0}+D)+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)

Therefore, we can take
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}:=2\kappa(\delta_{0}+D)+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
so that
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c(y,x))\newline
\leq D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}
.
∎

To prove
(1)
, we first show that if we change one of the qi sections, then the path we get is uniformly Hausdorff close to the other one. In other words, suppose
\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}
is another qi section through
x
. Let
c(x,y)
and
c_{1}(x,y)
be paths coming from the pairs
(\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y})
and
(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x},\Sigma_{y})
respectively. Then
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c_{1}(x,y))
is uniformly small, say, bounded by
D
. Hence we complete
(1)
by applying twice this process. Indeed,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c^{\prime}(x,y))\leq Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c_{1}(x,y))+Hd^{\prime}(c_{1}(x,y),c^{\prime}(x,y))\leq 2D
, where
\Sigma^{\prime}_{y}
is another qi section through
y
and the path
c^{\prime}(x,y)
is coming from the pair
(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x},\Sigma^{\prime}_{y})
.

Lemma 7.4
.

With the hypothesis of Proposition
7.1
, there is
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c_1(x,y)}}=D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c_1(x,y)}}(\kappa,A)
such that
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y),c_{1}(x,y))\leq D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c_1(x,y)}}
.

Proof.

Here also we consider
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
as
\kappa
-qi sections. Let the special ladder formed by pair
(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x},\Sigma_{y})
restricted over
B_{xy}
be
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{xy}
(see Lemma
6.20
). Let
V
be the
A
-neck of the ladder
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
(restriction of
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathfrak{B}
, see also Notation
6.15
) and
U^{\prime}_{xy}
be that of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{xy}
. Notice that
V\subseteq U_{xy}\cap U^{\prime}_{xy}
. We assume that
t^{\prime}_{xy}
is a nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
U^{\prime}_{xy}
. Let
\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}=[\bar{x},t^{\prime}_{xy}]
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{xy}=[\bar{y},t^{\prime}_{xy}]
