. First, we prove that
d_{B}(t_{xy},t^{\prime}_{xy})
is uniformly small.

d_{B}(t_{xy},t^{\prime}_{xy})
is uniformly small
: We fix a point
t\in V
and a geodesic
\alpha=[\bar{x},t]
. Now for
s\in\{t,\bar{x}\}
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s))\leq A
. So by Lemma
5.7

(2)
, for all
s\in\alpha
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. Again
U_{xy},U^{\prime}_{xy}
are
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex and so by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.31

(2)
]
, the arc-length parametrizations of
\alpha_{xy}\cup[t_{xy},t]
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}\cup[t^{\prime}_{xy},t]
are
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))
-quasi-geodesics. Thus by Lemma
2.5
, there is
D
depending on
\delta_{0}
,
3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
such that
Hd_{B}(\alpha,\alpha_{xy}\cup[t_{xy},t])\leq D
and
Hd_{B}(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}\cup[t^{\prime}_{xy},t])\leq D
. Then
Hd(\alpha_{xy}\cup[t_{xy},t],\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}\cup[t^{\prime}_{xy},t])\leq 2D
. Hence
\exists~t_{0}\in\alpha_{xy}
such that
d_{B}(t^{\prime}_{xy},t_{0})\leq 3D+\delta_{0}
or
\exists~t_{0}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}
such that
d_{B}(t_{xy},t_{0})\leq 3D+\delta_{0}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
d_{B}(t_{xy},t_{0})\leq 3D+\delta_{0}
for
t_{0}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}
. Since
T
is tree and
B_{v}
’s are isometrically embedded in
B
, we can take
t_{0}\in B_{y}
. In particular,
[t_{xy},t_{0}]\subseteq B_{xy}
.

Again for
s\in\alpha^{\prime}_{xy},~\exists~s^{\prime}\in\alpha
such that
d_{B}(s,s^{\prime})\leq D
. By taking lifts of geodesic
[s,s^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
),
d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s))\leq d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{x}(s^{\prime}))+d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(s^{\prime}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s^{\prime}))+d_{X}(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s^{\prime}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s))\leq 2D\delta_{0}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)+2D\delta_{0}=D_{1}
(say). As fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(s))

\leq\phi(D_{1})
. In particular,

d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(t_{0}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(t_{0}))\leq D_{1}\textrm{ and }d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(t_{0}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(t_{0}))\leq\phi(D_{1}).

Note that
[t_{xy},t_{0}]\subseteq B_{xy}
. Now by taking lifts of
[t_{0},t_{xy}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
, we have,
d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(t_{0}),\Sigma_{x}(t_{xy}))\leq 2\kappa(3D+\delta_{0})
and
d_{X}(\Sigma_{y}(t_{xy}),\Sigma_{y}(t_{0}))\leq 2\kappa(3D+\delta_{0})
. Again,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(t_{xy}),\Sigma_{x}(t_{xy}))\leq A
. Therefore, combining all these inequalities, we have,
d_{X}(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(t_{0}),\Sigma_{y}(t_{0}))\leq D_{1}+4\kappa(3D+\delta_{0})+A=D_{2}
(say). So
d^{f}(\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(t_{0}),\Sigma_{y}(t_{0}))\leq\phi(D_{2})
. Then by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(t_{0},t^{\prime}_{xy})\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,\phi(D_{2}))
. Hence
d_{B}(t_{xy},t^{\prime}_{xy})\leq d_{B}(t_{xy},t_{0})+d_{B}(t_{0},t^{\prime}_{xy})\leq D_{3}
where
D_{3}=3D+\delta_{0}+\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,\phi(D_{2}))
.

Let us come back to the proof of Hausdorff closeness of
c(x,y)
and
c_{1}(x,y)
. We only prove that
c(x,y)
lies inside uniform neighborhood of
c_{1}(x,y)
. Then by the symmetry of the proof we will be done.

Let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D_{3}+\delta_{0}
for some
\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}
. Since
\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha^{\prime}_{xy}
, from the above paragraph,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq\phi(D_{1})
. Therefore, by taking lift of geodesic
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c_{1}(x,y))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))+d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma^{\prime}_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+\delta_{0})+\phi(D_{1})
.

Now let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\tilde{\gamma}_{xy}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\gamma^{\prime}_{xy}
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D_{3}+\delta_{0}
. Taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c_{1}(x,y))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+\delta_{0})
.

Finally, we assume that
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap\mu_{xy}
. Then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c_{1}(x,y))\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+\delta_{0})+A
.

We note that
\phi(D_{1})>A
. Therefore,
c(x,y)\subseteq N_{2\kappa(D_{3}+\delta_{0})+\phi(D_{1})}(c_{1}(x,y))
. Hence, we can take
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c_1(x,y)}}:=2\kappa(D_{2}+\delta_{0})+\phi(D_{1})
.
∎

Corollary 7.5
.

With the hypothesis of Proposition
7.1
, there exists
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}=D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}(\kappa,A)
such that Hausdorff distance between any two paths joining
x,y\in\mathcal{L}
coming from the path-construction with
\kappa
-qi sections through
x,y
is bounded by
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}
in the path metric of
L_{KR}
.

Proof.

We can take
D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}=D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(y,x)}}(\kappa,A)+2D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c_1(x,y)}}(\kappa,A)
.
∎

Now we show that this family of paths are fellow-travel (
[
KS22
, Definition
1.60
]
). In other words, any two such paths whose starting points are same and ending points are at uniform distance are uniformly Hausdorff close.

Proposition 7.6

(Fellow-travelling property)
.

For all
r\geq 0
there exists
D_{\ref{fellow-travelling-property}}=D_{\ref{fellow-travelling-property}}(\kappa,A,r)
such that the following holds.

With the hypothesis of Proposition
7.1
, if
x,y,z\in\mathcal{L}
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, then

Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z),c(y,z))\leq D_{\ref{fellow-travelling-property}}.

Proof.

We will be working with
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y},\Sigma_{z}
as
\kappa
-qi sections respectively through
x,y,z
over
B_{x},B_{y},B_{z}
inside the ladder
\mathcal{L}
(explained in Case
1
below). We consider the following three cases depending on the position of
\bar{x}=\pi_{X}(x)
and
\bar{y}=\pi_{X}(y)
.

Case 1
: Let
\bar{x}=\bar{y}
. Since the fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded, without loss of generality, we assume that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq r
. Applying Lemma
5.12

(2)
, we may assume that these sections satisfy the following inclusion property when we restrict them to
B_{xyz}
. We have three possibilities
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}},

\Sigma_{x}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{yz}|_{B_{xyz}}
or
\Sigma_{z}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xy}|_{B_{xyz}}
. (To get this one has to consider
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y},\Sigma_{z}
as
\kappa=C^{(3)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-qi sections instead
K
-qi sections.) In the Subcase
(1A)
below, we will see that the proof for the inclusions
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}}
and
\Sigma_{x}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{yz}|_{B_{xyz}}
are similar. So we proof this proposition when
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}}
and
\Sigma_{z}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xy}|_{B_{xyz}}
in the following subcases.

Subcase
(1A): Suppose
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}}
. Recall that
U_{xy}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{xy}(A)|_{B_{xy}},~U_{xz}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{xz}(A)|_{B_{xz}}
and
U_{yz}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{yz}(A)|_{B_{yz}}
are
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex (Lemma
6.20
). Since
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}}
, so we have
U_{xz}\subseteq U_{xy}\cap U_{yz}
. By our notation,
t_{xz}
and
t_{yz}
are nearest point projections of
\bar{x}
and
\bar{y}
on
U_{xz}
and
U_{yz}
respectively. Hence for
M=max\{r,A\}
,
\forall~s\in\alpha_{xz},~d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{y}(s))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
(Lemma
5.7

(2)
).

Claim
:
d_{B}(t_{xz},t_{yz})
is uniformly bounded.

Proof of the claim
: Since
\alpha_{yz}\cup[t_{yz},t_{xz}]
is
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))
-quasi-geodesic,
\exists

t\in\alpha_{xz}
such that
d_{B}(t_{yz},t)\leq D
for some
D
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
(Lemma
2.5
). Since
T
is a tree and
B_{v}
’s are isometrically embedded in
B
, we can take
t\in\alpha_{xz}\cap B_{yz}
. Then by taking lifts of geodesic
[t_{yz},t]
in
\Sigma_{y}
and
\Sigma_{z}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(t),\Sigma_{z}(t))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(t),\Sigma_{y}(t_{yz}))+d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(t_{yz}),\Sigma_{z}(t_{yz}))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{z}(t_{yz}),\Sigma_{z}(t))\leq 2\kappa D+A+2\kappa D=4\kappa D+A

\Rightarrow d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(t),\Sigma_{z}(t))\leq\phi(4\kappa D+A)
. So,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(t),\Sigma_{z}(t))\leq d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(t),\Sigma_{y}(t))+d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(t),\Sigma_{z}(t))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)+\phi(4\kappa D+A)=R_{1}
(say). Then by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(t,t_{xz})\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,R_{1})
. Hence by triangle inequality,
d_{B}(t_{yz},t_{xz})\newline
\leq d_{B}(t_{yz},t)+d_{B}(t,t_{xz})\leq D_{1}
, where
D_{1}=D+\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,R_{1})
.

Now we show the Hausdorff closeness of paths. We only prove that
c(x,z)
lies in uniform neighborhood of
c(y,z)
. Then by the symmetry of the proof we will be done.

Let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xz}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\alpha_{yz}
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D_{1}+\delta_{0}
. Note that
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]\subseteq B_{y}
. Then by taking lift of geodesic
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)+2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0})
.

Now let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\gamma}_{xz}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\gamma_{yz}
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D_{1}+\delta_{0}
. Note that
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]\subseteq B_{z}
. So, by taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{z}
,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{z}(\eta),\Sigma_{z}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0})
(Lemma
5.12

(3)
).

Finally, let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\mu_{xz}
. Then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq 2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0})+A
.

Let
R_{2}:=max\{R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)+2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0}),2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0})+A\}=R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)+2\kappa(D_{1}+\delta_{0})
. Hence
c(x,z)\subseteq N_{R_{2}}(c(y,z))
. Therefore,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z),c(y,z))\leq R_{2}
.

Subcase
(1B): Suppose
\Sigma_{z}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xy}|_{B_{xyz}}
. Here also we will do the same as in Subcase
(1A)
. Let
a
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}=\bar{y}
on
B_{xyz}
. Since
\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
has girth
\leq A_{0}\leq A
, by Lemma
5.7

(2)
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{y}(s))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M),\forall~s\in[a,\bar{x}]
. (Note that
[a,\bar{x}]
could be
\{\bar{x}\}=\{a\}
if
\bar{x}\in B_{xyz}
.) In particular,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(a),\Sigma_{y}(a))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
. Again, since
\Sigma_{z}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xy}|_{B_{xyz}}
, we have,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(a),\Sigma_{z}(a))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
and
d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(a),\Sigma_{z}(a))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
. Note that
t_{yz}
and
t_{xz}
are also nearest point projections of
a
on
U_{yz}
and
U_{xz}
respectively. So by Lemma
5.7

(1)
, we have
D_{2}=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M))
such that
d_{B}(a,t_{xz})\leq D_{2}
and
d_{B}(a,t_{yz})\leq D_{2}
. Thus
d_{B}(t_{xz},t_{yz})\leq 2D_{2}
. Now we only show that
c(x,z)
lies in uniform neighborhood of
c(y,z)
. Then by symmetry of the proof we will be done.

Let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xz}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Note that
\eta\in[t_{xz},a]\cup[a,\bar{x}]
. If
\eta\in[a,\bar{x}]
, then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
. If
\eta\in[t_{xz},a]
, then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(a))+d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(a),\Sigma_{y}(a))\leq 2\kappa D_{2}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)
.

Now let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\gamma}_{xz}
and
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in\gamma_{yz}
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq 2D_{2}+\delta_{0}
. So taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{z}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{z}(\eta),\Sigma_{z}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa(2D_{2}+\delta_{0})
.

Finally, if
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\mu_{xz}
, then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq 2\kappa(2D_{2}+\delta_{0})+A
.

Therefore,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z),c(y,z))\leq max\{2\kappa(2D_{2}+\delta_{0})+A,2\kappa D_{2}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,M)\}

=R_{3}
(say).

Let
\bm{R_{4}(\kappa,A,r)}:=max\{R_{2},R_{3}\}
.

Now for the rest of the proof for this proposition, we assume that all the paths
c(\zeta,\zeta^{\prime})
are constructed using the qi sections
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
and
\Sigma_{z}
, where
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}\in\Sigma_{x}\cup\Sigma_{y}\cup\Sigma_{z}
.

Case 2
: Let
\pi(x)=\pi(y)
. Suppose
\Sigma_{y}(\bar{x})=y_{1}
. We also assume that
\Sigma_{y_{1}}=\Sigma_{y}
. Since
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, so
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq r
. Now by taking lift of geodesic
[\bar{x},\bar{y}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2\kappa r
. Thus
d_{X}(y_{1},x)\leq 2\kappa r+r
and so
d^{f}(y_{1},x)\leq\phi(2\kappa r+r)
. Therefore, by Case
1
,
Hd^{\prime}(c(y_{1},z),c(x,z))\leq R_{4}(\kappa,A,\phi(2\kappa r+r))
.

Now we investigate on
Hd^{\prime}(c(y_{1},z),c(y,z))
. For the consistency of notation, we let
\bar{y}_{1}=\pi_{X}(y_{1})
. Let
t_{y_{1}z}
be a nearest point projection of
\bar{y}_{1}
on
U_{y_{1}z}=U_{yz}
(since
\Sigma_{y}=\Sigma_{y_{1}}
). Again
U_{y_{1}z}
is
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex and
d_{B}(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y})\leq 2\kappa r
, so by lemma
2.20

(1)
, we have,
d_{B}(t_{y_{1}z},t_{yz})\leq(2\kappa r+1)C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))=D_{3}
(say). Note that
\bar{z}=\pi_{X}(z)
and
\alpha_{y_{1}z}=[\bar{y}_{1},t_{y_{1}z}]
,
\gamma_{y_{1}z}=[\bar{z},t_{y_{1}z}]
. Then
Hd_{B}(\gamma_{y_{1}z},\gamma_{yz})\leq D_{3}+\delta_{0}
and
Hd_{B}(\alpha_{y_{1}z},\alpha_{yz})\leq D_{3}+2\delta_{0}
(note that
D_{3}>2\kappa r
). Thus
Hd^{\prime}(\tilde{\alpha}_{yz},\tilde{\alpha}_{y_{1}z})\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+2\delta_{0})
and
Hd^{\prime}(\tilde{\gamma}_{yz},\tilde{\gamma}_{y_{1}z})\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+\delta_{0})
. Hence,
Hd^{\prime}(c(y,z),c(y_{1},z))\leq 2\kappa(D_{3}+2\delta_{0})+A
.

Therefore,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z),c(y,z))\leq R_{4}(\kappa,A,\phi(2\kappa r+r))+2\kappa(D_{3}+2\delta_{0})+A=:\bm{R_{5}(\kappa,A,r)}
(say).

Case 3
: Now we consider the general case. Let
\bar{x}_{1}
and
\bar{y}_{1}
be the nearest point projections of
\bar{x}
and
\bar{y}
on
B_{u_{xy}}
respectively (see Definition of family of paths for
u_{xy}
). Let
\Sigma_{x}(\bar{x}_{1})=x_{1}
and
\Sigma_{y}(\bar{y}_{1})=y_{1}
. Since
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq r
, so
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{x}_{1})\leq r
and
d_{B}(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y})\leq r
. Thus by taking lifts of geodesics
[\bar{x},\bar{x}_{1}]
and
[\bar{y},\bar{y}_{1}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
respectively, we get,
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq 2\kappa r
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2\kappa r
. So by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq 4\kappa r+r
. Note that
\pi(x_{1})=\pi(y_{1})
. Hence by Case
2
,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x_{1},z),c(y_{1},z))\leq R_{5}(\kappa,A,4\kappa r+r)
. Since
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq 2\kappa r
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2\kappa r
, we have,
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z),c(y,z))\leq R_{5}(\kappa,A,4\kappa r+r)+2\kappa r=:\bm{R_{6}(\kappa,A,r)}
(say).

Therefore, we can take
D_{\ref{fellow-travelling-property}}=max\{R_{i}(\kappa,A,r):i=4,5,6\}=R_{6}(\kappa,A,r)
.
∎

The proof for slimness of triangle formed by three paths (as in path construction) inside special
K
-ladder (see Definition
5.11
) was done in
[
MS12
, Lemma
3.11
for small girth ladder]
in case of metric graph bundles (see
[
MS12
, Definition
1.5
]
). One can proof the same in case of metric bundles (Lemma
7.7
); which we will use in Condition
(2)
below. We state it (for small girth ladder) without proof.

Lemma 7.7
.

(
[
MS12
, Lemma
3.11
]
)

Given
k\geq 1,\mathcal{A}\geq 0
, there is
D_{\ref{slimness-in-metric-ladder(special case)}}=D_{\ref{slimness-in-metric-ladder(special case)}}(k,\mathcal{A})
such that the following holds.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}(\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime})
is a special
k
-ladder (in a tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
) bounded by two
k
-qi sections
\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}
over an isometrically embedded subspace
B_{1}\subseteq B
such that inf
\{d^{f}(\Sigma(a),\Sigma^{\prime}(a)):a\in B_{1}\}\leq\mathcal{A}
. Let
x,y,z\in\mathcal{L}(\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime})
. Then the triangle formed by paths
c(x,y),c(x,z)
and
c(y,z)
, coming from the path construction, are
D_{\ref{slimness-in-metric-ladder(special case)}}
-slim in the induced path metric on
N_{2C^{(3)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k)}(\mathcal{L}(\Sigma,\Sigma^{\prime}))\subseteq X
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
: We verify the condition
(1)
and
(2)
of Proposition
2.10
for our family of paths. Here
\mathcal{L}
is
R
-dense in
L_{KR}
. We will be working with
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y},\Sigma_{z}
as
\kappa
-qi sections (explained in Condition
(2)
, Case
1
below).

Condition (1)
:
Let
x,y\in\mathcal{L}
such that
d_{X}(x,y)=r
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We want to show that the length of
c(x,y)
in the path metric of
(L_{KR},d^{\prime})
is bounded in terms of
r
. Let
c\in[\bar{x},\bar{y}]\cap B_{u_{xy}}
and
c_{1}\in[\bar{x},t_{xy}]\cap B_{u_{xy}},c_{2}\in[\bar{y},t_{xy}]\cap B_{u_{xy}}
such that
d_{B}(c,c_{i})\leq\delta_{0},~i=1,2
. (We refer to the ’definition of family of paths’ for
u_{xy}
.) Since
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, so
d_{B}(\bar{x},c)\leq r
and
d_{B}(\bar{y},c)\leq r
. Let
\Sigma_{x}(c)=\{x_{1}\}
and
\Sigma_{y}(c)=\{y_{1}\}
. Now taking lifts of
[\bar{x},c]
and
[\bar{y},c]
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
respectively, we have,
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq 2n\kappa
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2r\kappa
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). Then by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq r(4\kappa+1)
. So
d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(c_{1}),\Sigma_{y}(c_{1}))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(c_{1}),\Sigma_{x}(c))+d_{X}(\Sigma_{x}(c),\Sigma_{y}(c))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(c),\Sigma_{y}(c_{1}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}+r(4\kappa+1)+2\kappa\delta_{0}=4\kappa\delta_{0}+r(4\kappa+1)
. Thus
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(c_{1}),\Sigma_{y}(c_{1}))\leq\phi(4\kappa\delta_{0}+r(4\kappa+1))
. Since
t_{xy}
is a nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
U_{xy}
, by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(c_{1},t_{xy})\leq D
, where
D=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,\phi(4\kappa\delta_{0}+n(4\kappa+1)))
. So
d_{B}(c_{2},t_{xy})\leq d_{B}(c_{2},c_{1})+d_{B}(c_{1},t_{xy})\leq 2\delta_{0}+D
. Again
d_{B}(\bar{x},c_{1})\leq r+\delta_{0}
and
d_{B}(\bar{y},c_{2})\leq r+\delta_{0}
. Hence
d_{B}(\bar{x},t_{xy})\leq d_{B}(\bar{x},c_{1})+d_{B}(c_{1},t_{xy})\leq r+\delta_{0}+D
and
d_{B}(\bar{y},t_{xy})\leq d_{B}(\bar{y},c_{2})+d_{B}(c_{2},t_{xy})\leq r+3\delta_{0}+D
. Note that
\alpha_{xy}=[\bar{x},t_{xy}]
and
\gamma_{xy}=[\bar{y},t_{xy}]
. Therefore, by taking lifts of
\alpha_{xy}
and
\gamma_{xy}
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
respectively, we see that the length of
c(x,y)
is bounded by
2\kappa(r+\delta_{0}+D)+A+2\kappa(r+3\delta_{0}+D)=4\kappa(r+D+2\delta_{0})+A
. So the paths
c(x,y)
are
\psi
-properly embedded, where
\psi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
is a function such that

(2)

\displaystyle\psi(r)=4\kappa(r+D+2\delta_{0})+A

Condition (2)
:
Recall
U_{xy}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{xy}(A)|_{B_{xy}},~U_{xz}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{xz}(A)|_{B_{xz}}
and
U_{yz}=\mathcal{L}^{n}_{yz}(A)|_{B_{yz}}
are
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex and so is in
B
. We show that paths
c(x,y),c(x,z),c(y,z)
, coming from the above qi sections, are uniformly slim in the path metric of
(L_{KR},d^{\prime})
. Depending on the position of
t_{xy},t_{xz},t_{yz}
with respect to
B_{xyz}
, we consider the following two cases. Note that by the definition of
B_{xyz}
, either all of
t_{xy},t_{xz},t_{yz}
are in
B_{xyz}
or at most one of them is outside of
B_{xyz}
.

Case 1
: All of
t_{xy},t_{xz},t_{yz}
are in
B_{xyz}
.

In this case, without loss of generality, we assume that
\Sigma_{y}|_{B_{xyz}}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{xz}|_{B_{xyz}}
, i.e. in the ladder
\mathcal{L}
, we have the order,
bot(\mathcal{L}_{a,v})\leq\Sigma_{x}(a)\leq\Sigma_{y}(a)\leq\Sigma_{z}(a)\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{a,v})
, where
a\in B_{xyz}
and
v=\pi_{B}(a)
. (To get this one has to consider
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y},\Sigma_{z}
as
\kappa=C^{(3)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-qi sections instead
K
-qi sections, see Lemma
5.12

(2)
). Let
\bar{x}_{1},\bar{y}_{1},\bar{z}_{1}
be (the) nearest point projections of
\bar{x},\bar{y},\bar{z}
on
B_{xyz}
respectively. Let
\Sigma_{x}(\bar{x}_{1})=x_{1},\Sigma_{y}(\bar{y}_{1})=y_{1}
and
\Sigma_{z}(\bar{z}_{1})=z_{1}
. Further, we assume that the restriction of
c(x,y)
from
x_{1}
to
y_{1}
is
c(x_{1},y_{1})
. Likewise, we have
c(x_{1},z_{1})
and
c(y_{1},z_{1})
. Note that restriction of
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{z}
over
B_{xyz}
form a special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(\kappa)=C^{(4)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-ladder over
B_{xyz}
bounded by two qi sections
\Sigma_{x}|_{B_{xyz}}
and
\Sigma_{z}|_{B_{xyz}}
such that
inf\{d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(s),\Sigma_{z}(s)):s\in B_{xyz}\}\leq A
. Since
(X,B,T)
satisfies
C^{(7)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)=C^{(3)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(C^{(4)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K))
-flaring condition, so by Lemma
7.7
, the triangle formed by the paths
c(x_{1},y_{1}),c(x_{1},z_{1})
and
c(y_{1},z_{1})
are
D_{\ref{slimness-in-metric-ladder(special case)}}(C^{(4)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K),A)
-slim in the path metric of
L_{KR}
. Let
D_{1}=D_{\ref{slimness-in-metric-ladder(special case)}}(C^{(4)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K),A)
.

For the point
\xi\in c(x,y)
such that
\xi\notin c(x_{1},y_{1})
,
\xi
is
2\kappa\delta_{0}
-close to
c(x,z)\cup c(y,z)
in the path metric of
L_{KR}
. Same for others.

Note that
2\kappa\delta_{0}\leq D_{1}
. Therefore, the triangle formed by the paths
c(x,y),~c(x,z)
and
c(y,z)
are
D_{1}
-slim in the path metric of
L_{KR}
.

Case 2
:
One of
t_{xy},t_{xz},t_{yz}
is not in
B_{xyz}
(see Figure
4
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
t_{xy}\notin B_{xyz}
. In this case, we do not need to consider what exactly is happening to
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
and
\Sigma_{z}
over
B_{xyz}
.

Note that
\pi_{B}(t_{xy})=v_{xy}
. Let
t\in B_{v_{xy}}
such that
d_{B}(B_{xyz},B_{v_{xy}})=d_{B}(B_{xyz},t)
. Let us fix
s\in\mathfrak{B}\cap U_{xy}
such that
t\in[s,t_{xy}]
. (We can get such
s
as
\mathcal{L}^{g}|_{\mathfrak{B}}\leq A_{0}\leq A
, see Definition
6.19
for notation.) Since
s,t_{xy}\in U_{xy}
, by Lemma
5.7

(2)
, for all
\zeta\in[s,t_{xy}]
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\zeta),\Sigma_{y}(\zeta))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. In particular,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(t),\Sigma_{y}(t))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. Then by the fellow-travelling property (see Proposition
7.6
), we have,

Hd^{\prime}(c(x,z)|_{[t,t_{xz}]\cup[t_{xz},\bar{z}]},c(y,z)|_{[t,t_{yz}]\cup[t_{yz},\bar{z}]})\leq D_{\ref{fellow-travelling-property}}(\kappa,A,R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A))=D_{2}\textrm{ (say)}.

Again let
t_{x},t_{y}
be the nearest point projections of
\bar{x}
and
\bar{y}
respectively on
B_{v_{xy}}
. Then
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y)|_{[t_{x},\bar{x}]},c(x,z)|_{[t_{x},\bar{x}]})\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
and
Hd^{\prime}(c(x,y)|_{[t_{y},\bar{y}]},c(y,z)|_{[t_{y},\bar{y}]})\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
.

Now we only need to analyse what is happening to the paths
c(x,y),c(x,z)
and
c(y,z)
over
B_{v_{xy}}
to conclude the slimness, and here we go.

Figure 4
.
Case
2

A. The portion of the path
\bm{c(x,y)}
over
\bm{B_{v_{xy}}}
is uniformly close to
\bm{c(x,z)\cup c(y,z)}
:

Let
\xi\in c(x,y)\cap(\tilde{\alpha}_{xy}\cup\tilde{\gamma}_{xy})
such that
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\eta\in[t_{xy},t_{x}]\cup[t_{xy},t_{y}]
. First, we consider
\eta\in[t_{xy},t_{x}]
. Since
U_{xy}
is
K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
-quasiconvex,
t_{xy}
nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
and
s\in U_{xy}
, so by
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.31(2)
]
, the arc-length parametrization of
[t_{x},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},s]
is a
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))
-quasi-geodesic. In particular,
[t_{x},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},t]
is
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A))
-quasi-geodesic. Therefore, by Lemma
2.5
, there is
D_{3}
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa,A)
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq D_{3}
for some
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t_{x},t]
. So by taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa D_{3}
.

Now suppose
\eta\in[t_{xy},t_{y}]
. Then the slimness of
\triangle(t_{xy},t,t_{y})
says that
\eta\in N_{\delta_{0}}([t_{xy},t]\cup[t,t_{y}])
. Let
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t,t_{y}]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. Then by taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(y,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
. Now let
\eta^{\prime}\in[t,t_{xy}]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. Again, since
[t_{x},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},t]
is
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa))
-quasi-geodesic,
\exists~\eta^{\prime\prime}\in[t,t_{x}]
such that
d_{B}(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime})\leq D_{3}
. Taking lift of the geodesic
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
and that of the geodesic
[\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
and
d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq 2\kappa D_{3}
. Recall that
\forall~\zeta\in[s,t_{xy}]
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\zeta),\Sigma_{y}(\zeta))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
; in particular,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. So, by triangle inequality,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)+2\kappa D_{3}=D_{4}\textrm{ (say)}
.

Again, if
\xi\in\mu_{xy}
, then
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,z))\leq 2\kappa D_{3}+A\leq 2\kappa D_{3}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)\leq D_{4}
.

B. The portion of the path
\bm{c(y,z)}
over
\bm{B_{v_{xy}}}
is uniformly close to
\bm{c(x,y)\cup c(x,z)}
:

Note that the portion of
c(y,z)
over
B_{v_{xy}}
is
c(y,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{yz}
. Let
\xi\in c(y,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{yz}
such that
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\eta\in[t_{y},t]
, and the slimness of
\triangle(t_{xy},t_{y},t)
says that
\eta\in N_{\delta_{0}}([t_{y},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},t])
. First, we consider that
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t_{y},t_{xy}]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. So by taking lift of
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,y))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
. Now, suppose
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t_{xy},t]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. Recall that
[t_{x},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},t]
is
(3+2K_{\ref{neck-is-qc}}(\kappa))
-quasi-geodesic and so
\exists~\eta^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{x},t]
such that
d_{B}(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime})\leq D_{3}
(defined above in
A
). Taking lifts of the geodesic
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{y}
and that of the geodesic
[\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
and
d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq 2\kappa D_{3}
. Also,
\forall~\zeta\in[t_{xy},t],d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\zeta),\Sigma_{y}(\zeta))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
; in particular,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. Therefore, by triangle inequality,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,z))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)+2\kappa D_{3}=D_{4}
(defined above in
A
).

C. The portion of the path
\bm{c(x,z)}
over
\bm{B_{v_{xy}}}
is uniformly close to
\bm{c(x,y)\cup c(y,z)}
:

Note that the portion of
c(x,z)
over
B_{v_{xy}}
is
c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xz}
. Let
\xi\in c(x,z)\cap\tilde{\alpha}_{xz}
such that
\eta=\pi_{X}(\xi)
. Then
\eta\in[t_{x},t]
, and so
\eta\in N_{\delta_{0}}([t_{x},t_{xy}]\cup[t_{xy},t])
. If
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t_{x},t_{xy}]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
, then by taking lift of the geodesic
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,y))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}
. Now, let
\exists~\eta^{\prime}\in[t_{xy},t]
such that
d_{B}(\eta,\eta^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. Recall that
\forall~\zeta\in[t,t_{xy}]
,
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\zeta),\Sigma_{y}(\zeta))\leq R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)
. Again, if we look at
\triangle(t_{xy},t,t_{y}),\exists~\eta^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{xy},t_{y}]\cup[t_{y},t]
such that
d_{B}(\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
. If
\eta^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{xy},t_{y}]
, then by taking lifts of geodesics
[\eta,\eta^{\prime}]
and
[\eta^{\prime},\eta^{\prime\prime}]
in
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
respectively, we get,
d^{\prime}(\xi,c(x,y))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta),\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}))+d^{f}(\Sigma_{x}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}))+d^{\prime}(\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime}),\Sigma_{y}(\eta^{\prime\prime}))\leq 2\kappa\delta_{0}+R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(\kappa,A)+2\kappa\delta_{0}=D_{5}
(say). If
\eta^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{y},t]
, then the same inequality would imply that
\xi
is
D_{5}
-close to
c(y,z)
in the path metric of
L_{KR}
.

Let
D^{\prime}=max\{D_{1},D_{2},D_{4},D_{5}\}+2D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}(\kappa,A)
, representing the maximum of all constants obtained in Case
1
, Case
2
; additionally, considering Corollary
7.5
, we add
2D_{\ref{Hd-close-of-c(x,y)-c(x,y)}}(\kappa,A)
. Therefore, the triangle formed by the paths
c(x,y),c(x,z)
and
c(y,z)
, which we started with to show the combing criterion, are
D^{\prime}
-slim. Hence, by Proposition
2.10
,
L_{KR}
is
\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}=\delta_{\ref{combing}}(\psi,D^{\prime},R)
-hyperbolic, where
\psi
is defined in Condition
(1)
, equation
2
.
∎

7.2.
Hyperbolicity of ladders (general case)

Lemma 7.8

(Bisection of ladders)
.

There are constants
K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K)=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K),~C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,~\epsilon)\geq C,\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)\geq\epsilon
such that the following holds.

Suppose
z\in\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
and
\Sigma_{z}
is a maximal
K
-qi section in
\mathcal{L}
through
z
. Then
\Sigma_{z}
divide the ladder
\mathcal{L}
into two
(K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}})
-subladders,
\mathcal{L}^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}^{-}
with central base
\mathfrak{B}
such that

\displaystyle top(\mathcal{L}^{+})

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle top(\mathcal{L}),\ \Sigma_{z}\subseteq bot(\mathcal{L}^{+})\textrm{ and }

\displaystyle bot(\mathcal{L}^{-})

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle bot(\mathcal{L}),\ \Sigma_{z}\subseteq top(\mathcal{L}^{-})

Proof.

Since the proofs are similar, we prove only for, say,
\mathcal{L}^{+}
. Note that
\Sigma_{z}
is a maximal
K
-qi section over some base, say,
B_{z}\subseteq\pi^{-1}_{B}(T_{\mathcal{L}})
. Let
T_{z}=\pi_{B}(B_{z})
. There are two kinds of segments in the fibers of
\mathcal{L}^{+}
as follows.

First kind
: For all
v\in T_{z}
and for all
b\in B_{v},~\mathcal{L}^{+}_{b,v}=[top(\mathcal{L}_{b,v}),\Sigma_{z}(b)]\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{b,v}
.

Second kind
: Let
w\in T_{\mathcal{L}}\setminus T_{z}
and
v\in T_{z}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
S
be the connected component of
T\setminus\{v\}
containing
\{w\}
. If we have an order
h_{wv}(top(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))<\Sigma_{z}(\mathfrak{v})\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
(see Figure
5
left one), then
\mathcal{L}^{+}_{b,t}=\emptyset
for
t\in S\textrm{ and }b\in B_{t}
. If the order is
bot(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{v},v})<\Sigma_{z}(\mathfrak{v})\leq h_{wv}(bot(\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))
(see Figure
5
right one), then
\mathcal{L}^{+}_{b,t}=\mathcal{L}_{b,t}
for
t\in T_{\mathcal{L}}\cap S\textrm{ and }b\in B_{t}
with the same orientation as it was for
\mathcal{L}
. Also, the family of maps
\{h_{wv}\}
for
\mathcal{L}^{+}
are the restriction of that of
\mathcal{L}
.

Figure 5
.

Now with the help of Lemma
6.21
, we show that union of these fiber geodesics form a ladder. In view of Lemma
6.21
, we have to find
K^{\prime},C^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}
. By Lemma
5.12

(2)
, one observes that
K^{\prime}=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
,
C^{\prime}=C_{\ref{for-subladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon_{\ref{for-subladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
serve our purpose.

Therefore, by Lemma
6.21
,
\mathcal{L}^{+}
is a
(K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}})
-subladder in
\mathcal{L}
, where
K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(K^{\prime}),~C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(C^{\prime})
and
\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(\epsilon^{\prime})
for the above
K^{\prime},C^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}
. Since the family of maps
\{h_{wv}\}
for
\mathcal{L}^{+}
are restriction, so
k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(K^{\prime})=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
.
∎

In the same line, we also have the following lemma. Since the proof is similar to that of Lemma
7.8
, we omit the proof.

Lemma 7.9

(Trisection of ladders)
.

There are constants
K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}=K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K)=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K),C_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}=C_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,\epsilon),\epsilon_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}=\epsilon_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
such that the following holds.

Suppose
x,y\in\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
. Let
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
be maximal
K
-qi sections through
x,y
over
B_{x},B_{y}
respectively. We assume that
\forall~v\in\pi_{B}(B_{x}\cap B_{y})\textrm{ and }\forall~b\in B_{v},
we have an order
bot(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})\leq\Sigma_{x}(b)\leq\Sigma_{y}(b)\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})
in
\mathcal{L}_{b,v}
. Then we have
(K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}},C_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}},\epsilon_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}})
-subladder in
\mathcal{L}
bounded by
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
with central base
B_{x}\cap B_{y}
.

Lemma 7.10
.

For all
R\geq 2K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K)
there exists
R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}=R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K,R)
such that the following holds.

Let
x\in\mathcal{L}\cap X_{\mathfrak{B}}
and
\Sigma_{x}
be a maximal
K
-qi section in
\mathcal{L}
. Then we have two
(K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}},\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}})
-subladders,
\mathcal{L}^{+}
and
\mathcal{L}^{-}
, coming from Lemma
7.8
. Then
N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{+})\cap N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{-})\subseteq N_{R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}}(\Sigma_{x})
in both the path metric of
N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{+})
and
N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{-})
.

Proof.

For ease of notation, let
\mathcal{L}^{(1)}=\mathcal{L}^{+},~\mathcal{L}^{(2)}=\mathcal{L}^{-}
. Let
d_{i}
be the induced path metric on
N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{i}),~i=1,2
. Suppose
y\in N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})\cap N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{(2)})
and
y_{i}\in\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
such that
d_{i}(y,y_{i})\leq R,~i=1,2
. Then
d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq 2R
and so
d_{B}(\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{2})\leq 2R
, where
\pi_{X}(y_{i})=\bar{y}_{i},~i=1,2
. If
\mathfrak{T}\cap[\pi(y_{1}),\pi(y_{2})]=\emptyset
, we let
u\in T
such that
d_{T}(\mathfrak{T},[\pi(y_{1}),\pi(y_{2})])=d_{T}(\mathfrak{T},u)
; otherwise, we take
u\in\mathfrak{T}\cap[\pi(y_{1}),\pi(y_{2})]
arbitrary. Fix
c\in B_{u}\cap[\bar{y}_{1},\bar{y}_{2}]_{B}
. Then
d_{B}(\bar{y}_{i},c)\leq 2R
. Let
t_{i}
be the nearest point projection of
\pi(y_{i})
on
\mathfrak{T}
and
B_{y_{i}}=\mathfrak{B}\cup\pi_{B}^{-1}([t_{i},\pi(y_{i})])
for
i=1,2
. Then there is
K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K)
-qi sections, say,
\Sigma_{y_{i}}
over
B_{y_{i}}
through
y_{i}
in
\mathcal{L}^{(i)},~i=1,2
. Let
K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K)=K_{1}
Taking lifts of geodesic
[\bar{y}_{i},c]_{B}
in
\Sigma_{\bar{y}_{i}}
, we get,
d_{i}(y_{i},\Sigma_{y_{i}}(c))\leq 2K_{1}.2R=4K_{1}R,~i=1,2
. Then
d_{X}(\Sigma_{y_{1}}(c),\Sigma_{y_{2}}(c))\leq d_{X}(\Sigma_{y_{1}}(c),y_{1})+d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})+d_{X}(y_{2},\Sigma_{y_{2}}(c))\leq 2R(4K_{1}+1)
. So
d^{f}(\Sigma_{y_{1}}(c),\Sigma_{y_{2}}(c))\leq\phi(2R(4K_{1}+1))
. Since
\Sigma_{x}(c)\in[\Sigma_{y_{1}}(c),\Sigma_{y_{2}}(c)]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{c,u}
, so
d^{f}(\Sigma_{y_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x}(c))\leq\phi(2R(4K_{1}+1))
. Hence
d_{i}(y,\Sigma_{x})\leq d_{i}(y,\Sigma_{x}(c))\leq d_{i}(y,y_{i})+d_{i}(y_{i},\Sigma_{y_{i}}(c))+d_{i}(\Sigma_{y_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x}(c))\leq R+4K_{1}R+\phi(2R(4K_{1}+1)),~i=1,2
. So, we can take
R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}:=R(4K_{1}+1)+\phi(2R(4K_{1}+1))
.
∎

Now we are ready to state the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 7.11
.

For all
R\geq 2C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
, there exists
\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}(K,R)
such that
L_{KR}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}_{K})
is
\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic with respect to the path metric induced from
X
.

Proof.

Subdivision of ladder
:
We fix a fiber geodesic
\mathcal{L}_{a,u}
for some
u\in\mathfrak{T}
and
a\in B_{u}
. Let
K_{1}=K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K)
. We also fix
A_{0}>max\{\phi(2K+k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)),\phi(4K(2R+1)+2R+1),\phi(8KR+2R),\phi(4KD_{2}+D_{2})\}
where
D_{2}
is defined below in the verification of condition
(4)
of Proposition
2.11
. For
x\in\mathcal{L}_{a,u}
,
\Sigma_{x}
denote a maximal
K
-qi section in
\mathcal{L}
over, say,
B_{x}
. Let
\gamma:[0,l]\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{a,u}
be the arc length parametrization such that
\gamma(0)=bot(\mathcal{L}_{a,u})
and
\gamma(l)=top(\mathcal{L}_{a,u})
. Now we inductively subdivide
\mathcal{L}
into small girth ladders as follows. First, inductively we construct a finite sequence of points on
\mathcal{L}_{a,u}
and
K
-qi sections through that, which will help in subdivision. Note that set map from
\mathfrak{B}
to
bot(\mathcal{L})\cap\pi_{X}^{-1}(\mathfrak{B})
is
K
-qi section in
\mathcal{L}
. Set
x_{0}=\gamma(0)
and
\Sigma_{x_{0}}=bot(\mathcal{L})\cap\pi_{X}^{-1}(\mathfrak{B})
. Suppose
x_{i}=\gamma(t_{i})
has been constructed. Let

\Omega_{i+1}=\{t\in(t_{i},l]:\gamma(t)=x\textrm{ and }d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(s),\Sigma_{x}(s))>A_{0},\forall~s\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x}\}.

If
\Omega_{i+1}=\emptyset
, then we define
x_{i+1}=\gamma(l)
and stop the process. Otherwise, we take
x_{i+1}=\gamma(\textrm{ min }\{\textrm{ inf }\Omega_{i+1}+A_{0}/2,l\})
, and
x^{\prime}_{i+1}=\gamma(\textrm{ inf }\Omega_{i+1}-A_{0}/2)
. The construction of these points and sections stop at
n
-th step if
x_{n}=\gamma(l)
.

Claim
: Let
i>j
and
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(t),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(t))>A_{0}
,
\forall~t\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
. Then for
v\in\pi_{B}(B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}})
and
b\in B_{v}
, we have the order
bot(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})\leq\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b)\leq\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b)\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})
in the fiber geodesic
\mathcal{L}_{b,v}
.

Proof of the claim
: Indeed, because we have the family of order preserving monotonic maps
\{h_{wv}\}
, if
B_{v}
is single vertex, then we are done. Otherwise, let
b,b^{\prime}\in B_{v}
such that
d_{B}(b,b^{\prime})=1
and
bot(\mathcal{L}_{b^{\prime},v})\leq\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b^{\prime})\leq\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b^{\prime})\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{b^{\prime},v})
but
bot(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})\leq\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b)<\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b)\leq top(\mathcal{L}_{b,v})
. Let
\alpha=[bot(\mathcal{L}_{b^{\prime},u}),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b^{\prime})]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{b^{\prime},u}
and
\beta=[bot(\mathcal{L}_{b,u}),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b)]^{f}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{b,u}
. Consider the
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
-hyperbolic space
F_{b^{\prime}b}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}([b^{\prime},b])
(see Lemma
3.3
). Then we apply Lemma
2.8
, to
L^{\prime}_{0}
-quasi-geodesic
\alpha,~\beta
in
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
-hyperbolic space
F_{b^{\prime}b}
. So there is a point
z\in\beta
such that
d_{X_{u}}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b^{\prime}),z)\leq d_{F_{b^{\prime}b}}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b^{\prime}),z)\leq k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
. Thus by triangle inequality,
d_{X_{u}}(z,\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b))\leq 2K+k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K)
. Hence
d^{f}(z,\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b))\leq\phi(2K+k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K))
. Since
\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b)\in[z,\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b)]^{f}
, so
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{j}}(b),\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b))\leq d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(b),z)\leq\phi(2K+k_{\ref{monotonic-map-between-qgs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},K))<A_{0}

-
which contradicts to the fact that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(t),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(t))>A_{0}~\forall~t\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
.

Fact

1
: Hence by Lemma
7.9
, if
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(t),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(t))>A_{0}~\forall~t\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
, the
K
-qi sections
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
and
\Sigma_{x_{j}}
bounds a
(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
-subladder in
\mathcal{L}_{K}
over the central base
B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
, where
K_{1}=K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K),C_{1}=C_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
and
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K,C,\epsilon)
. If
j=i+1
, we denote this subladder by
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}=\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{x_{i}},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
.

Again, if
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(t),\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}(t))>A_{0}
,
\forall~t\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{i+1}}
, then
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
is a maximal
K
-qi section in
\mathcal{L}_{K}
through
x^{\prime}_{i+1}
over
B_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
. Also, from the construction,
\exists~a\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
and
\exists~b\in B_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}\cap B_{x_{i+1}}
such that

(3)

\displaystyle d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(a),\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}(a))\leq A_{0}\textrm{ and }d^{f}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}(b),\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}(b))\leq A_{0}

It is very well possible that
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
does not lie fully in
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
. In that case, considering Lemma
5.12

(2)
, we adjust
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
to lie inside
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
, turning it into a
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-qi section over possibly a smaller base than
B_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
. (We refer to the proof of Lemma
5.12

(2)
, i.e.,
[
MS12
, Lemma
3.1
]
.) We still denote this modified qi section as
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
and its base as
B_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
. We note that this modification will not effect to the girth condition
3
; and
K_{1}=K_{\ref{trisection-of-ladder}}(K)=C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
.

Therefore, by Lemma
7.8
, the
K_{1}
-qi section
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
subdivides the ladder
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
into two
(K_{2},C_{2},\epsilon_{2})
-subladders, where
K_{2}=K_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K_{1}),C_{2}=C_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
and
\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{\ref{bisection-of-ladder}}(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
. Let us denote these subladders of
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
by
\mathcal{L}^{i1}=\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{x_{i}},\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}})
and
\mathcal{L}^{i2}=\mathcal{L}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
. Note that
K_{2}=C^{(2)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
and the ladders
\mathcal{L}^{i1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{i2}
satisfy the small girth condition
3
.

Therefore, the ladder
\mathcal{L}
is subdivided into
(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
-subladders
\mathcal{L}^{(i)},~0\leq i\leq n-1
. Also,
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
’s are further subdivided into two
(K_{2},C_{2},\epsilon_{2})
-subladders
\mathcal{L}^{i1},\mathcal{L}^{i2}
in
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
except possibly for
i=n-1
.

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
x\in\Sigma_{x_{i}}
and
y\in\Sigma_{x_{j}}
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq D
and
i\neq j
. Then there is a point
c\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
such that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{X_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(c))\leq\phi(4KD+D)
.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{X}(x)=a
and
\pi_{X}(y)=b
. Suppose
c\in[a,b]
such that
a\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
. Since
B_{x_{i}}
’s are isometrically embedded in
B
,
[a,c]_{B}\subseteq B_{x_{i}}
and
[c,b]_{B}\subseteq B_{x_{j}}
. Now
d_{B}(a,b)\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq D
implies
d_{B}(a,c)\leq D
and
d_{B}(c,b)\leq D
. By taking
K
-qi lift of
[a,c]_{B}
and
[c,b]_{B}
in
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
and
\Sigma_{x_{j}}
respectively, we have
d_{X}(x,\Sigma_{x_{i}}(c))\leq 2KD
and
d_{X}(y,\Sigma_{x_{j}}(c))\leq 2KD
. Again by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(c))\leq 4KD+D
. Hence
d^{f}(\Sigma_{X_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(c))\leq\phi(4KD+D)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.11
: We use the following notations for the proof.

X_{i}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{(i)}),~L^{i1}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{i1}),~L^{i2}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{i2}),~0\leq i\leq n-1

From the construction, it follows that
L_{KR}=\cup_{i=0}^{n-1}X_{i}
. We will verify the conditions of Proposition
2.11
.

(1)

X_{i}
’s are uniformly hyperbolic,
0\leq i\leq n-1
.

Note that
(\mathcal{L}^{(i)})^{g}|_{B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{i+1}}}>A_{0}
except possibly for
i=n-1
(see Definition
6.19
for notation). If
(\mathcal{L}^{(n-1)})^{g}|_{B_{x_{n-1}}\cap B_{x_{n}}}\leq A_{0}
then by Proposition
7.1
,
X_{n-1}
is
\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}(K_{1},A_{0},R)
-hyperbolic. Otherwise, the ladder
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
is subdivided by a
K_{1}
-qi section
\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}
into two
(K_{2},C_{2},\epsilon_{2})
-subladders,
\mathcal{L}^{i1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{i2}
such that their girth over central base
\leq A_{0}
(see inequation
3
). Since
(X,B,T)
satisfies flaring condition, by Proposition
7.1
,
L^{i1}
and
L^{i2}
are
\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}(K_{2},A_{0},R)
-hyperbolic. Note that
N_{2K_{1}}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}})
is a connected subspace in
L^{i1}\cap L^{i2}
, and by Lemma
7.10
,
L^{i1}\cap L^{i2}\subseteq N_{R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R)}(N_{2K_{1}}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}}))
. Again the inclusions
N_{2K_{1}}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}})\hookrightarrow L^{i1}
and
N_{2K_{1}}(\Sigma_{x^{\prime}_{i+1}})\hookrightarrow L^{i2}
are
K_{1}(2K_{1}+1)
-qi embedding (see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). So by Lemma
2.3
,
L^{i1}\cap L^{i2}
is
L_{1}
-qi embedded in both
L^{i1}
and
L^{i2}
for some
L_{1}
depending on
K_{1}(2K_{1}+1)
and
R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R)
. Therefore, by Remark
2.12
,
L^{(i)}
is
\delta^{\prime}=\delta_{\ref{combi-hyp-sps-2}}(\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}(K_{2},A_{0},R),L_{1})
. Therefore, for
0\leq i\leq n-1
,
X_{i}
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic metric space, where
\delta_{1}=max\{\delta^{\prime},\delta_{\ref{hyp_small_ladder}}(K_{1},A_{0},R)\}
.

(2)
Let
0\leq i\leq n-2
. By Lemma
5.12

(3)
,
N_{2K}(\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
is
K(2K+1)
-qi embedded in both
X_{i}
and
X_{i+1}
. By
Fact

1
,
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
and
\Sigma_{x_{i+2}}
bounds
(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
-ladder. So by Lemma
7.10
,
X_{i}\cap X_{i+1}\subseteq N_{R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R)}(\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
. So by Lemma
2.3
,
X_{i}\cap X_{i+1}
is
L_{2}
-qi embedded in both
X_{i}
and
X_{i+1}
for some
L_{2}
depending on
K(2K+1)
and
R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R)
.

(3)
Let
x\in X_{i}
,
y\in X_{i+1}
and
\alpha
be a path in
L_{KR}
joining
x
and
y
.

Claim
: There is a point in
\alpha
which is
R
-close to
\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
and
\mathcal{L}^{(i+1)}
.

Proof of the claim
: Suppose this is not the case. Then there are points
z\in\alpha
,
z_{i}\in\mathcal{L}^{(i)}
and
z_{j}\in\mathcal{L}^{(j)}
such that
d_{X_{i}}(z,z_{i})\leq R
,
d_{X_{j}}(z,z_{j})\leq R
and
j-i\geq 2
. So
d_{X}(z_{i},z_{j})\leq 2R
. Then by Lemma
7.12
,
\exists~c\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{j}}
such that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{j}}(c))\leq\phi(8KR+2R)<A_{0}

-
which contradicts to the construction of
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
’s.

(4)
Now we want to prove that the pair
(Y_{i},Y_{i+1})
is uniformly cobonded for
1\leq i\leq n-2
where
Y_{i}=X_{i-1}\cap X_{i}
and
Y_{i+1}=X_{i}\cap X_{i+1}
. Since
X_{i}
’s are
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic and the inclusion
N_{2K}(\Sigma_{x_{i}})\hookrightarrow X_{i}
is
K(2K+1)
-qi embedding (see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), then
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
’s are
K^{\prime}
-quasiconvex in
X_{i}
, where
K^{\prime}=K_{\ref{quasi-goes-to-quasi}}(\delta_{1},K(2K+1),0)+2K
(see Lemma
2.21

(1)
). By similar argument, we have that
\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}
is also
K^{\prime}
-quasiconvex in
X_{i}
.

We prove that the set of nearest point projections of
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
on
\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}
in the metric of
X_{i}
is uniformly bounded; which will complete the proof. Indeed, let
\rho:\Sigma_{x_{i}}\rightarrow\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}
be a nearest point projection map in
X_{i}
such that the diameter of
\rho(\Sigma_{x_{i}})
is bonded by
D
in the metric of
X_{i}
. Then by Lemma
2.18
there is
D_{1}
depending on
\delta_{1},K^{\prime}
and
D
such that the pair
(\Sigma_{x_{i}},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
is
D_{1}
-cobounded in
X_{i}
. By
Fact

1
,
\Sigma_{x_{i-1}}
and
\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}
bounds a
(K_{1},C_{1},\epsilon_{1})
-ladder. So by Lemma
7.10
,
Hd(Y_{i},\Sigma_{x_{i}})
and
Hd(Y_{i+1},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})
are bounded by
R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R)
. Hence by Lemma
2.20

(2)
, the pair
(Y_{i},Y_{i+1})
is
D^{\prime}
-cobounded where
D^{\prime}=D_{1}+2E_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{1},K^{\prime},R_{\ref{union_of_two_ladders}}(K_{1},R))
.

Let
\rho(y_{j})=p_{j}
for
y_{j}\in\Sigma_{x_{i}}
and
p_{j}\in\Sigma_{x_{i+1}},~j=1,2
. We prove that
d_{X_{i}}(p_{1},p_{2})
is bounded by
D
. By
[
MS12
, Lemma
1.31(2)
]
, the arc-length parametrizations of
[y_{1},p_{1}]_{X_{i}}\cup[p_{1},p_{2}]_{X_{i}}
and
[y_{2},p_{2}]_{X_{i}}\cup[p_{2},p_{1}]_{X_{i}}
are
(3+2K^{\prime})
-quasi-geodesic in
X_{i}
.

Claim
:
d_{X_{i}}(p_{1},p_{2})\leq L_{\ref{lo_vs_gl}}(\delta_{1},3+2K^{\prime},3+2K^{\prime})=:D
.

Proof of claim
: On contrary, suppose
d_{X_{i}}(p_{1},p_{2})>L_{\ref{lo_vs_gl}}(\delta_{1},3+2K^{\prime},3+2K^{\prime})
. Then by Lemma
2.9
,
[y_{1},p_{1}]_{X_{i}}\cup[p_{1},p_{2}]_{X_{i}}\cup[p_{2},y_{2}]_{X_{i}}
is
\lambda
-quasi-geodesic in
X_{i}
, where
\lambda=\lambda_{\ref{lo_vs_gl}}(\delta_{1},3+2K^{\prime},3+2K^{\prime})
. Now by stability of quasi-geodesic (Lemma
2.5
) in
X_{i}
and
K^{\prime}
-quasiconvexity of
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
in
X_{i}
,
\exists~z_{1},z_{2}\in\Sigma_{x_{i}}
such that
d_{X_{i}}(p_{j},z_{j})\leq D_{2}
, where
D_{2}=D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{1},\lambda,\lambda)+K^{\prime},~j=1,2
. In particular,
d_{X}(\Sigma_{x_{i}},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})\leq D_{2}
. Then by Lemma
7.12
, there is
c\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{i+1}}
such that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}(c))\leq\phi(4KD_{2}+D_{2})<A_{0}
which contradicts to our construction of
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
’s.

(5)
On contrary, suppose
d_{X_{i}}(Y_{i},Y_{i+1})<1
. Then
d_{X_{i}}(\Sigma_{x_{i}},\Sigma_{x_{i+1}})\leq 2R+1
. Then by Lemma
7.12
,
\exists~c\in B_{x_{i}}\cap B_{x_{i+1}}
such that
d^{f}(\Sigma_{x_{i}}(c),\Sigma_{x_{i+1}}(c))\leq\phi(4K(2R+1)+2R+1)<A_{0}
which contradicts to our construction of
\Sigma_{x_{i}}
’s.

Therefore, we have shown that the collection
\{X_{i}:0\leq i\leq n-1\}
satisfies all conditions of Proposition
2.11
. Hence,
N_{R}(\mathcal{L}_{K})=L_{RK}
is
\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic, where
\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{combi-hyp-sps}}(\delta_{1},K(2K+1),D^{\prime})
.
∎

8.
Hyperbolicity of flow spaces

Suppose
R=6\delta_{0}+\theta_{\ref{modified-proj-on-qg}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})+4\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+8\delta^{\prime}_{0}>R_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})=2\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+5\delta^{\prime}_{0}
and
k=K_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}
. Let
u\in T
and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
be the flow space of
X_{u}
obtained for the parameters
R
and
k
(see Definition
6.7
). More precisely,
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
is
(K,C,\epsilon)
-semicontinuous family, where
K=K_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(k,R),~C=C_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}},~\epsilon=\epsilon_{\ref{const-of-flow-sp}}(R)
. This section is devoted to proving the (uniform) neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
is (uniformly) hyperbolic with the induced path metric.
In this section, we work with these flow spaces and these parameters
.
So we reserve

\bm{K,C}

and

\bm{\epsilon}

for the above values
. Sometimes we use the notations
\bm{\mathcal{U}}\bm{{}_{K}:=}\bm{\mathcal{F}}\bm{l_{K}(X_{u})}
and
\bm{U_{KL}:=Fl_{KL}(X_{u})}
. The idea is to apply Bowditch’s criterion (see Proposition
2.10
) to show that
U_{KL}
is hyperbolic (see Theorem
8.16
). Given a pair of points, we first find a ladder inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}
containing those points (see Corollary
8.8
), and in which we take a fixed geodesic path joining them for the family of paths to apply Proposition
2.10
. Then we show that this family of paths satisfies all conditions of Bowditch’s criterion. This strategy is elaborated in
[
KS22
, Chapter
5
]
when
X
is a tree of metric spaces. In the line of finding ladder, we prove something more in the following proposition. This proposition is kind of heart of this section. In view of Remark
5.8
, for this section, we require the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
to satisfy
max\{C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}),\mathcal{R}_{0}(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+1)\}=:\bm{k}_{\bm{*}}

-flaring condition, where
\mathcal{R}_{0}=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
is defined in the proof of Lemma
8.12
, Case
(1)
.

Proposition 8.1
.

There are constants
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}(K),c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}(K)
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}(K)
such that the following hold.

Suppose
x^{i}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\Sigma_{i}
is a
K
-qi section through
x^{i}
over
B_{x^{i}}:=B_{[u,\pi(x^{i})]}
lying inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}),

i=1,2,3
. Let
\mathfrak{B}=\bigcap\limits_{i=1}^{3}B_{x^{i}}
and
\mathfrak{T}=\pi_{B}(\mathfrak{B})
. Then we have the following.

(1)

There is
(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-ladder
\mathcal{L}^{i},~i=1,2,3
containing
\Sigma_{i}
with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
(possibly bigger) such that:

(a)

Let
S_{i}=\textrm{hull}(\pi(\mathcal{L}^{i}))
and
B_{i}=\pi_{B}^{-1}(S_{i}),~i=1,2,3
, and
B_{123}=\cap_{i=1}^{3}B_{i}
and
S_{123}=\cap_{i=1}^{3}S_{i}
. Then
\Xi=\{\bigcap\limits_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,v}:v\in S_{123},~b\in B_{v}\}
is a
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
-qi section over
B_{123}
and
\Xi\subseteq N^{f}_{5\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
.

(b)

\Sigma_{i}\subseteq bot(\mathcal{L}^{i})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K}
and
\Xi\subseteq top(\mathcal{L}^{i}),~i=1,2,3
.

(c)

\mathcal{L}^{i}\subseteq N^{f}_{6\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K}),~i=1,2,3
.

(2)

There exist
(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-ladder
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
with central base
\mathfrak{B}
containing
\Sigma_{i}
and
\Sigma_{j}
such that
bot(\mathcal{L}^{i})\subseteq top(\mathcal{L}^{ij})
,
bot(\mathcal{L}^{j})\subseteq bot(\mathcal{L}^{ij})
. Also,
\mathcal{L}^{ij}\subseteq N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
.

Although
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},~c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
depend on the constants
C,\epsilon
and the other structural constants, we keep those implicit.

Proof.

The construction of
\mathcal{L}^{i}
,
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
and
\Xi
are by induction on
d_{T}(u,v)
, where
v\in T
. As an initial step, first we explain how to get them in
X_{u}
. Note that
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{u}
is a
K
-qi section over
B_{u}
in the metric of
X_{u},~i=1,2,3
. Let
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{b,u}=\{x^{i}_{b,u}\}
,
i=1,2,3
. Then by Lemma
5.15
,
\forall~b\in B_{u}
,
\delta_{0}
-center, say,
z_{b,u}
of geodesic triangle
\triangle_{b,u}=\triangle(x^{1}_{b,u},x^{2}_{b,u},x^{3}_{b,u})
in the fiber
F_{b,u}
, forms a
k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(K)
-qi section over
B_{u}
in the metric of
X_{u}
. Let
Y_{b,u}:=\cup_{i=1}^{3}[z_{b,u},x^{i}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}},~b\in B_{u}
. We call
\cup_{b\in B_{u}}Y_{b,u}
as tripod of ladder over
B_{u}
and
[z_{b,u},x^{i}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}}
as legs of the tripod
Y_{b,u}
with vertices
\{x^{i}_{b,u}:i=1,2,3\}
. Recall that
Q_{b,u}=\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap F_{b,u}
. Now
Q_{b,u}(=F_{b,u})
is
2\delta_{0}
-quasiconvex in
F_{b,u}
implies
[x^{i}_{b,u},x^{j}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}}\subseteq N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
. So
\delta_{0}
-centers,
z_{b,u}
of geodesic triangles
\triangle_{b,u}
belong to
N^{f}_{5\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
and
Y_{b,u}=\cup_{i=1}^{3}[z_{b,u},x^{i}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}}\subseteq N^{f}_{6\delta_{0}}(Q_{b,u})
. Here
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,u}=[z_{b,u},x^{i}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}}
with
top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,u})=z_{b,u},~bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,u})=x^{i}_{b,u}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
and
\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{b,u}=[x^{i}_{b,u},x^{j}_{b,u}]_{F_{b,u}}
with
top(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{b,u})=bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,u})=x^{i}_{b,u},bot(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{b,u})=bot(\mathcal{L}^{j}_{b,u})=x^{j}_{b,u}
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
. We note that
\{z_{b,u}:b\in B_{u}\}\subseteq\Xi
(which we are constructing).

Now we assume the induction hypothesis. In other words, let
v,w\in\pi(\mathcal{U}_{K})
such that
d_{T}(u,v)=n,~d_{T}(u,w)=n+1
and
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Suppose we have constructed
\mathcal{L}^{i},~L^{ij}
and
\Xi
over
B_{t}
for all the vertices
t\in[u,v]
. Now we will explain how and when to extend
\mathcal{L}^{i},~\mathcal{L}^{ij}
and
\Xi
inside
X_{w}
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
.

We divide the construction into following cases and subcases. Before going into fabrication, let us fix some notations and collect some facts.

Notations
: We use the following notations
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,t}:=\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap F_{a,t},~\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{a,t}:=\mathcal{L}^{ij}\cap F_{a,t}
for
t\in T
and
a\in B_{t}
. Consider the nearest point projection maps by
P_{\mathfrak{w}}:F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}\rightarrow F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
,
P_{Y}:F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}\rightarrow Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and the modified projection (see Definition
2.24
) map by
\bar{P}_{Y}:F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\rightarrow Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. In the construction, we will see that either
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is a genuine tripod or a degenerate tripod (i.e., a geodesic segment). We denote the tripod, in the former case, by
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}:=\cup_{i=1}^{3}[x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},z_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}
(in Case
1
and Case
2
below); and in the later case, by
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}:=[x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f}
(in Case
3
and Case
4
below) with
top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Let
T_{vw}
be the connected component of
T\setminus\{v\}
containing
w
and
B_{T_{vw}}=\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{vw})
,
X_{T_{vw}}=\pi^{-1}(T_{vw})
.

Facts
: Let
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
be the point closest to
x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}(=bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}))
in the induced path metric of
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},~i=1,2,3
. Suppose
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}:=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
. Again if
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
then
\bar{x}^{i-1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\bar{x}^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
(
i\pm 1
is considered in module
3
). In this case, we set
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}:=\bar{x}^{i-1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Note that
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is the point closest to
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in the induced path metric of
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Suppose
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}:=P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
.

Fact

(1)
: Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Then by Lemma
2.25

(2)

(b)
,

d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}),~d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\textrm{ and }d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i\pm 1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})

are bounded by
D_{\ref{modified-proj-on-qg}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0})=D
(say).

Fact

(2)
: Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\nsubseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for any
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, i.e.,
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is in the interior of
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in the induced metric of
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. By Lemma
2.25

(2)

(a)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq D
.

Now suppose the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is not
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Then Lemma
2.16
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+5\delta^{\prime}_{0}
and so
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{Y}(F_{\mathfrak{w},w}),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq 4\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+8\delta^{\prime}_{0}
.

Fact

(3)
: Note that
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in P_{Y}(F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
. So,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 4\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+8\delta^{\prime}_{0}\leq K
. Again
d^{f}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},Q_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 6\delta_{0}
and since
R>6\delta_{0}+4\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+8\delta^{\prime}_{0}
, so
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
(see construction of flow spaces
6.1
).

Fact

(4)
: Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. By the same argument as in Fact
(3)
,
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 4\lambda^{\prime}_{0}+8\delta^{\prime}_{0}\leq K
. Now suppose that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K
. Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{Y}\circ P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 2K
and
P_{Y}\circ P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\in\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. So
d^{f}(P_{Y}\circ P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq\phi(2K)
. Again
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
\delta_{0}
-center of geodesic triangle with vertices
\{x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}:i=1,2,3\}
in the fiber
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
so
d^{f}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},[P_{Y}\circ P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]^{f})\leq 2\delta_{0}
. Thus
d^{f}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq\phi(2K)+2\delta_{0}
. Hence
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},Q_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq K+\phi(2K)+2\delta_{0}

Fact

(5)
: Fact
(3)
and Fact
(4)
also say that if
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=[x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}]
(i.e.,
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has one leg) then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K
.
Also by Fact
(1)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq D
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq D
.

Now we are ready to explain how and when to extend the tripod
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, in particular,
\mathcal{L}^{i},\mathcal{L}^{ij}
and
\Xi
, first in
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and then in the entire
X_{w}
. In the end of some cases and subcases, we make some note which will be used in Lemma
8.6
and Lemma
8.7
. We recommend the reader first to read the construction and then look at those notes while reading Lemma
8.6
and Lemma
8.7
. Also, all the time we refer to the Figure
6
.

Case 1
: Suppose
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has three legs and the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Depending on
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
is empty or nonempty, we consider the following subcases.

Subcase

(1A)
: If
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset,\forall~i\in\{1,2,3\}
, then we have tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
formed by qi sections
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w},~i=1,2,3
as described in initial step of the induction.

Subcase

(1B)
: Suppose
\Sigma_{i\pm 1}\cap X_{w}\neq\emptyset
and
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}=\emptyset
. Now we prove that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},\Sigma_{i-1}(\mathfrak{w}))\leq D_{1}
. Now the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma_{i\pm 1}(\mathfrak{w}),Y_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K
together imply
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma_{i+1}(\mathfrak{v}),\Sigma_{i-1}(\mathfrak{v}))\leq 4K+C
. Then
d^{f}(\Sigma_{i+1}(\mathfrak{v}),\Sigma_{i-1}(\mathfrak{v}))\leq\phi(4K+C)
and so
d^{f}(\Sigma_{i-1}(\mathfrak{v}),z_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq\phi(4K+C)+\delta_{0}
(as
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
\delta_{0}
-center geodesic triangle with vertices
\{\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{v}):i=1,2,3\}
in the fiber
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
). Therefore, by triangle inequality,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma_{i-1}(\mathfrak{w}),z_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq K+\phi(4K+C)+\delta_{0}=:D_{1}
.

Let
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
be the point closest to
x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
in the induced metric of
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
D_{1}
close (in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric) to a point
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Let
\gamma
be a
K
-qi section through
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
over
B_{w}
. Then we have tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
(as described in the initial step of the induction) formed by qi sections
\Sigma_{i\pm 1}\cap X_{w}
and
\gamma
.

Subcase

(1C)
: Suppose
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset
and
\Sigma_{i\pm 1}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\emptyset
. We consider the point
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
closest to
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
(in the fiber metric) which is
K
-close (in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
) to a point in
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. (Existence of such points are clear as
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset
.) We take
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
a nearest point projection of
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
on
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric. Here we have a degenerate tripod of ladder (i.e., special ladder) inside
X_{w}
formed by the qi section
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}
and a
K
-qi section
\gamma
through
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
over
B_{w}
. We set this as part of the ladder
\mathcal{L}^{i}
with
top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,w})=\gamma(a)
and
bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,w})=\Sigma_{i}(a),~a\in B_{w}
. Also, we set
\mathcal{L}^{ii+1}\cap X_{w}=\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap X_{w}
with same orientation as
\mathcal{L}^{i}
has and the other
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
’s are empty over
B_{T_{vw}}
.

Note
(1C)
: Since the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric, so by the construction of
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq C
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i\pm 1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq C
.

Subcase

(1D)
: Suppose
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}=\emptyset,\forall~i=1,2,3
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{i},\mathcal{L}^{ij}
are empty over
B_{T_{vw}}
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Figure 6
.
For ease of notation, we only use
i
,
\bar{x}^{i}
to denote
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
,
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
respectively in the figure and so on. We also omit some not to make it clumsy.

Case 2
: Suppose
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has three legs and the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is not
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. We consider the following two subcases depending on
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some
j\in\{1,2,3\}
or
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\nsubseteq\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for any
j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Subcase

(2A)
: Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some

. In this subcase, we consider further division

-

,

-

,

,

-

(2A
-

. Then we go back to Subcase
(1A)
.

(2A

-

-

, i.e.,

. Then by Fact
(3)
,

. Here we will have a tripod of ladder inside

-qi sections

over

and

.

Note
(2A
-

-

,

.

(2A
-

-
B)

). By second part of Fact

,

. Take a point

(in the fiber metric) such that

is
(K+\phi(2K)+2\delta_{0})
-close (in

-metric) to a point

. Let

be a
K
-qi section through

lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
over
B_{w}
. Then we have a tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
(as discussed in the initial step of induction) formed by qi sections
\Sigma_{i\pm 1}\cap X_{w}
and
\gamma
.

(2A
-

.

-

\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, i.e.,

. We set

. Now by Fact

over

. Also, we set

has and

.

Note
(2A

.

(2A
-
C
-

. We maintain the order of

-close (in

,

.

(2A
-

(2A

,

and

.

Subcase

(2B)
: Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\nsubseteq\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for any

, i.e.,

belong to the interior of

in the induced metric of

. We set
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
to be

provided

. We consider

-qi section through

over

if

; otherwise, the qi section to be
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}
. Then we have a tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
as described in the initial step of the induction formed by these
K
-qi sections through
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
.

Note
(2B)
: By Fact
(2)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{2K,D\}
for
i=1,2,3
.

Case 3
: Suppose
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has only one leg, i.e.,
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some
i\in\{1,2,3\}
and the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded. So
\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap X_{v}
is a special ladder bounded by two
K
-qi sections
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{v}
over
B_{v}
. Then by our construction in Case
1
and Case
