, either
\gamma_{1}
(say) is restriction of some
\Sigma_{i}
or both
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
are not restriction of
\Sigma_{i}
’s. In the later case,
\mathcal{L}^{i}
is empty over
B_{T_{vw}}
. Now we assume that
\gamma_{1}=\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{v}
. We consider the following subcases depending on whether
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}
is empty or non-empty.

Subcase

(3A)
: Suppose
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}\neq\emptyset
. Note that
top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. We set
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{w})
. Then take
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\in\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is the closest to
y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
(in the fiber metric) such that
\bar{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
K
-close (in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric) to a point
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\in Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. (Existence of such points are clear as
\Sigma_{i}\cap F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\neq\emptyset
.) Let
\gamma
be a
K
-qi section through
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
over
B_{w}
. Then we have a degenerate tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
bounded by qi sections
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}
and
\gamma
. We set this as part of
\mathcal{L}^{i}
with
top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,w})=\gamma(a)
and
bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,w})=\Sigma_{i}(a)
,
a\in B_{w}
. Also, note that this is the part of the same
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
as it was over
B_{v}
with orientation same as
\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap X_{w}
.

Note

(3A)
: Since the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
cobounded in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, so
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2K
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2K+C
.

Subcase

(3B)
: Suppose
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}=\emptyset
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{i}
is empty over
B_{T_{vw}}
.

Case

4
: Suppose
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has only one leg, i.e.,
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}=\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some
i\in\{1,2,3\}
and the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is not
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
. Then we have two extreme points
\bar{x}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
\bar{y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
of
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, which are
K
-close to points
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
of
Q_{\mathfrak{w},w}
respectively in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric (see Fact
(5)
). If
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}\neq\emptyset
, then we set
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{w})
. Suppose
\gamma
is a
K
-qi section through
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
if
\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}=\emptyset
; otherwise,
\gamma=\Sigma_{i}\cap X_{w}
. Let
\gamma^{\prime}
be
K
-qi sections through
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}\cap X_{w}
over
B_{w}
. Then we have a degenerate tripod of ladder inside
X_{w}
bounded by
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
with orientation is same as described in Subcase
(3A)
. Further, this is the part of the same
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
as it was over
B_{v}
with the orientation same as
\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap X_{w}
.

Note for Case
4
:
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\max\{2K+D\}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq D
(see Fact
(5)
).

Lemma 8.2
.

Suppose
\bar{Y}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
for some
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Then there is a constant
C_{\ref{other-pairs-are-cobounded}}
satisfying the following.

(1)

(
[
KS22
, Corollary
5.4
]
)
The pair
(\mathcal{L}^{t}_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C_{\ref{other-pairs-are-cobounded}}
-cobounded in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
for
t=i\pm 1
.

(2)
The pair
(\mathcal{L}^{i+1i-1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C_{\ref{other-pairs-are-cobounded}}
cobounded in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
.

Proof.

(1)
Fix
t\in\{i\pm 1\}
. Then by Fact
(1)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{t}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq 2D
(by triangle inequality). Again
d_{\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i-1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i+1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq 2D
. Then by Lemma
2.19
, we can take
C_{\ref{other-pairs-are-cobounded}}:=C_{\ref{cobounded-pairs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},2D)
.
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

There is a constant
C_{\ref{Lij-is-cobounded}}
satisfying the following.

If the pair
(Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is
C
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, then the pairs
(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
and
(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},F_{\mathfrak{w},w})
are
C_{\ref{Lij-is-cobounded}}
-cobounded in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.19
, we can take
C_{\ref{Lij-is-cobounded}}=C_{\ref{cobounded-pairs}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0},C)
.
∎

The above construction yields
\mathcal{L}^{i}
and
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
as a collection of geodesic segments in fibers. We still need to show that they form ladders (Lemma
8.6
and Lemma
8.7
) using Lemma
6.21
. Let
B_{i}:=\pi_{X}(\mathcal{L}^{i})
and
S_{i}:=\pi_{B}(B_{i})
for all
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Let
v\in S_{123}
. Recall for
b\in B_{v}
,
z_{b,v}
is
\delta_{0}
-center of geodesic triangle in the fiber
F_{b,v}
with vertices
\{bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,v}):i=1,2,3\}
. As we saw in the initial step of induction that
\{z_{b,v}:b\in B_{v}\}
form
k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(K)
-qi section in
X_{v}
over
B_{v}
. Therefore, to show
\Xi=\{z_{b,v}:v\in S_{123},b\in B_{v}\}
form a uniform qi section over
B_{123}
(Corollary
8.5
), we only need to prove
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},z_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is uniformly bounded, where
v,w\in S_{123}
,
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

(
[
KS22
, Lemma
5.8
]
)

There exists
k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},z_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
, where
v,w\in S_{123}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
.

Proof.

This situation happens in Subcase
(1A)
, Subcase
(1B)
,
(2A
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
B)
,
(2A
-
C
-
B)
and Subcase
(2B)
. We denote
\triangle_{v}
and
\hat{\triangle}_{v}
for geodesic triangle with vertices
\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},i=1,2,3
in
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
respectively. We also denote
\triangle_{w}
and
\hat{\triangle}_{w}
for geodesic triangle with vertices
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w},i=1,2,3
in
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
respectively. In all these cases,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{D_{1},K+\phi(2K)+2\delta_{0},K\}=D_{2}
(say),
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Since
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
\delta_{0}
-center of
\triangle(x^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},x^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v},x^{3}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
in
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, so
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
3\delta_{0}
-center of
\triangle_{v}
in
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Thus
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
(3\delta_{0}+D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0}))
-center
\hat{\triangle}_{v}
in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(as the inclusion
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}\hookrightarrow F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
is
L^{\prime}_{0}
-qi embedding, see Lemma
3.3
). Since the corresponding end points of the triangles
\hat{\triangle}_{v}
and
\hat{\triangle}_{w}
are
D_{2}
-distance apart from each other in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, then by slimness of quadrilateral in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, we get,
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is
(3\delta_{0}+D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0})+2\delta^{\prime}_{0}+D_{2})
-center of
\hat{\triangle}_{w}
. Again, as
z_{\mathfrak{w},w}
is
\delta_{0}
-center of
\triangle_{w}
and so is
(\delta_{0}+D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0}))
-center of
\hat{\triangle}_{w}
. Thus
z_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
z_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are two
D_{3}
-center of
\hat{\triangle}_{w}
in the path metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, where
D_{3}=3\delta_{0}+D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{0},L^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0})+2\delta^{\prime}_{0}+D_{2}
. Hence, by
[
KS22
, Lemma
1.76
]
, we have,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},z_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2D_{3}+9\delta^{\prime}_{0}=:k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
.
∎

Corollary 8.5
.

We have a constant
k_{\ref{Xi-form-qi-section}}=max\{k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(K),k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}\}
such that
\Xi=\bigcup\limits_{b\in B_{v},~v\in S_{123}}\{z_{b,v}\}
forms a
k_{\ref{Xi-form-qi-section}}
-qi section over
B_{123}
with
\Xi\subseteq N^{f}_{6\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
.

Here we show that
\mathcal{L}^{i}
and
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
form ladders.

Lemma 8.6
.

There are constants
k_{\ref{Li}},c_{\ref{Li}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}
such that
\mathcal{L}^{i}
is a ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
containing
\Sigma_{i}
with constants
k_{\ref{Li}},c_{\ref{Li}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}
,
i=1,2,3
.

Proof.

We check all conditions of Lemma
6.21
.

Condition
(1): Note that for all
v\in S_{i}
,
\mathcal{L}^{i}\cap X_{v}
is a special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(K))
-ladder over
B_{v}
. Let
v,w\in S_{i}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. If
v,w\in\mathfrak{T}
, then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))=d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v},z_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))=d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{v}),\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{w}))\leq K
. Otherwise, suppose
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
. Now let
v,w\in S_{123}
, i.e. both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have three legs (this happens in
(1A)
,
(1B)
,
(2A
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
B)
,
(2A
-
C
-
B)
and
(2B)
). Note that
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}=bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}),z_{\mathfrak{w},w}=top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
. Then
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
z_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are max
\{k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}},D_{2}\}=k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
-close to
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
, where

is as in Lemma

. Now let

and

, i.e.

,
(2A
-
C
-

,
(2A
-

). Then (by our construction)

are

-close to

in

-metric. Finally,
v,w\notin S_{123}
, i.e., both

and

have one leg, then

are
K
-close to
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Therefore, for the condition
(1)
of Lemma
6.21
, we can take
K^{\prime}=max\{C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K_{\ref{centers-forms-qi-section}}(K)),k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}},K\}
.

Condition
(2): Let
v,w\in S_{i}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
. To show a uniform bound on
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
, we first show that

d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})),bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))\textrm{ and }d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})),top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}))

are uniformly bounded, and then we apply
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
.

Suppose both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have three legs (this happens in
(1A)
,
(1B)
,
(2A
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
A)
,
(2A
-
B
-
B)
,
(2A
-
C
-
B)
and
(2B)
). In all these cases,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(z_{\mathfrak{v},v}),z_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2k_{\ref{zc_v-zc_w}}
. So we need bound only for
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
. In
(1A)
and
(2A
-
A)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2K
. In
(1B)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{2K,2D_{1}+C\}
. In
(2A
-
B
-
A)
and
(2B)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{2K,D\}
. In
(2A
-

-
B)
and
(2A
-
C
-
B)
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq max\{2K,2(K+\phi(2K)+\delta_{0})\}
.

Suppose
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has three legs and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
has one (this happens in
(1C)
,
(2A
-
C
-
A)
,
(2A
-
D)
). Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
and

are bounded by max
\{2K+C,2K,D\}
(see the Note in the end of each case).

Finally, we assume that both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v},Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have one leg (Case
3
and Case
4
). Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
and

are bounded by
max\{2K,2K+C,2K+D\}
(see Note for Case
3
and Note for Case
4
).

Let
D_{4}
be the maximum of all the above constants. Now by
[
KS22
, Corollary
1.116
]
, we have
C_{1}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
and
L^{\prime}_{0}
such that

Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),[P_{\mathfrak{w}}(bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(top(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}))]^{f})\leq C_{1}.

Since
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are
L^{\prime}_{0}
-quasi-geodesic in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, by slimness of quadrilateral in
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, there is
\epsilon^{\prime}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
,
D_{4}
,

and
L^{\prime}_{0}
such that
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\epsilon^{\prime}

Condition
(3): Suppose
v\in S_{i}
and
w\notin S_{i}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. That is
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\emptyset
. Then the pair

is

-cobounded in

(see Lemma
8.2
, Lemma
8.3
).

Therefore, to conclude the lemma, we take,
k_{\ref{Li}}=k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(K^{\prime})
,
c_{\ref{Li}}=c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(C^{\prime})
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(\epsilon^{\prime})
for the above
K^{\prime},C^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

There are constants
k_{\ref{Lij}},c_{\ref{Lij}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Lij}}
such that
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
is a ladder with a central base
\mathfrak{B}
containing
\Sigma_{i}
and
\Sigma_{j}
with constants
k_{\ref{Lij}},c_{\ref{Lij}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Lij}}
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Proof.

Here also we verify all conditions of Lemma
6.21
.

Condition
(1): Note that for all
v\in\pi(\mathcal{L}^{ij})
,
\mathcal{L}^{ij}\cap X_{v}
is a special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)
-ladder over
B_{v}
. Suppose
v,w\in\pi(\mathcal{L}^{ij})
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. If
v,w\in\mathfrak{T}
,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{v}),\Sigma_{i}(\mathfrak{w}))\leq K
for all
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Otherwise, we assume that
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
. In the construction, we have seen that
\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
matches with
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
or
\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
unless both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have three legs. Note that
Hd^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 2\delta_{0}
. Also,
\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and
\tilde{y}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
are
D_{4}
-close (in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric) to
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
, where
D_{4}
is as in Lemma
8.6
. Hence, in all the cases, there are points,
x_{1},y_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\tilde{x}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w},x_{1})\leq D_{4}+3\delta_{0}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(\tilde{x}^{j}_{\mathfrak{w},w},x_{1})\leq D_{4}+3\delta_{0}
with an order
bot(\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=bot(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq y_{1}\leq x_{1}\leq top(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v})=bot(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
.

Therefore, for the condition
(1)
of Lemma
6.21
, we can take
K^{\prime}=max\{D_{4}+3\delta_{0},C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K)\}
.

Condition
(2): Let
v,w\in\pi(\mathcal{L}^{ij})
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
and
d_{T}(v,\mathfrak{T})<d_{T}(w,\mathfrak{T})
. We prove that
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
is uniformly bounded.

If both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have one leg, then
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}
(by Lemma
8.6
). If
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
has three legs but
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
has one, then
\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathfrak{w},w}=\emptyset
for
s
is equal to either
i
or
j
. So by Lemma
8.6
, diam
(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq c_{\ref{Li}}
in
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
-metric. Again if both
Y_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
Y_{\mathfrak{w},w}
have three legs,
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{s}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
. Now
Hd^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{w},w},\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 2\delta_{0}
implies that in either case,
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}+2\delta_{0}+c_{\ref{Li}}
. Again
Hd^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 2\delta_{0}
and so by Lemma
2.20

(1)
,
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v}))\leq C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}).(2\delta_{0}+1)
. Therefore, combining these inequalities, we get,
Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\mathcal{L}^{ij}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\epsilon
, where
\epsilon=\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}+2\delta_{0}+c_{\ref{Li}}+C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}).(2\delta_{0}+1)
.

Therefore, for condition
(2)
of Lemma
6.21
, we take
\epsilon^{\prime}=max\{\epsilon,\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}}\}=\epsilon
.

Condition
(3): So for the condition
(3)
of Lemma
6.21
, we can take
C^{\prime}=max\{C_{\ref{other-pairs-are-cobounded}},C_{\ref{Lij-is-cobounded}}\}
(see Lemma
8.2
, Lemma
8.3
).

Hence, to conclude the lemma, we take,
k_{\ref{Lij}}=k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(K^{\prime})
,
c_{\ref{Lij}}=c_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(C^{\prime})
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Lij}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(\epsilon^{\prime})
for the above
K^{\prime},C^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}
.
∎

Therefore, to complete Proposition
8.1
, we take
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=max\{k_{\ref{Li}},k_{\ref{Lij}}\}
,
c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=max\{c_{\ref{Li}},c_{\ref{Lij}}\}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=max\{\varepsilon_{\ref{Li}},\varepsilon_{\ref{Lij}}\}
.
∎

Therefore, Proposition
8.1
gives us the following Corollary
8.8
. Below in Lemma
8.9
, we will investigate the (uniform) hyperbolicity of a (uniform) neighborhood of
\cup_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{L}^{i}
in a bit larger neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
, where
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s are the ladders obtained in Proposition
8.1
.

Corollary 8.8
.

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Suppose
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
are
K
-qi sections through
x
and
y
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}
over
B_{[u,\pi(x)]}
and
B_{[u,\pi(y)]}
respectively. Then there is a
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladder,
\mathcal{L}_{xy}
, with a central base (possibly bigger than)
B_{u}
containing the sections
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
such that
bot(\mathcal{L}_{xy})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K}
,
top(\mathcal{L}_{xy})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K}
and
\mathcal{L}_{xy}\subseteq N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
, where
k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}=k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},~c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}=c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
.

Lemma 8.9
.

Given
R\geq 2C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
, there exist
\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}=\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},R)
and
L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}=L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},R)
such that the following hold.

(1)

Y:=N_{R+2\delta_{0}}(\cup_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{L}^{i})
is a
\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}
-hyperbolic subspace (with the induced path metric) in
U_{K(R+8\delta_{0})}:=N_{(R+8\delta_{0})}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}))
.

(2)

The inclusion
L^{ij}=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{ij})\hookrightarrow Y
is
L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}
-qi embedding with their induced path metric.

Proof.

(1)
Note that
\mathcal{L}^{i}
is a
(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-ladder such that
\mathcal{L}^{i}\subseteq N^{f}_{6\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
for
i=1,2,3
, and so
Y\subseteq U_{K(R+8\delta_{0})}
. Let
L^{i}=N_{R+2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}),~i=1,2,3
. Since the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
satisfies
C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-flaring condition, by Theorem
7.11
,
L^{i}
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic, where
\delta_{1}=\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},R+2\delta_{0})
. Now we apply Proposition
2.11
twice on
L^{i}
’s,
i=1,2,3
; first on
L^{1}\cup L^{2}
and then on
(L^{1}\cup L^{2})\cup L^{3}
to show
Y
is hyperbolic.

\bm{L^{1}\cup L^{2}}
is Hyperbolic
: We verify the conditions of Proposition
2.11
for
n=2
(see Remark
2.12
).

(1)

L^{1}
and
L^{2}
are
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic.

(2)
Note that
N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi)\subseteq L^{1}\cap L^{2}
, and by Lemma
5.12

(3)
,
N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi)
is
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}(2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+1)
-qi embedded in both
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
. Let
N^{i}_{D}(N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi))
denote the
D
-neighborhood of
N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi)
in
L^{i}
-metric for
i=1,2
. If
L^{1}\cap L^{2}\subseteq N^{i}_{D}(N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi))
, then by Lemma
2.3
,
L^{1}\cap L^{2}
is
L_{1}
-qi embedded in both
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
for some
L_{1}
depending on
D
and
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}(2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+1)
. Now we will find
D
.

Finding

D
: Let
x\in L^{1}\cap L^{2}
. Then
\exists~x_{i}\in\mathcal{L}^{i}
such that
d_{L^{i}}(x,x_{i})\leq R+2\delta_{0},~i=1,2
. So
d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 2(R+2\delta_{0})
and
d_{B}(a_{1},a_{2})\leq 2(R+2\delta_{0})
, where
\pi_{X}(x_{i})=a_{i},i=1,2
. Let
v
be the center of the tripod with vertices
\pi(x_{1}),\pi(x_{2}),u
in
T
. If any one of
\pi(x_{1})
,
\pi(x_{2})
is
u
then we set
v
to be
u
. Let
c\in B_{v}\cap[a_{1},a_{2}]
. Then
d_{B}(a_{i},c)\leq 2(R+2\delta_{0}),i=1,2
. Suppose
\gamma_{i}
is
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}
-lift through
x_{i}
of geodesic
[a_{i},c]
lying inside
\mathcal{L}^{i}
. Let
\gamma_{i}(a_{i})=c_{i},i=1,2
. So
d_{L^{i}}(x_{i},c_{i})\leq 4(R+2\delta_{0})k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},i=1,2
. Thus
d_{X}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq d_{X}(c_{1},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})+d_{X}(x_{2},c_{2})\leq 2(R+2\delta_{0})(4k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+1)
, and so
d^{f}(c_{1},c_{2})\leq\phi(2(R+2\delta_{0})(4k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+1))=D_{1}
(say). We also note that
v\in\mathfrak{B}
where
\mathfrak{B}
is a central base for all
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s. Since
z_{c,v}\in\Xi
is
\delta_{0}
-center of geodesic triangle with vertices
\{bot(\mathcal{L}^{j}_{c,v});j=1,2,3\}
in
F_{c,v}
, so
z_{c,v}
is
3\delta_{0}
-close (in fiber distance) to a point
c_{3}\in[c_{1},c_{2}]^{f}
. Then, for
i=1,2
,
d_{L^{i}}(c_{i},z_{c,v})\leq d^{f}(c_{i},z_{c,v})\leq d^{f}(c_{i},c_{3})+d^{f}(c_{3},z_{c,v})\leq D_{1}+3\delta_{0}
. Hence, for
i=1,2
,
d_{L^{i}}(x,z_{c,v})\leq d_{L^{i}}(x,x_{i})+d_{L^{i}}(x_{i},c_{i})+d_{L^{i}}(c_{i},z_{c,v})\leq R+2\delta_{0}+4(R+2\delta_{0})k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+D_{1}+3\delta_{0}=:D
. Therefore,
L^{1}\cap L^{2}\subseteq N^{i}_{D}(\Xi)\subseteq N^{i}_{D}(N_{2k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}}(\Xi))
, where
N^{i}_{D}(\zeta)
denotes
D
-neighborhood around
\zeta\in L^{i}
in the path metric of
L^{i}
,
i=1,2
.

Therefore, by Remark
2.12
, we conclude that
L^{1}\cup L^{2}
is
\delta_{2}
-hyperbolic, where
\delta_{2}=\delta_{\ref{combi-hyp-sps-2}}(\delta_{1},L_{1})
.

The exact proof works mutatis mutandis for
(L^{1}\cup L^{2})\cup L^{3}
, and we conclude that Y is uniformly hyperbolic with the induced path metric. We take that hyperbolic constant to be
\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}
.

(2)
Note that
L^{ij}=N_{R}(\mathcal{L}^{ij})\subseteq Y\subseteq U_{K(R+8\delta_{0})}
as
\mathcal{L}^{ij}\subseteq N^{f}_{\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}\cup\mathcal{L}^{j})
. Since
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
is a
(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-ladder (see Proposition
8.1
), then by Corollary
6.5
, the inclusion
L^{ij}\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},R)
-qi embedding and so is the inclusion
L^{ij}\hookrightarrow Y
. Therefore, we can take
L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}=L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},R)
.
∎

Given a pair of points in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
, we get a ladder according to Corollary
8.8
. But this ladder is far from being canonical. In the following proposition, we show that different ladders for different choices of qi sections for the same pair of points give rise to uniform Hausdorff-close geodesic paths joining those points in the respective ladders. In fact, this is more generally true, but we prove it according to our requirements. For the proposition below,
r_{2}
is defined in Lemma
8.14
.

Proposition 8.10
.

There is a constant
D_{\ref{paths-are-Hd-close}}=D_{\ref{paths-are-Hd-close}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
such that the following holds.

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Suppose
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
are
K
-qi sections through
x
and
y
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}
over
B_{[u,\pi(x)]}
and
B_{[u,\pi(y)]}
respectively. Let
\mathcal{L}^{1}_{xy}
and
\mathcal{L}^{2}_{xy}
be two
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladders containing
\Sigma_{x},\Sigma_{y}
(see Corollary
8.8
). Further we assume that
c^{1}(x,y)
and
c^{2}(x,y)
are geodesics joining
x,y
inside
L^{1}_{xy}:=N_{r_{2}}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{xy})
and
L^{2}_{xy}:=N_{r_{2}}(\mathcal{L}^{2}{xy})
respectively. Then
c^{1}(x,y)
and
c^{2}(x,y)
are
D_{\ref{paths-are-Hd-close}}
-Hausdorff-close in
X
.

Proof.

In this proposition, we omit the subscript
xy
when denoting the ladders to avoid excessive notation. We denote
\mathcal{L}^{i}:=\mathcal{L}^{i}_{xy}
,
L^{i}:=N_{r_{2}}(\mathcal{L}^{i})
and the fibers of
\mathcal{L}^{i}
by
\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,v}\subseteq F_{b,v},i=1,2
. Let
\pi_{X}(\mathcal{L}^{i})=B_{i},~\pi(\mathcal{L}^{i})=S_{i},i=1,2
. Note that both the ladders
\mathcal{L}^{1},~\mathcal{L}^{2}
contain
\Sigma_{x}
and
\Sigma_{y}
, so for
v\in\pi(\Sigma_{x})\cup\pi(\Sigma_{y})\textrm{ and }b\in B_{v}
,
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v})\leq\delta_{0}
; in particular, the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v})
is
5\delta_{0}
-close in
F_{b,v}
.

The proof is divided into two steps. In Step
1
, we find a common base
\bar{B}
and develop lemmas which are needed in Step
2
. In Step
2
, we show that there is a common subspace containing
x,y
which is qi embedded in both
L^{1},L^{2}
. Finally, we conclude the proposition.

Step 1
:
Construction of common base
\bm{\bar{B}}
: Let
B^{\prime}=\{b\in B_{v}:d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v})\leq 5\delta_{0},v\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}\}
. Note that
B_{u}\subseteq\pi_{X}(\Sigma_{x})\cup\pi_{X}(\Sigma_{y})\subseteq B^{\prime}
. Let
B^{\prime}_{v}=\textrm{hull}(B^{\prime})\cap B_{v}
and
\bar{B}_{v}=N_{\delta_{0}}(B^{\prime}_{v})\cap B_{v}
, where
v\in\pi_{B}(B^{\prime})
. Suppose
\bar{B}_{1}=\cup_{v\in\pi_{B}(B^{\prime})}\bar{B}_{v}
and so
\pi_{B}(\bar{B}_{1})
is a subtree of
S_{1}\cap S_{2}
. Then by
[
KS22
, Lemma
1.93
]
and the fact that
B_{v}
’s are isometrically embedded in
B
, we note that
\bar{B}_{1}
is
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedded in
B
. Finally, we will add a few more vertices and edges to
\bar{B}_{1}
to complete the construction of
\bar{B}
. Suppose
v\in\pi_{B}(\bar{B}_{1}),w\notin\pi_{B}(\bar{B}_{1})
and
w\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
such that
d_{T}(v,w)=1
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Further, we assume that
\mathfrak{v}\in\bar{B}_{v}
and the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
is
5\delta_{0}
-close in the fiber
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Then we include only the vertex
\mathfrak{w}
and the edge
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
to
\bar{B}_{1}
. And, we will use the same notation for these extra vertices, i.e., here
\bar{B}_{w}=\bar{B}\cap B_{w}=\{\mathfrak{w}\}
. Notice that
\bar{B}
is still
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedded in
B
. Let
\bar{S}=\pi_{B}(\bar{B})
.

Let
v\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
and
b\in B_{v}
. Let
P^{i}_{b,v}:F_{b,v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,v}
be a nearest point projection map in
F_{b,v}
(see Lemma
2.20

(1)
) and
\bar{P}^{i}_{b,v}:F_{b,v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{i}_{b,v}
is modified projection (see Definition
2.24
) corresponding to
P^{i}_{b,v}
,
i=1,2
. We denote
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v}:=\bar{P}^{1}_{b,v}(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v})\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v}
and
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v}:=\bar{P}^{2}_{b,v}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v})\subseteq\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v}
. We take
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}:=\bigcup\limits_{b\in\bar{B}_{v},~v\in\bar{S}}\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{b,v},i=1,2
.

Note 8.11
.

(i)
Let
v\in\pi_{B}(B^{\prime})
and
b\in B^{\prime}\cap B_{v}
. Then by Remark
2.17

(2)
,
Hd^{f}(P^{1}_{b,v}(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,v}),P^{2}_{b,v}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,v}))\leq 2\delta_{0}+5\delta_{0}=7\delta_{0}
. So
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v})\leq 13\delta_{0}
by Remark
2.26
. However, we will prove below in Lemma
8.12
that
\forall~v\in\bar{S}\textrm{ and }\forall~b\in\bar{B}_{v}
,
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v})
is uniformly bounded.

(ii)
If
v\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
, then by Lemma
5.16
,
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}\cap X_{v}:=\bigcup_{b\in B_{v}}\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{b,v}
form a (uniformly) special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K))
-ladder bounded by
K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)
-qi sections,
i=1,2
.

Lemma 8.12
.

With the above notations, we have
R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
such that
\forall~v\in\bar{S}\textrm{ and }\forall~b\in\bar{B}_{v}
,

Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v})\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}.

Proof.

Let
v\in\bar{S},~c\in\bar{B}_{v}
. We divide the proof into following cases. First three cases deal with the vertices
v\in\pi_{B}(\bar{B}_{1})=\pi_{B}(B^{\prime})
and Case
(4)
with the extra vertices.

Case 1:
Suppose
c\in\textrm{hull}(B^{\prime})
such that
c\in[a,b]_{B}
for some
a,b\in B^{\prime}
and
a\in B_{u}
. Let
w=\pi_{B}(b),v=\pi_{B}(c)
. We prove that
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c,v})
is uniformly bounded and hence we are through by Remark
2.17

(2)
and Remark
2.26
. As the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,w},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,w})
is
5\delta_{0}
-close, we take
x\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,w}
such that
d^{f}(x,\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,w})\leq 5\delta_{0}
. Consider
k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
-qi section, say,
\gamma_{x}
through
x
over
B_{[u,w]}
in the ladder
\mathcal{L}^{1}
(since
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s are
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladders). Now we apply Mitra’s retraction (see Theorem
6.3
),
\rho_{\mathcal{L}^{2}}
on
\gamma_{x}
and get a
\mathcal{R}_{0}(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+1)
-qi section, say,
\gamma^{\prime}_{x}
over
B_{[u,w]}
in
\mathcal{L}^{2}
, where
\mathcal{R}_{0}:=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-semicts-subsp}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
. Then we have two
\mathcal{R}_{0}(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+1)
-qi sections
\gamma_{x},\gamma^{\prime}_{x}
over
B_{[u,w]}
such that (as
a\in B_{u}
)
d^{f}(\gamma_{x}(s),\gamma^{\prime}_{x}(s))\leq 5\delta_{0},~s\in\{a,b\}
. Again the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
satisfies
\mathcal{R}_{0}(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+1)
-flaring condition. Thus the restriction of
\gamma_{x},\gamma^{\prime}_{x}
on geodesic
[a,b]
and Lemma
5.7

(2)
imply
d^{f}(\gamma_{x}(c),\gamma^{\prime}_{x}(c))\leq R_{1}
, where
R_{1}=R_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(L_{0}(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+1),5\delta_{0})
. Hence
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c,v})\leq R_{1}
and so by Remark
2.17

(2)
and Remark
2.26
,
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{c,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{c,v})\leq 8\delta_{0}+R_{1}
.

Case 2:
Suppose
c\in\textrm{hull}(B^{\prime})
such that
c\in[a,b]_{B}
for some
a,b\in B^{\prime}
and none of
a,b
belong to
B_{u}
. Let
v=\pi_{B}(c)
. More precisely, we assume that
v
is the center of the geodesic triangle
\triangle(\pi_{B}(a),u,\pi_{B}(b))
, otherwise, it will land in Case
1
. Let
a^{\prime}\in B_{u}
. Then by
\delta_{0}
-slimness of the geodesic triangle
\triangle(a,b,a^{\prime})
and without loss of generality, we may assume that
d_{B}(c,c^{\prime})\leq\delta_{0}
for some
c^{\prime}\in[a^{\prime},b]
. So by Case
1
, we have
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c^{\prime},v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c^{\prime},v})\leq R_{1}
. Let
x\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c^{\prime},v},y\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c^{\prime},v}
such that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq R_{1}
. Now we take
k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
-qi lifts, say,
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
of geodesic
[c,c^{\prime}]
in
\mathcal{L}^{1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{2}
through
x
and
y
respectively. Then
d_{X}(\gamma_{1}(c),\gamma_{2}(c))\leq d_{X}(\gamma_{1}(c),x)+d_{X}(x,y)+d_{X}(y,\gamma_{2}(c))\leq 2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1}+2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}=4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1}
. Thus
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c,v})\leq\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1})
, where fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded in
X
. Therefore, by Remark
2.17

(2)
and Remark
2.26
,
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{c,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{c,v})\leq 8\delta_{0}+\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1})
.

Case 3:
Suppose
c\in\bar{B}_{1}
and
\pi_{B}(c)=v
. Then by construction of
\bar{B}_{1}
, there exists
c_{1}\in B^{\prime}_{v}\subseteq\textrm{hull}(B^{\prime})
such that
d_{B}(c,c_{1})=d_{B_{v}}(c,c_{1})\leq\delta_{0}
. Since
c_{1}\in
hull
(B^{\prime})
, by Case 2, we know that
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c_{1},v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c_{1},v})\leq\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1})
. Now we use the same argument used in the last part of Case 2. Let
x\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c_{1},v},y\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c_{1},v}
such that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1})
. By taking
k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
-qi lifts through points
x
and
y
of the geodesic
[c,c_{1}]
in the ladders
\mathcal{L}^{1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{2}
respectively, one can conclude that
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{c,v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{c,v})\leq\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1}))
. Hence, by Remark
2.17

(2)
and Remark
2.26
,
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{c,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{c,v})\leq 8\delta_{0}+\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+\phi(4k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1}))
.

Case 4:
Here we will check for extra vertices if any. Suppose
c\in\bar{B}\setminus\bar{B}_{1}
. Let
\pi_{B}(c)=w
and
[v,w]
be the edge such that
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
. Let
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
be the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Note that
c=\mathfrak{w}
. Then by the construction of
\mathfrak{w}\in\bar{B},~d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 5\delta_{0}
and so
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq 13\delta_{0}
(see Remark
2.17

(2)
). Suppose
x\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and
y\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq 13\delta_{0}
. We consider
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w},~y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),x^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y),y^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
(since
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s are
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladders). Again
P_{\mathfrak{w}}
is
L^{\prime}_{1}
-coarsely Lipschitz retraction in the metric
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
(see Lemma
3.3
~(2)
) and so
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y))\leq L^{\prime}_{1}d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,y)+L^{\prime}_{1}\leq L^{\prime}_{1}(13\delta_{0}+1)
. Thus by triangle inequality,
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq 2\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(13\delta_{0}+1)

\Rightarrow d^{f}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\phi(2\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(13\delta_{0}+1))
. Now
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w},~y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
and so by Remark
2.17

(2)
and Remark
2.26
, we have,
d^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq 8\delta_{0}+\phi(2\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(13\delta_{0}+1))
.

Therefore, we can take
R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
to be the maximum of all four constants we get in four cases, i.e.,
R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}=max\{\phi(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+\phi(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}\delta_{0}+R_{1})),8\delta_{0}+\phi(2\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(13\delta_{0}+1))\}
.
∎

Next we show that
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}
’s are more general ladders. More precisely, they are semicontinuous families and they need not satisfy the condition
(5)
of Definition
6.1
trivially (i.e., in the notation of Definition
6.1
,
B^{\prime}\subsetneq\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{\mathfrak{Y}})
), and they behave like ladders.

Lemma 8.13
.

There are constants
k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},~c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}
such that the following hold.

Suppose
[v,w]
is an edge in
T
such that
d_{T}(u,v)<d_{T}(u,w)
and
[\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}]
is the edge joining
\mathfrak{v}\in B_{v}
and
\mathfrak{w}\in B_{w}
. Let
\mathfrak{v},\mathfrak{w}\in\bar{B}
. Then:

(1)

\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
.

(2)

Hd_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v}),\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}
.

(3)

Suppose
a\in\bar{B},~b\notin\bar{B}
with
d_{B}(a,b)=1
and
\pi_{B}(a)=s,~\pi_{B}(b)=t
. Then diam
{}^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s})\leq c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}
if
s=t
or the pair
(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}
-cobounded in the path metric of
F_{ab}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}([a,b])
if
s\neq t
.

In particular,
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}
is
(k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}})
-semicontinuous family,
i=1,2
.

Proof.

(1)
We will only prove for
i=1
as the proof for
i=2
involves a simple change of indices. Since
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s are
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladders, for
x\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
,
\exists~x_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,x_{1})\leq k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
. Let
y\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{w},w},~y_{1}\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
(by Lemma
8.12
) and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y,y_{1})\leq k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
. Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x_{1},x)+d^{f}(x,y)+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(y,y_{1})\leq 2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
. Hence
d^{f}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq R_{2}
, where
R_{2}=\phi(2k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}})
. Note that
P^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}:F_{\mathfrak{v},v}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
is a nearest point projection map in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. For simplicity, let
P=P^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
. Then
d^{f}(P(x_{1}),P(y_{1}))=d^{f}(x_{1},P(y_{1}))\leq C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},\delta_{0})(R_{2}+1)
(see Lemma
2.20

(1)
). So,
d^{f}(x_{1},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},\delta_{0})(R_{2}+1)
. Hence
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,x_{1})+d^{f}(x_{1},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\leq k_{1}
, where
k_{1}=k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},\delta_{0})(R_{2}+1)
. Therefore,
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{k_{1}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{\mathfrak{v},v})
. Thus
k_{1}
works for
(1)
but for the second part of this lemma, we have defined
k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}>k_{1}
in the end.

(2)
Here also we will only prove for
i=1
. Let
x\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
and we take
y\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d^{f}(x,y)\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
(by Lemma
8.12
). We take
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w},~y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),x^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
and
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y),y^{\prime})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
(since
\mathcal{L}^{i}
’s are
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladders). Again
P_{\mathfrak{w}}
is
L^{\prime}_{1}
-coarsely Lipschitz in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}
, so
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),P_{\mathfrak{w}}(y))\leq L^{\prime}_{1}d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,y)+L^{\prime}_{1}\leq L^{\prime}_{1}(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+1)
. Therefore, (by triangle inequality)
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq 2\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+1)

\Rightarrow d^{f}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\phi(2\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+L^{\prime}_{1}(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+1))=R_{3}
(say). Note that
P^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}:F_{\mathfrak{w},w}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
is a nearest point projection map in the metric of
F_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. For simplicity, let
P=P^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Since
d^{f}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq R_{3}
, then by Lemma
2.20

(1)
,
d^{f}(x^{\prime},P(y^{\prime}))=d^{f}(P(x^{\prime}),P(y^{\prime}))\leq C_{\ref{proj-on-qc}}(\delta_{0},\delta_{0})(R_{3}+1)=R_{4}
(say). So
d^{f}(x^{\prime},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq R_{4}
. Hence
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x),x^{\prime})+d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x^{\prime},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w})\leq\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}+R_{4}=\varepsilon_{1}
(say), i.e.,
P_{\mathfrak{w}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v})\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{\varepsilon_{1}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
.

For the other inclusion, let
x\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}
. Then by
(1)
,
\exists~x_{1}\in\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{v},v}
such that
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(x,x_{1})\leq k_{1}
. Then
d_{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}(P_{\mathfrak{w}}(x_{1}),x)\leq 2k_{1}
. Hence
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w}\subseteq N^{\mathfrak{v}\mathfrak{w}}_{2k_{1}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{\mathfrak{w},w})
.

Therefore, we can take
\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}:=max\{\varepsilon_{1},2k_{1}\}
.

(3)
Suppose
s=t
. Then
a,b\in B_{s}
. Since
b\notin\bar{B}
, so
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{b,s},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{b,s})>5\delta_{0}
. Then by Remark
2.17

(1)
, diam
{}^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{b,s})\leq 8\delta_{0}
for
i=1,2
. Let
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s}=[\eta^{i}_{a,s},\zeta^{i}_{a,s}]
for
i=1,2
. Since
d_{B}(a,b)=1
, by Note
8.11

(ii)
,
d_{X}(\eta^{i}_{a,s},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{b,s})\leq 2K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)
and
d_{X}(\zeta^{i}_{a,s},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{b,s})\leq 2K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)
for
i=1,2
. Then by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(\eta^{i}_{a,s},\zeta^{i}_{a,s})\leq 4K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)+8\delta_{0}
for
i=1,2
. Therefore, diam
{}^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s})\leq\phi(4K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)+8\delta_{0})
for
i=1,2
.

Now suppose
s\neq t
. Note that since
a\in\bar{B},~b\notin\bar{B}
, then
d_{T}(u,s)<d_{T}(u,t)
and
[a,b]
is the edge joining
a\in B_{s}
,
b\in B_{t}
. If
t\notin S_{1}\cup S_{2}
, then the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{i}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
. So by Lemma
2.18
, there is a constant
C_{1}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
and
c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
such that the pair
(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
C_{1}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
.

Now let
t\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
. Since
b\notin\bar{B}
, then by the construction of
\bar{B}
,
d^{f}(\mathcal{L}^{1}_{a,s},\mathcal{L}^{2}_{a,s})>5\delta_{0}
. Thus by Remark
2.17

(1)
, diam
{}^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s})\leq 8\delta_{0}
for
i=1,2
. Therefore, by Lemma
2.18
, there is a constant
C_{2}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0},\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
and
8\delta_{0}
such that the pair
(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
C_{2}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
for
i=1,2
.

Finally, we assume that
t
belong to only one of the
S_{1}
,
S_{2}
. Without of loss of generality, let
t\in S_{1}
but
t\notin S_{2}
. Note that
s\in S_{2}
. Then the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{2}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
. So by Lemma
2.18
, the pair
(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
C_{1}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
(where
C_{1}
is defined above). Again,
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{a,s},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{a,s})\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
and a nearest point projection map
P:F_{ab}\rightarrow F_{b,t}
is
L^{\prime}_{1}
-coarsely Lipschitz (see Lemma
3.3
~(2)
) together imply that the diameter (in the metric of
F_{ab}
) of
\{P(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{a,s})\}
is bounded by
2L^{\prime}_{1}(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+1)+C_{1}=D
(say). Then by Lemma
2.18
, there is a constant
C_{3}
depending on
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
,
\lambda^{\prime}_{0}
and
D
such that the pair
(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{a,s},F_{b,t})
is
C_{3}
-cobounded in
F_{ab}
.

Therefore, we can take
c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}=max\{\phi(4K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K)+8\delta_{0}),C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}\}
.

For second part, we note that
\bar{B}
is
(1,6\delta_{0})
-qi embedded in
B
. Now by Note
8.11

(ii)
,
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}\cap X_{v}
is special
C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K))
-ladder in
X_{v},v\in S_{1}\cap S_{2}
. Therefore, by Lemma
6.21
,
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}
is
(k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}})
-ladder, where
k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}}=k_{\ref{promoting-of-ladder}}(k^{\prime})>k_{1}
and
k^{\prime}=max\{k_{1},C_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(K_{\ref{modified-projection-gives-qi-sections}}(K))\}
, and
i=1,2
.
∎

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
r_{1}=max\{2k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},2\delta_{0}+1\},r_{2}=max\{2C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}),R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+r_{1}+\delta_{0}\}
and
\bar{L}^{i}:=N_{r_{1}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}),~L^{i}:=N_{r_{2}}(\mathcal{L}^{i}),~i=1,2
. Then there is a constant
L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
such that the inclusion
\bar{L}^{i}\hookrightarrow L^{i}
is
L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
-qi embedding.

Proof.

Note that
\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i}
is
(k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},c_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}})
-semicontinuous family (see Lemma
8.13
) and
\bar{L}^{i}\subseteq L^{i}
. Then by Corollary
6.5
, the inclusion
\bar{L}^{i}\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},r_{1})
-qi embedding and so is the inclusion
\bar{L}^{i}\hookrightarrow L^{i},i=1,2
. Therefore, we can take
L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}=L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{Li-bar-behaves-like-ladder}},r_{1})
.
∎

Step 2
:
We fix
\bm{r_{1}}
and
\bm{r_{2}}
as in Lemma
8.14
for the rest of the proof
. Here we construct a common qi embedded subspace of
L^{1}
and
L^{2}
containing both
x
and
y
and which will show that
c^{1}(x,y)
and
c^{2}(x,y)
are uniformly Hausdorff-close.

Construction of common qi embedded subspace of
\bm{L^{1}}
and
\bm{L^{2}}
: Let
v\in\bar{S}\textrm{ and }b\in\bar{B}_{v}
. Suppose
\mathcal{Z}_{b,v}:=\textrm{hull}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v}\cup\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v})\subseteq F_{b,v},~\mathcal{Z}:=\bigcup\mathcal{Z}_{b,v}
and
Z:=N_{r_{1}}(\mathcal{Z})
, where the quasiconvex hull and its neighborhood is taken in the corresponding fiber. We also have
Hd^{f}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{1}_{b,v},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{2}_{b,v})\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}
(by Lemma
8.12
). Suppose
L^{i}
and
\bar{L}^{i}
are as in Lemma
8.14
. Then
\bar{L}^{i}\subseteq Z\subseteq N_{r_{1}+R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}}(\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{i})=N_{R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}}(\bar{L}^{i})
in the metric
L^{i}
and so
Hd_{L^{i}}(\bar{L}^{i},Z)\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}
for
i=1,2
. The subspace
Z
is our required common subspace and in the below lemma we will see that it is uniformly qi embedded in
L^{i}
for
i=1,2
.

Lemma 8.15
.

With the above notations, there is a (uniform) constant
L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
such that the inclusion
Z\hookrightarrow L^{i}
is
L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
-qi embedding in the path metric of
L^{i},~i=1,2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.14
, the inclusion
\bar{L}^{i}\hookrightarrow L^{i}
is
L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
-qi embedding. Since
Hd_{L^{i}}(\bar{L}^{i},Z)\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}
, with the reference to
[
KS22
, Lemma
1.19
]
, our task is to show that
Z
is uniformly properly embedded in
L^{i}
.

Z
is properly embedded in
L^{i}
: Let
x,y\in Z
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
d_{L^{i}}(x,y)\leq n
. Then
\exists~x_{1},y_{1}\in\bar{L}^{i}
such that
d_{Z}(x,x_{1})\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0},~d_{Z}(y,y_{1})\leq R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}
. So,
d_{L^{i}}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq 2(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0})+n
and by Lemma
8.14
,
d_{Z}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq d_{\bar{L}^{i}}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq(2(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0})+n)L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}+L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}^{2}=D(n)
(say). Thus,

\displaystyle d_{Z}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{Z}(x,x_{1})+d_{Z}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{Z}(y_{1},y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}+D(n)+R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0})+D(n)=:g(n)\textrm{ (say) }

So
Z
is
g
-properly embedded in
L^{i}
for the function
g:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
defined above. Therefore, for
i=1,2
, the inclusion
Z\hookrightarrow L^{i}
is
L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
-qi embedding, where
L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}}
depends on
L_{\ref{Li-bar-is-qi-emb-in-Li}},~R_{\ref{Li's-bar-are-Hd-close}}+\delta_{0}
and
g
(see
[
KS22
, Lemma
1.19
]
).
∎

Conclusion
: Let
c^{*}(x,y),~c^{1}(x,y)
and
c^{2}(x,y)
be geodesic paths in
Z,~L^{1}
and
L^{2}
respectively joining
x,y
. Since
(X,B,T)
satisfies
C^{(9)}_{\ref{qi-sec-inside-lad-len-lift}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-flaring condition. So by Theorem
7.11
,
L^{i}
is
\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},r_{2})
-hyperbolic,
i=1,2
. Therefore, by stability of quasi-geodesic (Lemma
2.5
),
Hd_{X}(c^{1}(x,y),c^{2}(x,y))\leq D_{\ref{paths-are-Hd-close}}:=2D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{\ref{general-ladder-is-hyp}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},r_{2}),L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}},L_{\ref{Y-is-qi-emb-in-Li}})
.
∎

Now, we are at a stage to show the uniform hyperbolicity of a uniform neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
with the induced path metric.

Theorem 8.16
.

Suppose
r_{2}
as in Lemma
8.14
. Then for any
R\geq r_{2}+8\delta_{0}
there is
\delta_{\ref{flow-sp-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{flow-sp-is-hyp}}(K,R)
such that
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})

:=N_{R}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}))
is
\delta_{\ref{flow-sp-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic metric space.

Proof.

We show that
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})
satisfies all the conditions of Proposition
2.10
. Note that
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
is
R
-dense in
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})
. For a point
x\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
, we fix once and for all a
K
-qi section
\Sigma_{x}
through
x
over
B_{x}:=B_{[u,\Pi(x)]}
lying inside
\mathcal{U}_{K}
. Now given a pair
(x^{1},x^{2})
of distinct points in
\mathcal{U}_{K}
, by Corollary
8.8
, there is a
(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
-ladder, say,
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{12}
containing
\Sigma_{x^{1}},~\Sigma_{x^{2}}
such that
top(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{12})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K},~bot(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{12})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{12}\subseteq N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
.

We take
\tilde{c}(x^{1},x^{2})
, a geodesic path joining
x^{1},x^{2}
in
\tilde{L}^{12}:=N_{r_{2}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{12})
. For a given pair of points, once and for all, we fix this ladder and the geodesic path. These paths serve as family of paths for Proposition
2.10
.

Let us start with three points
x^{i}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}),~i=1,2,3
and geodesic paths
\tilde{c}(x^{i},x^{j})
in the respective ladders
\tilde{L}^{ij}:=N_{r_{2}}(\tilde{\mathcal{L}}^{ij})
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
. Note that
\tilde{L}^{ij}\subseteq U_{KR}
.

Condition (1)
: As
\tilde{L}^{ij}
is
L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},r_{2})
-qi embedded in
X
and so is in
U_{KR}
. Then the path
\tilde{c}(x^{i},x^{j})
is
h
-properly embedded in
U_{KR}
, where
h:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
sending
r\mapsto rL_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},r_{2})+(L_{\ref{semicts-is-qi-emb}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},r_{2}))^{2}
.

Condition (2)
: By Proposition
8.1
, given any three points
x^{i},i=1,2,3
, we have,
(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}})
-ladders,
\mathcal{L}^{ij}
containing
\Sigma_{i},~\Sigma_{j}
such that
top(\mathcal{L}^{ij})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K},~bot(\mathcal{L}^{ij})\subseteq\mathcal{U}_{K}
and
\mathcal{L}^{ij}\subseteq N^{f}_{2\delta_{0}}(\mathcal{U}_{K})
. Let
c(x^{i},x^{j})
be a geodesic path joining
x^{i},x^{j}
in
L^{ij}:=N_{r_{2}}(\mathcal{L}^{ij})\subseteq U_{KR}
. Note that
k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},~c_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
and
\varepsilon_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}}=\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}}
(Lemma
8.8
). So by Proposition
8.10
,
Hd_{X}(\tilde{c}(x^{i},x^{j}),c(x^{i},x^{j}))\leq D
, where
D=D_{\ref{paths-are-Hd-close}}(k_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},c_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}},\varepsilon_{\ref{ladder-in-flow-sp-for-two-pts}})
. Thus by Proposition
6.11
, their Hausdorff distance is bounded by
\eta_{1}(D)
in the path metric of
U_{KR}
, where
\eta_{1}:=\eta_{\ref{fsandgss-are-proper-emb}}(K,R)
.

Now by Lemma
8.9
, there is a
\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},r_{2})
-hyperbolic subspace
Y(:=N_{r_{2}+2\delta_{0}}(\cup_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{L}^{i}))
such that the inclusion
i:L^{ij}\hookrightarrow Y
is
L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},r_{2})
-qi embedding. Also, note that
Y\subseteq U_{K(r_{2}+8\delta_{0})}\subseteq U_{KR}
. Let
\delta_{1}=\delta_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},r_{2})
and
L_{1}=L_{\ref{paths-are-slim}}(k_{\ref{existence-of-tripod-ladder}},r_{2})
. Then by Lemma
2.5
, the triangle formed by the paths
c(x^{i},x^{j})
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
, are
D_{1}
-slim in the path metric of
Y
and so is in the path metric of
U_{KR}
, where
D_{1}:=2D_{\ref{ml}}(\delta_{1},L_{1},L_{1})+\delta_{1}
.

Hence the triangle formed by the paths
\tilde{c}(x^{i},x^{j})
for all distinct
i,j\in\{1,2,3\}
, are
D_{2}
-slim in the path metric of
U_{KR}
, where
D_{2}:=2\eta_{1}(D)+D_{1}
.

Therefore, by Proposition
2.10
,
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})
is
\delta_{\ref{flow-sp-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic metric space with the induced path metric from
X
, where
\delta_{\ref{flow-sp-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{combing}}(h,D_{2},R)
, where
h
and
D_{2}
are defined above.
∎

9.
Hyperbolicity of
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))

Let
u,v\in T
, and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
are the flow spaces as described in the first paragraph of Section
8
. We also assume that
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\neq\emptyset
. In this section, we will prove that
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is uniformly hyperbolic. We use the notations
Fl_{KD}:=N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})),~Fl_{KD}:=N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
for
D\geq 0
. Here, we require the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
to satisfy
(2(L^{\prime})^{2}(2K+1)+L^{\prime})
-flaring condition where
L^{\prime}=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}(K)
.

So far we have the following
(\mathcal{H}0)-(\mathcal{H}6)
. We will use these properties in this section.

(\mathcal{H}0)
Suppose
w,w^{\prime}\in T
and
e
is the edge on
[w,w^{\prime}]
incident on
w^{\prime}
. Let
T^{\prime}
be the maximal subtree of
T
containing
w^{\prime}
not containing
e
. Then
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w})\cap X_{T^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w^{\prime}})\cap X_{T^{\prime}}
.

(\mathcal{H}1)
For all
w\in T
, we have
L^{\prime}:=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}(K)
-coarsely Lipschitz retraction
\rho_{w}:X\rightarrow\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w})
such that
\forall~x\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w})
,
\pi_{X}(\rho_{w}(x))=\pi_{X}(x)
(see Proposition
6.10
).

(\mathcal{H}2)
Let
w\in T
. For all
x\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w})
there is a
K
-qi section lying in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{w})\cap\pi^{-1}([w,\pi(x)])
through
x
over
B_{[w,\pi(x)]}
.

(\mathcal{H}3)
Let
w\in T
. For
L\geq 2K
, there is
\eta(L):\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the inclusion
Fl_{KL}(X_{w})\hookrightarrow X
is
\eta(L)
-proper embedding (see Proposition
6.11
).

(\mathcal{H}4)
Let
\mathcal{G}=\{\gamma:\gamma\textrm{ is a }(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})\textrm{-qi section }\textrm{ over }B_{[u,v]}\}
. Note that
\mathfrak{T}\neq\emptyset
as
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
. For
w\in[u,v]
,
b\in B_{w}
let
H_{b,w}=\textrm{hull}\{\gamma(b):\gamma\in\mathfrak{T}\}\subseteq F_{b,w}
and
H=\bigcup_{w\in[u,v],~b\in B_{w}}H_{b,w}
. (Here quasiconvex hull is considered in the corresponding fiber.) Then by Lemma
5.14
,
H
is
\kappa
-metric bundle over
B_{[u,v]}
where
\kappa=K_{\ref{getting-metric-graph-bundles}}(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})\geq 2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime}
. Now we consider flow of
H
with parameters
\kappa,\kappa
(see Definition
6.8
). According to our notation (see
6.14
), we have
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
and it also satisfies the following. Let
w\in T
and
T_{uvw}
be the tripod with vertices
u,v,w
. Since
\kappa\geq 2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime}
, by Lemma
6.13
, we have that for any
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section
\gamma
over
B_{T_{uvw}}
,
\gamma\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
.

By Notation
6.14
, we also have flow spaces
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(2)}}(X_{u})
containing
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
and
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(2)}}(X_{v})
containing
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
.

(\mathcal{H}5)
Let
R_{0}
be large enough so that
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
are
\delta
-hyperbolic for some
\delta\geq 0
(see Theorem
8.16
).

(\mathcal{H}6)
Since flow spaces are semicontinuous family, for all
L\geq max\{2\kappa^{(1)},2\delta_{0}+1\}
there is
\bar{L}(L)
such that the inclusions
Fl_{KL}(X_{u})\rightarrow X
,
Fl_{KL}(X_{v})\rightarrow X
and
Fl_{\kappa^{(1)}L}(H)\newline
\rightarrow X
are
\bar{L}(L)
-qi embedding (see Proposition
6.5
).

We know that uniform neighborhood of flow spaces are uniformly properly embedded in the total space (see
(\mathcal{H}3)
). In the following proposition, we prove the same for the union of two intersecting flow spaces.

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}=2(L^{\prime})^{2}(2K+1)+L^{\prime}
. For all
L\geq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}(\geq 2K)
there exists
\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}=\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}(K,L):\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the inclusion
N_{L}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\rightarrow X
is
\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-proper embedding.

Proof.

Our proof goes in the same methodology as in the book
[
KS22
]
for trees of metric spaces (see
[
KS22
, Subsection
6.1.1
]
). We denote the induced path metric on
Fl_{KL}(X_{u})\cup F_{KL}(X_{v})
by
d^{\prime}
. We divide the proof by reducing the tree
T
to intervals and the general tree in the following three cases. We first prove in all the cases that for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
x,y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
with
d_{X}(x,y)=r
,
d^{\prime}(x,y)
is bounded in terms of
r
. In the end, we prove for the points in
Fl_{KL}(X_{u})\cup F_{KL}(X_{v})
.

Let
x,y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
such that
d_{X}(x,y)=r
. Suppose
\pi(x)=u^{\prime},\pi(y)=v^{\prime},\pi_{X}(x)=\bar{x},~\pi_{X}(y)=\bar{y}
. As
L\geq 2K
, we may assume that
x\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\setminus\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
and
y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\setminus\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
, otherwise, by
(\mathcal{H}3)
,
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq\eta(L)(r)
.

Case 1
: We first assume that
T=[u,v]
. Then
u^{\prime}\neq v
, otherwise,
x\in X_{v}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
. Also
v^{\prime}\neq u
, otherwise,
y\in X_{u}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Depending on positions of
u^{\prime},v^{\prime},x
and
y
, we consider following subcases.

Subcase
(1A): Suppose
u^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
and
x=\rho_{u}(y)
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
). Consider a
K
-qi section,
\gamma_{y}
over
B_{[v^{\prime},v]}
through
y
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Since
\rho_{u}
is
L^{\prime}
-coarsely Lipschitz retraction (see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
, so applying
\rho_{u}
on
\gamma_{y}
, we get a
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section, say,
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
over
B_{[v^{\prime},v]}
such that
x=\rho_{u}(y)=\bar{\gamma}_{y}(\bar{y})
. Let
b
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
B_{v}
(note that such
b
exists as
v^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\neq v
). Applying
\rho_{v}
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) on
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and we get a
2.(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})L^{\prime}+L^{\prime}=k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-qi section, say,
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
over
B_{[v^{\prime},v]}
. Note that
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)=\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(b)
(as
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)\in X_{v}
). Let
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(\bar{x})=y^{\prime}
. Then
\rho_{v}(x)=y^{\prime}
and since
\rho_{v}(y)=y
,
d_{X}(y^{\prime},y)=d_{X}(\rho_{v}(x),\rho_{v}(y))\leq L^{\prime}d_{X}(x,y)+L^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime}(r+1)
. So
d_{X}(x,y^{\prime})\leq d_{X}(x,y)+d_{X}(y,y^{\prime})\leq r(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime}
and
d^{f}(x,y^{\prime})\leq\phi(r(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime})
, where the fibers are
\phi
-properly embedded in total space. Here we have two
k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-qi sections
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
over
B_{[v^{\prime},v]}
such that
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)=\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(b)
and
d^{f}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(\bar{x}),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(\bar{x}))=d^{f}(x,y^{\prime})\leq\phi(r(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime})
. Let
a
be the point on
[\bar{x},b]_{B}
closest to
\bar{x}
such that
d^{f}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))\leq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
. Since
L\geq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
,
d^{\prime}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))\leq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
. Again, the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
satisfies flaring condition, so by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(\bar{x},a)\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}},C)
, where
C=max\{M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}},\phi(r(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime})\}
. Let
C_{1}=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}},C)
. Then by taking lifts of geodesic
[\bar{x},a]_{B}
in
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
), we get,
d^{\prime}(x,\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a))\leq 2k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}C_{1}
and
d^{\prime}(y,\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))\leq 2k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}C_{1}
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a))+d^{\prime}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))+d^{\prime}(\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a),y)\leq 4k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}C_{1}+M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}=:\eta_{1}(r)
for some
\eta_{1}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Subcase
(1B): Let
y^{\prime}=\rho_{u}(y)
. In this subcase,
y^{\prime}
need not be equal to
x
. Since
\rho_{u}(x)=x
,
d_{X}(x,y^{\prime})=d_{X}(\rho_{u}(x),\rho_{u}(y))\leq L^{\prime}r+L^{\prime}=L^{\prime}(r+1)
. So
d_{X}(y^{\prime},y)\leq d_{X}(y^{\prime},x)+d_{X}(x,y)\leq L^{\prime}(r+1)+r
. Since
L\geq 2K
,
Fl_{KL}(X_{u})
is
\eta(L)
-properly embedded in
X
(see
(\mathcal{H}3)
). So
d^{\prime}(x,y^{\prime})\leq\eta(L)(L^{\prime}(r+1))
.
Note that
\pi_{X}(y^{\prime})=\pi_{X}(y)
(as
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
) and
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Then by
Subcase

(1A)
,
d^{\prime}(y,y^{\prime})\leq\eta_{1}(L^{\prime}(r+1)+r)
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq\eta(L)(L^{\prime}(r+1))+\eta_{1}(L^{\prime}(r+1)+r)
.

We assume
\bm{\zeta_{1}(r)}:=max\{\eta_{1}(r),\eta(L)(L^{\prime}(r+1))+\eta_{1}(L^{\prime}(r+1)+r)\}
, maximum distortion in this Case
1
for some
\zeta_{1}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Case 2
: We now assume that
T=[w,w^{\prime}]\supsetneq[u,v]
such that
u
is closest to
w
. Then
v^{\prime}\notin[w,u]
, otherwise, by
(\mathcal{H}0)
,
y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Also,
u^{\prime}\notin[v,w^{\prime}]
, otherwise, by
(\mathcal{H}0)
,
x\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
. We consider the following subcases depending on the position of
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
.

Let
S=[u,v]
and
X_{S}:=\pi^{-1}(S)
. Let
d^{\prime\prime}
denote the induced path metric on
L
-neighborhood (in
X_{S}
-metric) of
(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\cap X_{S}
inside
X_{S}
. We note that the restriction
\pi_{X}|_{X_{S}}:X_{S}\rightarrow B_{S}
also satisfies flaring condition (Remark
5.8

(b)
).

Subcase
(2A): Suppose
u^{\prime}\in[w,u]
and
v^{\prime}\in[v,w^{\prime}]
. Let
b^{\prime}
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
B_{u}
and
b^{\prime\prime}
be that of
\bar{y}
on
B_{v}
. Then
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
implies
d_{B}(\bar{x},b^{\prime})\leq r
,
d_{B}(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\leq r
and
d_{B}(b^{\prime\prime},\bar{y})\leq r
. Let
\gamma_{x}
be
K
-qi lift through
x
of geodesic
[\bar{x},b^{\prime}]_{B}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\gamma_{y}
be that through
y
of
[b^{\prime\prime},\bar{y}]_{B}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Let
\gamma_{x}(b^{\prime})=x^{\prime}
and
\gamma_{y}(b^{\prime\prime})=y^{\prime}
. Then
d^{\prime}(x,x^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
and
d^{\prime}(y,y^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). So by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq 4Kr+r
, and that implies
d_{X_{S}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r)
(see Proposition
5.3
). Then by Case
1
,
d^{\prime\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))
. Therefore,
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,x^{\prime})+d^{\prime\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq 4Kr+\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))
.

Subcase
(2B): Suppose
u^{\prime}\in[u,v]\setminus\{u,v\}
and
v^{\prime}\in[v,w^{\prime}]
. Let
b^{\prime\prime}
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{y}
on
B_{v}
. Then
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
implies
d_{B}(\bar{y},b^{\prime\prime})\leq r
(as
u^{\prime}\in[u,v]\setminus\{u,v\})
. Let
\gamma_{y}
be a
K
-qi lift of the geodesic
[b^{\prime\prime},\bar{y}]_{B}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
through
y
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
) and let
\gamma_{y}(b^{\prime\prime})=y^{\prime}
. Then
d^{\prime}(y,y^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). Again,
d_{X}(y^{\prime},x)\leq d_{X}(y^{\prime},y)+d_{X}(y,x)\leq 2Kr+r
. So by Proposition
5.3
,
d_{X_{S}}(y^{\prime},x)\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r)
. Hence by
Subcase

(1A)
,
d^{\prime\prime}(x,y^{\prime})\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))
. So
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq d^{\prime\prime}(x,y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))+2Kr
.

Subcase
(2C): Suppose
u^{\prime}\in[w,u]
and
v^{\prime}\in[u,v]\setminus\{u,v\}
. Then this is a symmetry of
Subcase

(2B)
. So
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))+2Kr
.

Subcase
(2D): Finally, we assume that
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\in[u,v]\setminus\{u,v\}
. Then by Proposition
5.3
,
d_{X_{S}}(x,y)\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r)
. So by Case
1
,
d^{\prime\prime}(x,y)\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r))
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime\prime}(x,y)\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r))
.

We assume
\bm{\zeta_{2}(r)}:=max\{4Kr+\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r)),\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))+2Kr,\newline
\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r))\}
, maximum distortion in this Case
2
for some
\zeta_{2}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Case 3
: Here we consider the general case, where
T
is any tree. Depending on the position of
u,v,u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
, we consider the following subcases.

Let
S
be an interval in
T
containing
u,v
and
X_{S}:=\pi^{-1}(X_{S})
. We denote the induced path metric on
L
-neighborhood (in
X_{S}
-metric) of
(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\cap X_{S}
inside
X_{S}
by
d^{\prime\prime}
. We will use this notation below. We note that the restriction
\pi_{X}|_{X_{S}}:X_{S}\rightarrow B_{S}
also satisfies flaring condition (see Remark
5.8

(b)
).

Subcase
(3A): Suppose
u,v,u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
lie on an interval in
T
. We fix one such interval
S
in
T
containing
u,v,u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
. So
d_{X_{S}}(x,y)\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r)
(by Proposition
5.3
). Now we restrict the flow spaces to
X_{S}
. Then by Case
2
,
d^{\prime\prime}(x,y)\leq\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r))
. So
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime\prime}(x,y)\leq\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r))
.

Now we consider the subcases when all of
u,v,u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
do not lie on an interval.

Subcase
(3B): Suppose there is no interval containing
u,v
that contains both
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
; but there is an interval containing
u,v
which contains one of
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
. We give a proof when an interval containing
u,v
also contains
u^{\prime}
, and leave the other case because its involves only a change in indices. We fix one such interval
S
in
T
containing
u,v
and
u^{\prime}
. Let
t
be the nearest point projection of
v^{\prime}
on
S
in
d_{T}
-metric and
b^{\prime\prime}
be that of
\bar{y}
on
B_{t}
in
d_{B}
-metric. Since
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, then
d_{B}(\bar{y},b^{\prime\prime})\leq d_{B}(\bar{y},\bar{x})\leq r
. Let
\gamma_{y}
be a
K
-qi lift of the geodesic
[b^{\prime\prime},\bar{y}]_{B}
through
y
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Suppose
\gamma_{y}(b^{\prime\prime})=y^{\prime}
. Then
d^{\prime}(y,y^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). Again
d_{X}(y^{\prime},x)\leq d_{X}(y^{\prime},y)+d_{X}(y,x)\leq 2Kr+r
, and so by Proposition
5.3
,
d_{X_{S}}(y^{\prime},x)\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r)
. Now we restrict the flow spaces to
X_{S}
. Hence by Case
2
,
d^{\prime\prime}(y^{\prime},x)\leq\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))
. Therefore,
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq d^{\prime\prime}(x,y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))+2Kr
.

Subcase
(3C): Suppose there is no interval containing
u,v
that contains either of
u^{\prime},v^{\prime}
. We fix
S=[u,v]
. Let
t_{1}
and
t_{2}
be the nearest point projections of
u^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
on
S
respectively. Then
t_{1},t_{2}\in[u,v]\setminus\{u,v\}
, otherwise, it will land in Subcase
(3B)
. We divide the proof into two cases depending on whether
t_{1},t_{2}
are same or not.

(a)
Suppose
t_{1}\neq t_{2}
. Let
b^{\prime}
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}
on
B_{t_{1}}
and
b^{\prime\prime}
be that of
\bar{y}
on
B_{t_{2}}
. Since
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, then
d_{B}(\bar{x},b^{\prime})\leq r
and
d_{B}(\bar{y},b^{\prime\prime})\leq r
. Let
\gamma_{x}
be a
K
-qi lift of the geodesic
[\bar{x},b^{\prime}]_{B}
through
x
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\gamma_{y}
be that of
[\bar{y},b^{\prime\prime}]_{B}
through
y
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Let
x^{\prime}=\gamma_{x}(b^{\prime})
and
y^{\prime}=\gamma_{y}(b^{\prime\prime})
. Then
d^{\prime}(x,x^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
and
d^{\prime}(y,y^{\prime})\leq 2Kr
. So
d_{X}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq d_{X}(x^{\prime},x)+d_{X}(x,y)+d_{X}(y,y^{\prime})\leq 4Kr+r
. Then by Proposition
5.3
,
d_{X_{S}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r)
. Note that
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
. Now we restrict the flow spaces to
X_{S}=X_{[u,v]}
. Hence by Case
1
,
d^{\prime\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))
, and so
d^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))
. Therefore,
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,x^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})+d^{\prime}(y^{\prime},y)\leq 4Kr+\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))
.

(b)
Suppose
t_{1}=t_{2}=t
(say). Let
s
be the center of
\bigtriangleup(u^{\prime},t,v^{\prime})
and
c\in[\bar{x},\bar{y}]\cap B_{s}
. Since
d_{B}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
, so
d_{B}(\bar{x},c)\leq r
and
d_{B}(c,\bar{y})\leq r
. Let
\gamma_{1}
be a
K
-qi lift through
x
of the geodesic
[\bar{x},c]_{B}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\gamma_{2}
be that through
y
of the geodesic
[\bar{y},c]_{B}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Let
x_{1}=\gamma_{1}(c)
and
y_{1}=\gamma_{2}(c)
. Then
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq 2Kr
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq 2Kr
.

Now we only need to show that
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})
is bounded in terms of
r
. We will apply the same trick as in Case
1
. Let
\gamma_{y}
be a
K
-qi section over
B_{[s,v]}
through
y_{1}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Now we apply
\rho_{u}
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) on
\gamma_{y}
and get a
L^{\prime}(2K+1)
-qi section, say,
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
over
B_{[s,v]}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
(see Figure
7
). (This is possible as
[s,v]\subseteq\pi(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}))
.) By triangle inequality,
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq 4Kr+r
. Let
\rho_{u}(y_{1})=x_{2}
. Since
\rho_{u}(x_{1})=x_{1}
, then
d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})=d_{X}(\rho_{u}(x_{1}),\rho_{u}(y_{1}))\leq L^{\prime}d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})+L^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime}(4Kr+r+1)
. Then
d_{X}(y_{1},x_{2})\leq d_{X}(y_{1},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 4Kr+r+L^{\prime}(4Kr+r+1)=(4Kr+r)(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime}
. Again we apply
\rho_{v}
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) on
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and get a
k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-qi section, say,
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
over
B_{[s,v]}
in
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(see Figure
7
). Let
\rho_{v}(x_{2})=y_{2}
. Since
\rho_{v}(y_{1})=y_{1}
,
d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq d_{X}(\rho_{v}(y_{1}),\rho_{v}(x_{2}))\leq L^{\prime}d_{X}(y_{1},x_{2})+L^{\prime}\leq L_{1}
, where
L_{1}=L^{\prime}((4Kr+r)(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime})+L^{\prime}
. Then
d_{X}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq d_{X}(x_{2},y_{1})+d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq L_{2}
, where
L_{2}=(4Kr+r(L^{\prime}+1)+L^{\prime}+L_{1}
. So
d^{f}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq\phi(L_{2})
.

Figure 7
.

Note that
d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq L^{\prime}(4Kr+r+1)
and
d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq L_{1}
. Again
