x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
, so by
(\mathcal{H}3)
,
d^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq\eta(L)(L^{\prime}(4Kr+r+1))
and
d^{\prime}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq\eta(L)(L_{1})
. So to get a bound on
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})
, we need to get a bound on
d^{\prime}(x_{2},y_{2})
; which we will show now.

Let
b
be the nearest point projection of
c
on
B_{v}
. Then
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)\in X_{v}
and so
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)=\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(b)
. Note that
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
are two
k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-qi sections over
B_{[s,v]}
such that
d^{f}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(c),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(c))=d^{f}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq\phi(L_{2})
and
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(b)=\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(b)
. Now we restrict the qi sections
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
on the geodesic
[c,b]_{B}\subseteq B
. Let
a
be the point on
[c,b]
closest to
c
such that
d^{f}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))\leq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
. Since the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
satisfies flaring condition, by Lemma
5.7

(1)
,
d_{B}(c,a)\leq\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}},D)
, where
D=max\{M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}},\phi(L_{2})\}
. Let
D_{1}=\tau_{\ref{flaring-lemma}}(k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}},D)
. Then by taking
k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}
-qi lifts of the geodesic
[c,a]_{B}
in
\bar{\gamma}_{y}
and
\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}
, we get,
d^{\prime}(x_{2},\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a))\leq 2k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}D_{1}
and
d^{\prime}(\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a),y_{2})\leq 2k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}D_{1}
(note that
\bar{\gamma}_{y}(c)=x_{2},~\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(c)=y_{2}
). Again
L\geq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
implies
d^{\prime}(\bar{\gamma}_{y}(a),\bar{\bar{\gamma}}_{y}(a))\leq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq 4k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}D_{1}+M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
(by triangle inequality).

Again by triangle inequality,
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq d^{\prime}(x_{1},x_{2})+d^{\prime}(x_{2},y_{2})+d^{\prime}(y_{2},y_{1})\leq L_{3}
, where
L_{3}=\eta(L)(L^{\prime}(4Kr+r+1))+4k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}D_{1}+M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}+\eta(L)(L_{1})
. So
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})+d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})+d^{\prime}(y_{1},y)\leq 4Kr+L_{3}
.

Let
\bm{\zeta_{3}(r)}:=max\{\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(r)),~\zeta_{2}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(2Kr+r))+2Kr,~\zeta_{1}(\eta_{\ref{subsp-is-proper-emb}}(4Kr+r))+4Kr,~4Kr+L_{3}\}
, maximum distortion in this Case
3
for some
\zeta_{3}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Let
\zeta:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that
\zeta(r):=max\{\eta(L)(r),\zeta_{1}(r),\zeta_{2}(r),\zeta_{3}(r)\}
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We have proved that if
x,y\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
are at most
r
-distance apart in the metric of
X
, then they are at most
\zeta(r)
-distance apart in the induced metric on
Fl_{KL}(X_{u})\cup F_{KL}(X_{v})
. Now we take points
x,y\in Fl_{KL}(X_{u})\cup F_{KL}(X_{v})
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Let
x_{1},y_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
such that
d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})\leq L
and
d^{\prime}(y,y_{1})\leq L
. Then
d_{X}(x,y)\leq d_{X}(x,x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{X}(y_{1},y)\leq r+2L
. So
d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq\zeta(r+2L)
. Hence
d^{\prime}(x,y)\leq d^{\prime}(x,x_{1})+d^{\prime}(x_{1},y_{1})+d^{\prime}(y_{1},y)\leq 2L+\zeta(r+2L)
.

Therefore, we can take
\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
sending
r\mapsto\zeta(r+2L)+2L
.
∎

To show the hyperbolicity of
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
, we construct a bigger uniformly hyperbolic space
Y=Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}
containing both
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
as uniformly quasiconvex subsets. As
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
, so a uniform neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
in
Y
is uniformly hyperbolic. Let
N^{\prime}_{D}(y)
denote
D
-neighborhood at
y\in Y
in the path metric of
Y
. Next, we show that
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\subseteq Y
and
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\subseteq X
are (uniformly) quasi-isometric, and that completes the proof.

Construction of the space
Y
: By
(\mathcal{H}5)
,
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
is
\delta
-hyperbolic metric space. Also by
(\mathcal{H}6)
,
Fl_{KR_{0}}(X_{u})
is
\bar{L}(R_{0})
-qi embedded in
X
and so is in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. Then by Lemma
2.21

(1)
, there is
K_{1}
depending on
\delta
and
\bar{L}(R_{0})
such that

Fl_{KR_{0}}(X_{u})
is
K_{1}
-quasiconvex in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. So
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
is
(K_{1}+R_{0})
-quasiconvex in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. Also by
(\mathcal{H}6)
,
Fl_{\kappa^{(1)}R_{0}}(H)
is
\bar{L}(R_{0})
-qi embedded in
X
and so is in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. Then by Lemma
2.21

(1)
, there is
K_{2}
depending on
\delta
and
\bar{L}(R_{0})
such that
Fl_{\kappa^{(1)}R_{0}}(H)
is
K_{2}
-quasiconvex in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. So
\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
is
(K_{2}+R_{0})
-quasiconvex in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. Let
K_{3}=max\{K_{1}+R_{0},K_{2}+R_{0}\}
.
As
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)\neq\emptyset
, so
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
is
(K_{3}+\delta)
-quasiconvex in
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
. Let
Y^{\prime}_{1R}:=N^{\prime}_{R}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H))
be
R
-neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)\subseteq Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
in the induced path metric on
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
where

(4)

\displaystyle\bm{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle max\{K_{3}+\delta+1,~M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}\}

Hence, by Lemma
2.22
, there is
L_{1}
depending on
\delta
,
K_{3}
and
R
such that the inclusion
Y^{\prime}_{1R}\hookrightarrow Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})
is
L_{1}
-qi embedding.

We fix this
R
for the rest of this section
. Thus there is
\delta_{1}
depending on
\delta
and
L_{1}
such that
Y^{\prime}_{1R}
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic with the induced path metric. Again, the inclusion
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{u})\hookrightarrow X
is
\bar{L}(R_{0})
-qi embedding (see
(\mathcal{H}6)
). Thus the inclusion
Y^{\prime}_{1R}\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{2}
-qi embedding for some
L_{2}
depending on
L_{1}
and
\bar{L}(R_{0})
.

Let
Y^{\prime}_{2R}:=N^{\prime}_{R}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H))
be
R
-neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)

\subseteq Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{v})
in the induced path metric on
Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{v})
. Then by similar argument, we can show that
Y^{\prime}_{2R}
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic and the inclusion
Y^{\prime}_{2R}\hookrightarrow Fl_{\kappa^{(2)}R_{0}}(X_{v})
is
L_{2}
-qi embedding.

We take
Y:=Y_{1R}\cup Y_{2R}
where
Y_{1R}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H))\subseteq X
and
Y_{2R}:=N_{R}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H))\subseteq X
. Note that these neighborhoods are considered in
X
.

Hyperbolicity of
Y
:

Lemma 9.2
.

There is a uniform constant
\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}}
such that
Y_{iR}
is
\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic metric space with the induced path metric for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

Since
Y^{\prime}_{iR}\hookrightarrow X
is
L_{2}
-qi embedding, so is the inclusion
Y^{\prime}_{iR}\hookrightarrow Y_{iR}
. Also
Y^{\prime}_{iR}\hookrightarrow Y_{iR}
is
R
-coarsely surjective. Hence the inclusion
Y^{\prime}_{iR}\hookrightarrow Y_{iR}
is
(L_{2},L_{2},R)
-quasi-isometry for
i=1,2
(see Subsection
2.1
). Since the hyperbolicity is quasi-isometry invariant and
Y^{\prime}_{iR}
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic,
Y_{iR}
is
\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic for
i=1,2
, where
\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}}
depends on
\delta_{1},L_{2},R
.
∎

Lemma 9.3
.

There exists a uniform function
\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that the inclusion
Y_{iR}\hookrightarrow X
is
\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}
-proper embedding for
i=1,2
.

Proof.

We prove it only for
i=1
as the proof for
i=2
is similar. We denote the induced path metric on
Y_{1R}
and
Y^{\prime}_{1R}
by
d_{1}
and
d^{\prime}_{1}
respectively. Let
x,y\in Y_{1R}
such that
d_{X}(x,y)=r
for
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. We take points
x_{1},y_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)\subseteq Y^{\prime}_{1R}
such that
d_{1}(x,x_{1})\leq R,~d_{1}(y,y_{1})\leq R
. So
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq r+2R
. Since
Y^{\prime}_{1R}
is
L_{2}
-qi embedded in
X
, then
d^{\prime}_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq(r+2R)L_{2}+L_{2}^{2}
. Since
Y^{\prime}_{1R}\subseteq Y_{1R}
and so
d_{1}(x,y)\leq d_{1}(x,x_{1})+d^{\prime}_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{1}(y_{1},y)\leq(r+2R)L_{2}+L_{2}^{2}+2R=:\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(r)
.
∎

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
d_{i}
denote the induced path metric on
Y_{iR}
for
i=1,2
. There is a uniform constant
D_{\ref{containment}}
such that
Y_{1R}\cap Y_{2R}\subseteq N^{i}_{D_{\ref{containment}}}(Y_{0})
, where
N^{i}_{D_{\ref{containment}}}(Y_{0})
denotes the
D_{\ref{containment}}
-neighborhood of
Y_{0}
in
d_{i}
-metric.

Proof.

Let
x\in Y_{1R}\cap Y_{2R}
. Then there exist
x_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
such that
d_{i}(x,x_{i})\leq R,~i=1,2
. So
d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 2R
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
x_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\setminus\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H),~x_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\setminus\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
, otherwise,
x\in Y_{0}:=Fl_{\kappa^{(1)}R}(H)
. Let
\pi_{X}(x_{i})=\bar{x}_{i},~\pi(x_{i})=t_{i}
for
i=1,2
. Let
w_{i}
be the nearest point projection of
t_{i}
on
[u,v],i=1,2
. We consider the following two cases, depending on whether
w_{1}=w_{2}
or
w_{1}\neq w_{2}
.

Case
\bm{1}
: Suppose
w_{1}\neq w_{2}
. Let
\bar{y}_{i}
be the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}_{i}
on
B_{w_{i}}
for
i=1,2
. Then
d_{B}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\leq d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})\leq 2R
implies
d_{B}(\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i})\leq 2R
for
i=1,2
. Let
\gamma_{x_{1}}
be a
K
-qi section through
x_{1}
inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
over
B_{[t_{1},u]}
and
\gamma_{x_{2}}
be that through
x_{2}
inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
over
B_{[t_{2},v]}
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Suppose
\gamma_{x_{i}}(\bar{y}_{i})=y_{i},~i=1,2
. Then by taking lift of the geodesic
[\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i}]_{B}
in
\gamma_{x_{i}}
, we get,
d_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq 2K.2R=4KR
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
) for
i=1,2
. Now we restrict the
K
-qi section
\gamma_{x_{2}}
over
B_{[w_{2},v]}
and apply
\rho_{u}
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) on this restriction of
\gamma_{x_{2}}
over
B_{[w_{2},v]}
. We set this projection as
\bar{\gamma}_{2}
. Note that
B_{[w_{2},v]}\subseteq\pi(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}))
, then
\bar{\gamma}_{2}
is a
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section over
B_{[w_{2},v]}
inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. As
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
, then we can extend
\bar{\gamma}_{2}
to a
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section over
B_{[u,v]}
. Then in particular, we have,
\bar{\gamma}_{2}\subseteq H
(see
(\mathcal{H}4)
). Again,
d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq d_{X}(y_{1},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})+d_{X}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq 8KR+2R
. Note that
\rho_{u}(y_{1})=y_{1}
and
\rho_{u}(y_{2})=\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2})
. Since
\rho_{u}
is
L^{\prime}
-coaresly Lipschitz retraction (see
(\mathcal{H}1)
),
d_{X}(y_{1},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq L^{\prime}d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})+L^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime}
. Since
y_{1},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2})\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\subseteq Y_{1R}
, by Lemma
9.3
,
d_{1}(y_{1},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
. Now
\bar{\gamma}_{2}\subseteq H
implies
d_{1}(x,Y_{0})\leq d_{1}(x,\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq d_{1}(x,x_{1})+d_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{1}(y_{1},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq R+4KR+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
.

Again,
d_{X}(x_{2},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq d_{X}(x_{2},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{X}(y_{1},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq 2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime}
. Since
x_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\subseteq Y_{2r}
and
\bar{\gamma}_{2}\subseteq H\subseteq Y_{2r}
, so by Lemma
9.3
,
d_{2}(x_{2},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
. Thus
d_{2}(x,Y_{0})\leq d_{2}(x,x_{2})+d_{2}(x_{2},\bar{\gamma}_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq R+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
.

Case
\bm{2}
: Suppose
w_{1}=w_{2}
. Let
w
be the center of the tripod
\triangle(t_{1},t_{2},w_{1})
. Suppose
\bar{y}_{i}
is the nearest point projection of
\bar{x}_{i}
on
B_{w}
for
i=1,2
. Let
\gamma_{x_{1}}
be a
K
-qi section through
x_{1}
inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
over
B_{[t_{1},u]}
and
\gamma_{x_{2}}
be that through
x_{2}
inside
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
over
B_{[t_{2},v]}
(see
(\mathcal{H}2)
). Again
d_{B}(\bar{x}_{1},\bar{x}_{2})\leq 2R
implies
d_{B}(\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i})\leq 2R
for
i=1,2
. Let
\gamma_{x_{i}}(\bar{y}_{i})=y_{i},~i=1,2
. Then by taking lift of the geodesic
[\bar{x}_{i},\bar{y}_{i}]_{B}
in
\gamma_{x_{i}}
, we have
d_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})\leq 2K.2R=4KR
(see Lemma
5.12

(3)
). Now let us restrict the
K
-qi section,
\gamma_{x_{2}}
, over
B_{[w,v]}
and apply
\rho_{u}
(see
(\mathcal{H}1)
) on this restriction of
\gamma_{x_{2}}
over
B_{[w,v]}
. We denote the image under
\rho_{u}
by
\gamma_{2}
. Since
B_{[w,v]}\subseteq\pi(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}))
,
\gamma_{2}
is a
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section over
B_{[w,v]}
. Let
T_{uvw}
is the tripod in
T
with vertices
u,v,w
and
B_{T_{uvw}}:=\pi_{B}^{-1}(T_{uvw})
. As
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap X_{v}\neq\emptyset
, we can extend
\gamma_{2}
to a
(2KL^{\prime}+L^{\prime})
-qi section over
B_{T_{uvw}}
. Then in particular, we have
\gamma_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
(see
(\mathcal{H}4)
). Now we apply line by line argument as in
\textrm{Case }1
. Note that
d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})\leq d_{X}(y_{1},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},x_{2})+d_{X}(x_{2},y_{2})\leq 8KR+2R
and
\rho_{u}(y_{1})=y_{1}
,
\rho_{u}(y_{2})=\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2})
. So
d_{X}(y_{1},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq L^{\prime}d_{X}(y_{1},y_{2})+L^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime}
. Since
y_{1},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2})\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}\subseteq Y_{1R}
, by Lemma
9.3
,
d_{1}(y_{1},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
. Again, we have,
\gamma_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)
, so
d_{1}(x,Y_{0})\leq d_{1}(x,x_{1})+d_{1}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{1}(y_{1},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq R+4KR+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
.

Again,
d_{X}(x_{2},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq d_{X}(x_{2},x_{1})+d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})+d_{X}(y_{1},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq 2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime}
. Since
x_{2}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\subseteq Y_{2R}
and
\gamma_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)\subseteq Y_{2R}
, so by Lemma
9.3
,
d_{2}(x_{2},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
. Thus
d_{2}(x,Y_{0})\leq d_{2}(x,x_{2})+d_{2}(x_{2},\gamma_{2}(\bar{y}_{2}))\leq R+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})
.

Therefore, we can take
D_{\ref{containment}}=max\{R+4KR+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime}),R+\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(2R+4KR+L^{\prime}(8KR+2R)+L^{\prime})\}
.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

There is
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}=\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R)
such that
Y
is
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}
-hyperbolic metric space.

Proof.

We verify all the conditions of Proposition
2.11
for
n=2
(see Remark
2.12
). Note that
Y=Y_{1R}\cup Y_{2R}
.

(1)

Y_{iR},~i=1,2
are
\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic.

(2)

Y_{0}
is
\bar{L}(R)
-qi embedded in
X
(see
(\mathcal{H}6)
), so is in both
Y_{1R}
and
Y_{1R}
. Again
Y_{1R}\cap Y_{1R}\subseteq N_{D_{\ref{containment}}}(Y_{0})
(see Lemma
9.4
), so by Lemma
2.3
, the inclusion
Y_{1R}\cap Y_{1R}\hookrightarrow Y_{iR}
is
L_{\ref{hd-imp-qi}}(\bar{L}(R),D_{\ref{containment}})
-qi embedding for
i=1,2
.

Therefore,
Y
is
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}:=\delta_{\ref{combi-hyp-sps-2}}(\delta_{\ref{Yir-is-hyp}},L_{\ref{hd-imp-qi}}(\bar{L}(R),D_{\ref{containment}}),1)
-hyperbolic.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

The inclusion
Y\hookrightarrow X
is
\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}=\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}(R)
-proper embedding for some uniform function
\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
and
x,y\in Y
such that
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
. Then
\exists~x_{1},y_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{\kappa^{(1)}}(H)\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
such that
d_{Y}(x,x_{1})\leq R
and
d_{Y}(y,y_{1})\leq R
. So by triangle inequality,
d_{X}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq r+2R
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
x_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
and
y_{1}\in\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
. Otherwise, by Lemma
9.3
,
d_{Y}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(r+2R)
. Again
R\geq M_{k_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}}
, and thus by Proposition
9.1
,
d_{Y}(x_{1},y_{1})\leq\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}(K,R)(r+2R)
. Therefore, in either case,
d_{Y}(x,y)\leq 2R+max\{\eta_{\ref{Yir-is-proper-emb}}(r+2R),\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}(K,R)(r+2R)\}=:\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}(r)
.
∎

Proposition 9.7
.

There exists a constant
D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
such that for all
D\geq D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
we have
\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}(D)
for which
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is
\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic metric space with the induced path metric from
X
.

Proof.

In the proof, we define
D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
. For
D\geq D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
, we denote the induced path metric on
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
by
\bar{d}
. By
(\mathcal{H}6)
,
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})
is
\bar{L}(R)
-qi embedded in
X
and so is in
Y
. Hence
Fl_{KR}(X_{u})
is
K_{1}
-quasiconvex in
Y
for some
K_{1}
depending on
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R)
and
\bar{L}(R)
. So
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u}
is
K_{2}
-quasiconvex in
Y
, where
K_{2}=K_{1}+R
. Also, by the similar argument
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
is
K_{2}
-quasiconvex in
Y
. Since
Y
is
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R)
-hyperbolic and
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cap\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})\neq\emptyset
, so
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
is
(K_{2}+\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R))
-quasiconvex in
Y
. Let
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
be the
D
-neighborhood of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v})
(inside
Y
) in the path metric on
Y
. We set
D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}>K_{2}+\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R)+1
. Thus for
D\geq D_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
, (by Lemma
2.22
) the inclusion
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\hookrightarrow Y
is
L_{1}
-qi embedding, where
L_{1}=L_{\ref{qi-emb-in-Y-X}}(\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R),K_{2}+\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R),D)
. Therefore,
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic, where
\delta_{1}
depends on
\delta_{\ref{Y-is-hyperbolic}}(R)
and
L_{1}
. Now we show that the subset
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))\subseteq N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
satisfies all the conditions of Proposition
2.10
. Note that
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is a
D
-dense in
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
in the
\bar{d}
-metric. For any pair
(x,y)
of distinct points in
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
, we fix once and for all a geodesic path, say,
c(x,y)
joining
x
and
y
in the
\delta_{1}
-hyperbolic space
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
. These paths serve as family of paths for Proposition
2.10
. Then any triangle formed by these paths are
\delta_{1}
-slim in the induced path metric of
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
and so is in
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
. Hence we are left to show the proper embedding of these paths in
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
. Indeed, suppose
x,y\in N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
such that
\bar{d}(x,y)\leq r
for some
r\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
. Then
d_{X}(x,y)\leq r
and by Lemma
9.6
,
d_{Y}(x,y)\leq\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}(r)
. Since
N^{\prime}_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is
L_{1}
-qi embedded in
Y
, the path
c(x,y)
is
\eta_{1}
-properly embedded, where
\eta)1:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
sending
r\mapsto\eta_{\ref{Y-is-proper-emb}}(r)L_{1}+L^{2}_{1}
.

Therefore,
N_{D}(\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})\cup\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{v}))
is
\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}
-hyperbolic metric space with the induced path metric from
X
, where
\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}=\delta_{\ref{combing}}(\eta_{1},\delta_{1},D)
.
∎

10.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

We think of the tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
as a tree of metric spaces
\pi:X\rightarrow T
as explained in Remark
1.5
. For a vertex
u\in T
, we take
\mathcal{M}(X_{u})=\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
as in Lemma
6.9
. Now we show that it satisfies property
(\mathcal{P}0)-(\mathcal{P}4)
of Section
4
. Note that
(\mathcal{P}0)
follows from the definition of
\mathcal{F}l_{K}(X_{u})
. Again by Proposition
6.10
,
L^{\prime}=L_{\ref{mitra's-retraction-on-fsandgss}}(K)
and we have
C=D_{\ref{R-sep-D-cobdd}}(\delta^{\prime}_{0},L^{\prime}_{0})
for
(\mathcal{P}1)
. Taking into account of Proposition
9.7
, we set
L_{0}
large enough so that for
L\geq L_{0}
, we can take
\eta^{\prime}(L)=\eta_{\ref{union-of-two-flow-sp-is-proper-emb}}(K,L)
(by Proposition
9.1
) for
(\mathcal{P}3)
and
\delta(L)=\delta_{\ref{union-of-flow-sp-is-hyp}}(L)
(by Proposition
9.7
) for
(\mathcal{P}4)
. Finally, by Proposition
6.11
, for
L\geq L_{0}
, we can take
\eta(L)=\eta_{\ref{fsandgss-are-proper-emb}}(K,L)
for
(\mathcal{P}2)
.∎

11.
Applications to complexes of groups

We refer to
[
BH99
, Chapter III
.C
]
,
[
Hae92
]
and
[
Cor92
]
for basic notions of developable complexes of groups. All the groups we consider here are finitely generated.

The construction of a tree of metric bundles for a given complex of groups in the setup
\mathcal{C}
explained in Introduction
1
follows from the idea of
[
Cor92
]
,
[
Hae92
]
. We briefly discuss the same below.

Suppose
\mathcal{Y}
is a finite connected simplicial complex and
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
is a developable complex of groups over
\mathcal{Y}
. Let
G
be the fundamental group of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
. As in
[
Cor92
]
and more generally,
[
Hae92
, Theorem
3.4.1
]
, we consider a cellular aspherical realization (see
[
Hae92
, Definition
3.3.4
]
)
\mathcal{X}
of the complex of groups
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
with cellular map
p:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
. Note that
\mathcal{X}
is constructed by gluing along the Eilenberg-Mac Lane complexes of the local groups of the complex of groups
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
; where for each local group
G_{\sigma}
corresponding to a face
\sigma
of
\mathcal{Y}
, the
0
-skeleton of
K(G_{\sigma},1)
is a point
x_{\sigma}
and the
1
-skeletons form wedge of circles coming from a finite generating set of
G_{\sigma}
. Now we consider the universal covering
\pi:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
with the standard CW-complex structure on
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
coming from
\mathcal{X}
. We identify
G
with the group of deck transformation of the covering map
p:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
. Let
y\in\mathcal{Y}
and
\sigma
be the face containing
y
in its interior, and
a
be the barycenter of
\sigma
. Now we collapse each connected component of
\{(p\circ\pi)^{-1}(y)\}
to a point. Note that since
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
is developable, the inclusion
p^{-1}(a)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}
is
\pi_{1}
-injective and hence
\{(p\circ\pi)^{-1}(y)\}
are copies of universal cover of
\mathcal{X}_{a}:=p^{-1}(a)
. We do this for all
y\in\mathcal{Y}
. Let
\mathcal{B}
be the space we get after collapsing and
q:\tilde{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
be the quotient map; where
\mathcal{B}
is called the universal cover of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
. Thus we get the quotient map
\bar{\pi}:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}/G=\mathcal{Y}
and the following commutative diagram.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\curvearrowright}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Figure 8
.

Let
X:=\tilde{\mathcal{X}}^{(1)}
and
B:=\mathcal{B}^{(1)}
, where
Z^{(1)}
denotes the
1
-skeleton of a CW-complex
Z
. Assume that each edge in
X
and
B
has length
1
. Put the length metric on
X
and
B
. Since the groups are finitely generated, by covering space argument, we have the following facts.

(1)

The restriction of the map
q
on
X
,
q|_{X}:X\rightarrow B
is
G
-equivariant, surjective and
1
-Lipschitz.

(2)

The action of
G
on
X
is proper and cocompact; and,
G
-action on
B
is cocompact (but not necessarily proper unless local groups are all finite).

(3)

There is an isomorphism of graphs
r:B/G\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}
such that for all
\sigma_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}^{(0)}
and
a\in\{r^{-1}(\sigma_{0})\}
,
G_{a}
(the stabilizer subgroup of
a\in B^{(0)}
) is conjugate to
G_{\sigma_{0}}
in
G
.

(4)

Let
a\in B^{(0)}
and
F_{a}:=(q|_{X})^{-1}(a)
. Then the action of
G_{a}
(the stabilizer subgroup of
a
) on
F_{a}^{(0)}
is transitive and on
F_{a}^{(1)}
is uniformly cofinite. In particular,
G_{a}
is uniformly quasi-isometric to
F_{a}
(with the induced path metric from
X
).

(5)

Since a finitely generated subgroup of a finitely generated group is properly embedded (with respect to their finite generating sets), for all
a\in B^{(0)}
, the inclusion
F_{a}\hookrightarrow X
is uniformly properly embedding, where
F_{a}:=(q|_{X})^{-1}(a)
.

Complexes of groups as explained in setup
\mathcal{C}
: Now suppose
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y},Y)
is a complex of groups over
\mathcal{Y}
as explained in Introduction
1
. Note that for this discussion, we do not require the hypotheses of Problem
1.1
but those of Theorem
1.2
. Then we have a natural graph of groups, say,
(\mathcal{G},Y)
over
Y
such that the vertex groups are
G_{s}:=\pi_{1}(\mathcal{G}_{s}(\mathcal{Y}_{s})),\forall~s\in Y^{(0)}
and the edge groups are
G_{e}
for
e=[u,v]
joining two vertices
u,v\in\mathcal{Y}
so that restriction of
p_{Y}
on
e
is injective. Note that the monomorphisms from edge groups to the corresponding vertex groups are the restriction of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y},Y)
.

As a corollary of
[
BH99
, Proposition
3.9
, III
.C
]
, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 11.1
.

The complex of groups
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y},Y)
is developable.

11.1.
Trees of metric bundles coming from complexes of groups

Consider the above discussion on complexes of groups for
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y},Y)
(setup
\mathcal{C}
). For now onward, we denote the restriction map
q|_{X}
by
\pi_{X}
. Let
s\in Y^{(0)}
and
G_{s}=\pi_{1}(\mathcal{G}_{s}(\mathcal{Y}_{s}))
. Consider the corresponding graph of groups
(\mathcal{G},Y)
as explained above. Then note that
X
is the corresponding tree of metric spaces over the Base-Serre tree of the graph of groups
(\mathcal{G},Y)
. Let
T
be the Base-Serre tree and
\pi:X\rightarrow T
be the projection map. Again vertex spaces of
X
are acted (properly and cocompactly) upon by the conjugates of
G_{s}
in
G
,
s\in Y^{(0)}
. Now by condition
(2)
of setup
\mathcal{C}
(see Introduction
1
), it follows from
[
KS20
, Section
3.3
]
that the vertex spaces of
\pi:X\rightarrow T
are metric graph bundles with uniform parameters. For instance, if
s\in Y^{(0)}
,
g\in G
,
u=gG_{s}\in T^{(0)}
and
B_{s}
is the
1
-skeleton of the universal cover of
G_{s}(\mathcal{Y}_{s})
, then
X_{u}:=\pi^{-1}(u)
is the metric graph bundle over
B_{u}=gB_{s}\subseteq B
and the subgroup
gG_{s}g^{-1}
acts on
X_{u}
properly and cocompactly. Also, note that
T
is obtained by collapsing the universal cover of
\mathcal{G}_{s}(\mathcal{Y}_{s})
(for
s\in\mathcal{Y}^{(0)}
) in
B
and its
G
-translates to points. Let
\pi_{B}:B\rightarrow T
be the projection map. The maps
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow B
and
\pi_{B}:B\rightarrow T
are
G
-equivariant. Therefore, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 11.2
.

Suppose
G
is the fundamental group of
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{Y})
. Then there is a natural tree of metric bundles
(X,B,T)
and an action of
G
by isometries on both
X
and
B
such that the following hold.

(1)

The map
\pi_{X}
is
G
-equivariant.

(2)

The action of
G
on
X
is proper and cocompact; and,
G
-action on
B
is cocompact (but not necessarily proper unless all local groups are finite).

(3)

There is an isomorphism of graphs
r:B/G\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{(1)}
such that for all
\sigma_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}^{(0)}
and
a\in\{r^{-1}(\sigma_{0})\}
,
G_{a}
(the stabilizer subgroup of
a\in B^{(0)}
) is conjugate to
G_{\sigma_{0}}
in
G
.

(4)

Let
a\in B^{(0)}
and
u=\pi_{B}(a),~F_{a,u}:=\pi_{X}^{-1}(a)=q^{-1}(a)^{(1)}
. Then the action of
G_{a}
on
F_{a,u}^{(0)}
is transitive and on
F_{a,u}^{(1)}
is uniformly cofinite. In particular, if
\sigma_{0}\in\mathcal{Y}^{(0)}
and
G_{\sigma_{0}}
is hyperbolic, then for all
a\in\{r^{-1}(\sigma_{0})\},~F_{a,u}
is uniformly hyperbolic, where
u=\pi_{B}(a)
.

Note that condition
(2)
of setup
\mathcal{C}
(see Introduction
1
) in Proposition
11.2
is necessary to get trees of metric bundles.

Now we are ready to prove the main application of Theorem
1.4
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
: It follows from Proposition
11.2
and Theorem
1.4
.∎

Proof of Corollary
1.8
: It follows from Proposition
11.2
and Remark
1.7
.∎
